Open a method to see what it is, where it came from, and the exact path — each path has a Copy button so you can paste it into Explorer, Finder or a terminal.

Expanding a method shows the document itself, step by step — headings, numbered steps, bullet lists and tables, as written — and a Download button for the Word, Excel and PowerPoint originals. PDFs are not served: most are publisher or vendor material and this site sits in a public repository, so those stay on the lab share, where group 304186 can read and write them.

Protocol numbering is the lab's own scheme — 100s human · 200s mouse · 300s molecular / cell engineering · 400s bacterial · 500s TCR engineering · 600s tetramer. Unnumbered vendor and reference documents are filed by topic.

Document record

No embedded metadata (legacy binary format).

The protocol, step by step

[Prot 101] Immunofluorescence Staining Protocol using Directly Conjugated Antibodies:

(Used this protocol on 2/6/19 for Albert’s phenotype panel for exp 1903p)

  1. Prepare desired amount of single cell suspension (in DMEM) into 12x75mm polypropylene FACS tube and wash in 2mL staining buffer (PBS). Use ~1M PBMCs.
  2. Centrifuge (300 xg, 5 min, 4oC – already preset), discard supernatant by decanting.
  3. (Optional) Add 5ul/tube of blocking antibody (e.g. Fc Block). (We did not do this step.
    1. Vortex and incubate for 2 min at room temp.
  4. Add Live-Dead Fixable Blue dead cell stain (we used L-34961 from Thermofisher, which takes up the DAPI channel).
    1. Vortex, stain for 15 min @ RT in dark.
  5. Add monoclonal antibody mix.
    1. Vortex and incubate for 15 min @ RT in dark.
  6. Add 2mL of FACS buffer (wash step, 1 is enough)
    1. Vortex and centrifuge (300 xg, 5 min, 4oC).
    2. Discard supernatant.
  7. Resuspend cells in 200uL FACS buffer
    1. Keep on ice.
    2. **Cells may be centrifuged and fixed in 1 ml of 1% paraformaldehyde (in PBS) at 4C for analysis next day. (we didn’t do this)

NOTES:
Use buffers without Phenol Red
The blocking antibody step (4 and 5) is optional but should be included if cells express high levels of Fc receptors which will contribute to non-specific binding and background fluorescence. We didn’t need to do this for the human experiment.

Prot 102 — PBMC Harvest from Whole Blood (Ficoll-Paque)

Density-gradient PBMC isolation, with a documented variation depending on whether plasma must be retained (if so, the sample is not diluted).

The protocol, step by step

[Prot 102] Peripheral Blood Mononuclear Cell (PBMC) Harvest using Ficoll-Paque
Last Updated 6/27/19

**Note protocol variation depending on whether or not plasma need to be kept. If plasma needs to be stored, we do not dilute sample.

IF NEED TO KEEP PLASMA:
  1. Draw desired amount of whole blood into lithium-heparin tubes.
  2. Pool blood into 50cc conical-bottom tube.
  3. Underlay with 15cc Ficoll-Paque density gradient media (GE)
    1. Put pipette tip to very bottom and slowly add the Ficoll (use slowest dispense setting on pipetter)
  4. Spin at 510g for 20 minutes at 21-25C with breaks off. Can also do this at RT.
  5. Carefully harvest the PBMC and plasma layer (above Ficoll layer) using a pipette into a separate 50cc tube.
    1. To do this, when suctioning with pipette, gently move around the tube right above the PBMC layer while applying suction.
    2. Sample will contain both plasma and PBMC
    3. Perform cell count on this sample
  6. Centrifuge PBMCs at 450g for 10 minutes at 21-25C
  7. Decant plasma into separate 50cc tube; aliquot in cryovials for freezing
  8. Resuspend pellet in 40cc saline
  9. Centrifuge PBMCs at 450g for 5min at 21-25C
  10. Decant
  11. Resuspend pellet in desired amount of human DMEM culture media
    1. Aliquot cell in cryovials and add DMSO as needed for freezing.
IF DO NOT NEED TO KEEP PLASMA:
  1. Draw desired amount of whole blood into lithium-heparin tubes.
  2. Pool blood into 50cc conical-bottom tube.
    1. Make up volume to 32cc with saline
  3. Underlay with 8-15cc Ficoll-Paque density gradient media (GE)
    1. Put pipette tip to very bottom and slowly add the Ficoll (use slowest dispense setting on pipetter)
  4. Spin at 510g for 20 minutes at 21-25C with breaks off. Can also do this at RT.
  5. Carefully harvest the PBMC layer (above Ficoll, below plasma) using a pipette into a separate 50cc tube.
    1. To do this, when suctioning with pipette, gently move around the tube right above the PBMC layer while applying suction.
    2. May get 10-20cc plasma
    3. Perform cell count on this sample
  6. Centrifuge PBMCs at 450g for 10 minutes at 4C
  7. Decant
  8. Resuspend pellet in 40cc saline
  9. Centrifuge PBMCs at 450g for 5min at 4C
  10. Decant
  11. Resuspend pellet in 1-3cc human DMEM culture media
  • Note for my experimental run on 2/6/19, 18cc of whole blood yielded ~1.2cc of lymphocytes at a concentration of 14M/cc. 84% lymphs.

The protocol, step by step

[Prot 103] PBMC PMA-Ionomycin Stimulation and Intracellular Staining Protocol
Updated 12.21.23
Sources:
  1. T-Helper Cells – Methods and Protocols (2014, Waisman and Becher)
  2. PMID 28738016
  3. PMID 23416458
  4. https://www.thermofisher.com/us/en/home/references/protocols/cell-and-tissue-analysis/protocols/staining-intracellular-antigens-flow-cytometry.html#nuclear
Textbook Prep:
  1. Prep stock solutions of ionomycin and PMA by dilution in DMSO to achieve concentration of 1mg/mL for ionomycin and 0.1mg/mL for PMA, store at -20C.
  2. For Fixable dead cell stain, reconstitute one vial of dye and store in 5uL aliquots @ -20C
  3. Prepare re-stimulation medium: for every 1mL, add 1:1000 (1uL) ionomycin and 1:2000 (0.5uL) PMA for final concentration of ionomycin 1ug/mL (for humans; use 500ng/mL for mice) and PMA 50ng/mL respectively. Also add 1:1,000 GolgiStop (brefeldin, 1mg/mL) for a final concentration of 1ug/mL.
  4. For surface staining mix, dilute desired antibodies in staining buffer at up to 2x optimal concentration.
  5. For intracellular staining mix, dilute desired antibodies in perm/wash buffer at up to 2x the optimal concentration.
Hill Lab Prep:
  1. PMA stock solution: 5mg/mL (1mg in 200ul DMSO); freeze 20ul aliquots separately
  2. Ionomycin stock solution: 2mM (1mg in 0.675 ml DMSO)
  3. PMA/ionomycin mix (200uL – enough for 20x1ml cultures).
    1. Dilute 20ul PMA (100ug) in 2.0 ml IMDM (10% FCS) to make stock solution of 50ug/mL (diluted PMA) – Note I tried 2-month old stock, which still works.
    2. Mix 20uL diluted PMA (1ug; final 5ug/mL) with 6.75uL (use 12.5uL for humans) ionomycin (10ug; final 50ug/mL; or 100ug/mL for humans) into 173.25uL culture media.
  4. Add 10 uL PMA/ionomycin mix per 1 mL cells for final concentration of PMA 50ng/mL and ionomycin 500ng/mL (or 1ug/mL for humans), respectively.
Reagents:
  1. Phorbol myristate acetate (PMA; Sigma-Aldrich)
  2. Ionomycin (Sigma-Aldrich)
  3. Brefeldin A (Biolegend)
  4. Foxp3/transcription factor fixation/permeabilization kit (eBiosciences)
Protocol:
  1. Isolated PBMCs; for intracellular staining, advisable to start with at least 1 million cells
  2. Transfer cells into 5cc FACS tubes
  3. Add 2cc FACS buffer; centrifuge at 500g x 2 min at 4C
    1. Decant supernatant
  4. Resuspend cells in 1mL re-stimulation medium containing ionomycin, PMA, and brefeldin A.
    1. Incubate cells for 4 hours @ 37C and 5% CO2.
    2. Centrifuge at 500g for 2min @ 4C
    3. Decant supernatant, drain tube on paper towel to remove residual liquid
  5. Add 0.5uL Live/dead cell dye, pulse vortex
    1. Incubate for 15min at RT in dark
  6. Add surface staining mix, pulse-vortex
    1. Incubate for 15min at RT in dark
  7. Add 2cc FACS buffer to wash, centrifuge at 500g for 2min at 4C
    1. Decant supernatant, blot
    2. Repeat this step (esp if doing ICC stain)
  8. Add fixing agent (can use 1% paraformaldehyde instead of commercial buffer). If using Foxp3 Fixation/Perm kit, add 1 mL of fixation/perm solution (3:1 dilution)
    1. Pulse vortex
    2. Incubate for 30-60min at RT in dark. If doing TF staining, want at least 45 minutes.
    3. Note that after this step, cells can be stored in perm/wash buffer overnight at 4C
  9. Add 2cc permeabilization buffer (contains saponin, note you need the buffer present during all subsequent steps for intracellular staining)
    1. Centrifuge at 500g for 5min @ 4C (make sure to spin for 5 min as cells don’t pellet as well after being treated with perm solution!)
    2. Decant supernatant, blot
  10. Add intracellular staining mix (must be prepared in perm buffer), pulse vortex.
    1. Incubate for 30min at RT in dark
    2. Prepare comp beads here
  11. Add 2cc perm buffer
    1. Centrifuge at 500g for 5min @ 4C
    2. Decant supernatant
    3. Repeat this step
  12. Resuspend cells in 200uL perm buffer within 72 hours for flow cytometry

Prot 104 — Freezing and Thawing PBMCs

Freezing medium recipe (IMDM / 20% DMSO / 20% FCS) and the freeze-thaw procedure. Note: the same medium is used for mouse cells.

The protocol, step by step

[Prot 104] Freezing and Thawing PBMCs
Freezing medium:

IMDM (for human cells) 24ml – Note, can use same media for mouse cells.

DMSO (to 20%) 8ml

FCS (to 20%) 8ml

Freezing down PBMC:
  1. Pellet cells by centrifugation at 1500rpm for 5mins.
  2. Resuspend cell pellet in IMDM (for human cells). Use 500ul for freezing 1 cryovial. (Store ~10M/vial)
  3. Add 500ul freezing media dropwise, slowly and while shaking cells simultaneously.
  4. Aliquot into a cryogenic vial (1ml/vial).
  5. Place into a Mr. Frosty and into -70oC overnight. Transfer on dry ice to liquid nitrogen for long term storage.

NB: Always add the same volume of freezing media to cells as the IMDM volume so as to dilute the DMSO to a final concentration of 10%.

Thawing medium (per sample):

IMDM (for human cells) 8ml

FCS (to 20%) 2ml

Thawing PBMC:
  1. Prepare and warm media to 37C (increases viability)
  2. Thaw cells quickly by submerging in 37C bath for 1 minute
  3. Immediately transfer cells to 10cc of warm media (minimize time exposed to DMSO)
  4. Spin down at 10min, 500G

Prot 105 — Human Endoscopy Sample Freezing & Formalin Fixation

Handling of endoscopic biopsies for single-cell sequencing and for histology. Samples must stay on ice and be processed ASAP to limit RNA degradation.

The protocol, step by step

[Prot 105] Human Endoscopy Sample Processing - Freezing and Formalin Fixation

**Samples are placed in 15mL conical tube with ~5mL RPMI cell culture media. MUST BE KEPT ON ICE AND PROCESSED ASAP TO MINIMIZE RNA DEGRADATION.

Freezing protocol for SCseq
  1. Drop tissue into the pre-filled / pre-labeled cryovial (1mL 93%FCS/7%DMSO)
  2. Transfer vial to isopropanol frosty at -80C then transfer to LN2
Formalin fixation protocol
  1. Transfer fresh tissue (washed with PBS) into 10% formalin for 24-48 hrs (Hutch core recommended 48 hrs) at RT (assuming <5x5x5mm)
    1. Make sure than there is ~10-20x amount of formalin as there is tissue (~2mL is sufficient)
      1. If 5x5x5mm, than 125mm3 ~0.125mL, and 0.125mL x 10 to 20 = 1.25 to 2.5mL
    2. Note that fixation occurs at rate of ~1mm/hr
    3. Controversial whether to fix at 4C to slow down metabolism as rate of fixation is slower, so we can just fix at RT
  2. Wash with PBS, then transfer to 70-80% EtOH
  3. Submit to histopathology lab as soon as possible, ideally within 1 week
  4. Can ask them to re-embed multiple samples to save on blocks
GI Brushings:
Materials:
  1. Product: “Infinity cytology device” from Steris | US Endoscopy: BX00711499, 2.4mm sheath diameter, 230cm length, 5U/box (about $20-40 each).
  2. Rep: Chance Sanders, 425-246-0898, csanders@usendoscopy.com
  3. Wire cutters (ordered, must be cleaned with EtOH prior to each use), provide both brush and wire cutters to nurse in the case and let the attending know

Protocol: (Adapted from Curr Protcol Immunol; PMID 25367129 – Protocol 3: Human intestinal mucosal lavage collection and processing)

  1. Have brushing tip clipped in 15mL conical tube with 2mL RPMI.
  2. After collection, vortex tip in RPMI (to shake off contents) for 30-60 seconds.
  3. Aliquot 1mL in 2x Eppendorf tubes, and centrifuge for 15 minutes at 4000G.
  4. Pippette supernatant in 2 separate cyrovials (1mL each)
  5. Mix remaining pellet in 1mL of RPMI, aliquot in 2 separate cyrovials (0.5mL each)
  6. For the cyrovials with the pellets, at 0.5mL media containing 20% FCS/20%DMSO to make final concentration of 10%FCS/10%DMSO.
  7. Use frosty to freeze both supernant (S) and pellet samples at -80C.
  8. Transfer to LN2 when appropriate

Prot 106 — Human Biopsy Prep for Single-Cell Assays (base version)
Word35.8 KB· Yeh MD, Albert C· edited 2021-02-06
▼

Collagenase/DNase digestion buffer preparation and biopsy digest — the original shared version of the protocol.

⬇ Download Word · 35.8 KB

Source & citation

Document record

Document author: showsd
Last edited by: Yeh MD, Albert C
Created: 2019-04-23
Last modified: 2021-02-06
Revisions: 3
Company: Amgen

The protocol, step by step

Set up before the thaw & digests
  1. Prepare enough digestion buffer for a total of 10mL per biopsy (single or multiples)

You will add 9mL of 1.1x enzymes to 1mL of biopsy + freezing medium (7%DMSO/FBS)

  • Determine how much overall dissociation medium you will need at a minimum
    1. The base medium can be made well ahead and stored for a few weeks cold.
    2. Add all enzymes on day of digestion
    3. Heat medium to 37oC before applying to tissues
  • Determine how much collagenase (C’aseI) you would need for at least that volume
  • Measure powdered C’aseI into 50mL tube to at least that minimum mass you calculated in the previous step
  • Calculate how much DNase you will be adding to the total fluid volume to have a final of 0.01% in the dissociation medium
  • Calculate how much fluid you will have at the end based on how much C’aseI you tapped out into the 50mL tube.
  • Add some RPMI 5% Ca++, Mg++ to the powder, invert gently to suspend the C’aseI into solution, add the desired amount of DNase, mix again, then bring fluids up to final desired total volume.
  1. 9mL X_____ samples + extra = make about ______ X 9mL= ______mL dissociation buffer:
    1. RPMI 1640
    2. 5% FBS
    3. 25mM Hepes
    4. PSG
    5. 1mM each MgCL2, CaCl2
  2. [150U/ml]final Collagenase type I (our current stock is at 340U/mg)
    1. 1.1x150U/mL = 165U/mL working solution to make
    2. 340U/mg / 165U/mL = 2.27x factor for our current lot enzymatic activity
    3. calculate about how much you’ll need _________mL / 2.7 = ____mg of C’aseI
    4. pour powdered C’aseI into 50mL tube to at least that amount
    5. 2.7 x _____mg C’aseI actual in tube = ________mL final dissociation medium
  3. DNase (our current stock is 2% kept in 66% glycerol/RPMI)
    1. 1.1 X 0.01%/ 2% stock X ______mL = _____mL DNase stock to add
  1. Prepare RPMI 10%FBS + PSG+ HEPES (R10) for washing & homogenizing
  2. Put things in the biosafety cabinet for biohazard work
    1. Small biohazard bag, medium sharps container, box of gloves, disinfection bucket
    2. Fully charged pipettor, pipettes
    3. Labelled 15mL and PS 5mL tubes for digesting biopsies

Samples used donor biopsies

THAW biopsies, digest for LPMC/IEL enrichments

In general, handle tissue-derived cells more gently than you might PBMC; they are more fragile having been through digest and physical disruptions. Centrifugations should be no more than 1000K in a standard Beckman bench top model. I typically run 900-970rpm for 8-10 minutes. These cells can tend to be clumpy even after filtration, so additional straining may be desired downstream.

  1. Thaw vial of tissue quickly by swirling in 37oC water bath until the only solid is small frozen ball
  2. Wipe vial with 70% ethanol to sanitize exterior
  3. In BSC, transfer just barely thawed cells from cryovial to 15mL tube by pouring
  4. Add 9mL RT 1.1x collagenase all at once
  5. Incubate 30 minutes on high speed 37oC shaker
    1. MultiTherm shaker @1300rpm, 40.1oC = 37oC fluids temperature
  6. Spin down cells at 1000rpm for 8-10min
  7. Suspend biopsies in 3mL cold R10 with 18g needle on 3cc syringe
  8. Draw tissue with the liquid up & down in the syringe, ala homogenizing for RNA extractions
  9. Filter through 70uM filtercons into FACs tubes
  10. Spin down cells at 1000rpm for 8-10min
  11. Aspirate fluids from pellet
  12. Proceed to assay of choice

REAGENTS USED for cell suspensions:

FCS Hyclone SH30072.03, lot AJA9537 56oC 30 minutes heat denatured

RPMI1640 Life Technologies 11875-093, lot 1707307

HEPES Corning 25-060-3I, lot 25060116

PSG; Corning 30-009-CI, lot 300009042. Penicillin 10KIU/mL / streptomycin 10Kug/mL, L-glutamine 29.2mg/mL

Magnesium chloride Sigma

Calcium chloride Sigma

Collagenase type I Gibco 17100-017, lot 1371008

DNase Sigma DN25-1G, lot 070M7032V

50mL tube

Bottle for preparing digestion medium

15mL tubes

Polystyrene 5mL tubes

3cc syringes

18g 1-1.5” needles

70uM filter units (BD or Miltenyi filtercon or cell strainers or screens, any style are OK)

The protocol, step by step

Human gut biopsy digestion – from frozen samples (Turtle lab protocol) Motoko 6-21-2019
Albert 8-27-2024

For one biopsy (including single or multiple pinches), 10 ml digestion buffer is required.

1 mL of biopsy + freezing medium (7%DMSO/93% FBS)

9 ml collagenase&DNase-including digestion buffer

2% DNAse stock: 1g DNase + 33 ml Glycerol + 17 ml RPMI (total 50 ml)

Concentration and reagents in Final Digestion Buffer:

150 u/ml collagenase type I

0.01% DNase (2% stock) (or 0.2% stock of 2mg/mL)

5% FBS

25 mM HEPES

Penicillin 10KIU/mL / streptomycin 10Kug/mL, L-glutamine 29.2mg/mL (PSG)

1 mM MgCl2

1 mM CaCl2

Digestion Buffer (9ml): made 1.1 fold concentration to mix 1 ml biopsy sample

1.1 x 150 U/ml collagenase type I (260.0U/mg) need 1.1 x 150U x 10 ml = 1650 U = 6.346 mg

1.1 x 0.01% DNase (2% stock) need 0.011% in 10 ml = 0.055 ml of 2 % stock

OR 1.1 x 0.01% DNase (0.2% stock) need 0.011% in 10 ml = 0.5 ml of 2 % stock

5% FBS 0.5 ml

1.1 x 25 mM HEPES (1M stock) 0.275 ml

Penicillin 10KIU/mL, streptomycin 10Kug/mL (x100) 0.1 ml

L-glutamine (200 mM) (x100) 0.1 ml

1.1 mM MgCl2 (95.211 g/mol) 0.001047 g= 1.047 mg (very small amount!)

1.1 mM CaCl2 (111.0 g/mol) 0.001221 g= 1.221 mg (very small amount!)

RPMI 1640 8.975 ml

Total 10 ml take 9 ml/ sample

RPMI 10%FBS + PSG+ HEPES (R10)

10% FBS 1 ml

Penicillin 10KIU/mL, streptomycin 10Kug/mL (x100) 0.1 ml

L-glutamine (200 mM) (x100) 0.1 ml

25 mM HEPES (1M stock) 0.25 ml

RPMI 1640 8.55 ml

Total 10 ml

(!! Made some extra volume)

  1. Thaw vial of tissue quickly by swirling in 37oC water bath until the only solid is small frozen ball
  2. Wipe vial with 70% ethanol to sanitize exterior
  3. In BSC, transfer just barely thawed cells from cryovial to 15mL tube by pouring
  4. Add 9mL digestion buffer (room temperature) all at once
  5. Incubate 30 minutes on high speed 37oC shaker
    1. MultiTherm shaker @1300rpm, 40.1oC = 37oC fluids temperature
      • Miltenyi rotator, high
  6. Spin down cells at 1000rpm for 10min
  7. Suspend biopsies in 3mL cold R10 with 18g needle on 3cc syringe
  8. Draw tissue with the liquid up & down in the syringe, ala homogenizing for RNA extractions
  • Instead, vortex 30 sec.
  1. Filter through 70uM filtercons into FACs tubes
  2. Spin down cells at 1000rpm for 8-10min
  3. Aspirate fluids from pellet
  4. Proceed to assay of choice

Prot 106M — Human Biopsy Prep, Motoko version

Gut biopsy digestion from frozen samples: 10 mL digestion buffer per biopsy, 1 mL biopsy in 7% DMSO/93% FBS plus 9 mL collagenase+DNase buffer.

The protocol, step by step

Human gut biopsy digestion – from frozen samples (Turtle lab protocol) Motoko 6-21-2019

For one biopsy (including single or multiple pinches), 10 ml digestion buffer is required.

1 mL of biopsy + freezing medium (7%DMSO/93% FBS)

9 ml collagenase&DNase-including digestion buffer

2% DNAse stock: 1g DNase + 33 ml Glycerol + 17 ml RPMI (total 50 ml)

Concentration and reagents in Final Digestion Buffer:

150 u/ml collagenase type I

0.01% DNase (2% stock)

5% FBS

25 mM HEPES

Penicillin 10KIU/mL / streptomycin 10Kug/mL, L-glutamine 29.2mg/mL (PSG)

1 mM MgCl2

1 mM CaCl2

Digestion Buffer (9ml): made 1.1 fold concentration to mix 1 ml biopsy sample

1.1 x 150 U/ml collagenase type I (260.0U/mg) need 1.1 x 150U x 10 ml = 1650 U = 6.346 mg

1.1 x 0.01% DNase (2% stock) need 0.011% in 10 ml = 0.055 ml of 2 % stock

5% FBS 0.5 ml

1.1 x 25 mM HEPES (1M stock) 0.275 ml

Penicillin 10KIU/mL, streptomycin 10Kug/mL (x100) 0.1 ml

L-glutamine (200 mM) (x100) 0.1 ml

1.1 mM MgCl2 (95.211 g/mol) 0.001047 g= 1.047 mg

1.1 mM CaCl2 (111.0 g/mol) 0.001221 g= 1.221 mg

RPMI 1640 8.975 ml

Total 10 ml take 9 ml/ sample

RPMI 10%FBS + PSG+ HEPES (R10)

10% FBS 1 ml

Penicillin 10KIU/mL, streptomycin 10Kug/mL (x100) 0.1 ml

L-glutamine (200 mM) (x100) 0.1 ml

25 mM HEPES (1M stock) 0.25 ml

RPMI 1640 8.55 ml

Total 10 ml

(!! Made some extra volume)

  1. Thaw vial of tissue quickly by swirling in 37oC water bath until the only solid is small frozen ball
  2. Wipe vial with 70% ethanol to sanitize exterior
  3. In BSC, transfer just barely thawed cells from cryovial to 15mL tube by pouring
  4. Add 9mL digestion buffer (room temperature) all at once
  5. Incubate 30 minutes on high speed 37oC shaker
    1. MultiTherm shaker @1300rpm, 40.1oC = 37oC fluids temperature
      • Miltenyi rotator, high
  6. Spin down cells at 1000rpm for 10min
  7. Suspend biopsies in 3mL cold R10 with 18g needle on 3cc syringe
  8. Draw tissue with the liquid up & down in the syringe, ala homogenizing for RNA extractions
  • Instead, vortex 30 sec.
  1. Filter through 70uM filtercons into FACs tubes
  2. Spin down cells at 1000rpm for 8-10min
  3. Aspirate fluids from pellet
  4. Proceed to assay of choice

Prot 107 — CMV Peptide Stimulation
Word21.8 KB· Yeh MD, Albert C· edited 2021-02-06
▼

Multi-parametric intracellular cytokine staining and degranulation analysis using CTL epitope peptides, GolgiPlug and GolgiStop.

⬇ Download Word · 21.8 KB

Source & citation

Document author metadata: Michael Rist; adopted and edited by Albert Yeh.

Document record

Document author: Michae lRist
Last edited by: Yeh MD, Albert C
Created: 2020-07-22
Last modified: 2021-02-06
Revisions: 5

The protocol, step by step

Multi-parametric Intracellular Cytokine Staining and Degranulation Analysis
  1. Dilute peptides to 0.2µg/mL in RPMI1640-10%FCS (final concentration in the assay will be 1µg/mL). Add 100µL of the appropriate CTL epitope, 100uL PMA/Ionomycin or 100µL RPMI1640-10%FCS (no peptide control) to the appropriate wells of a 96-well V-bottom plate.
  2. Dilute PBMC to 1x106 to 5x106 cells/mL in RPMI1640-10%FCS.
  3. Add GolgiPlug (Brefeldin A) to cells for a final ratio of 2µL/mL of cells (final concentration of GolgiPlug in the assay will be 1µL/mL) and GolgiStop (monensin) to cells for a final ratio of 1.4µL/mL of cells (final concentration of GolgiStop in the assay will be 0.7µL/mL). Add FITC conjugated anti-CD107a to cells for a final ratio of 50µL/mL (final amount of anti-CD107a is 5µL/test).
  4. Add 100µL of cell suspension (2x105 to 1x106 cells) per well to the required wells of the 96-well V-bottom plate.
  5. Incubate for 4 hours at 37ºC/6.5%CO2.
  6. Centrifuge the plate at 2300rpm for 2 minutes.
  7. Discard the supernatant. Wash cells by adding 200µL of PBS per well and centrifuge the plate at 2300rpm for 2 minutes.

Surface Cell Antigen Staining

  1. Resuspend cells in 50µL/well of PBS containing 0.125 of perCP-Cy5.5 conjugated anti-CD8, 0.25µL of pacific blue conjugated anti-CD4 and 0.1L of Live/Dead NIR. Incubate for 30 minutes at 4ºC.
  2. Centrifuge the plate at 2300rpm for 2 minutes. Discard the supernatant. Wash the cells by adding 200µL of PBS per well. Centrifuge the plate at 2300rpm for 2 minutes.
  3. Resuspend cells in 100µL per well of cytofix/cytoperm solution and incubate at 4ºC for 20 minutes.
  4. Centrifuge the plate at 2300rpm for 2 minutes. Discard the supernatant. Wash the cells by adding 200µL of Perm/Wash per well. Centrifuge the plate at 2300rpm for 2 minutes. Repeat this step.
Intracellular Cytokine Staining
  1. Resuspend fixed/permeabilised cells in 50µL/well of Perm/Wash solution containing 1µL of PE-conjugated anti-IL-2, 1µL of AF700 conjugated anti-IFN and 0.25µL of APC conjugated anti-TNF. Incubate for 30 minutes at 4ºC.
  2. Centrifuge the plate at 2300rpm for 2 minutes. Discard the supernatant. Wash the cells by adding 200µL of Perm/Wash per well. Centrifuge the plate at 2300rpm for 2 minutes. Repeat this step.
  3. Resuspend the cells in 200µL of PBS-2%paraformaldehyde and store at 4ºC.
  4. Acquire cells using BD Fortessa. Analyse cytokine production/degranulation using Flow Jo software.

Prot 108 — 51Cr Release Assay
Word125.6 KB· Yeh MD, Albert C· edited 2021-02-06
▼

Classic chromium-release cytotoxicity assay against K562 parental and K562-tCD19 targets, run at 30:1 / 10:1 / 3:1 / 1:1 E:T ratios.

⬇ Download Word · 125.6 KB

Source & citation

Document author metadata: kstankey; edited by Albert Yeh.

Document record

Document author: kstankey
Last edited by: Yeh MD, Albert C
Created: 2020-08-12
Last modified: 2021-02-06
Revisions: 3
Company: FHCRC

The protocol, step by step

Planning the assay:

Make sure your target cells are healthy and that you have enough of them. If the targets are K562 or LCL, avoid splitting them too hard close to when you need to chromate them. A 1:2 split about three days before chromating should suffice. Since the number of targets/well will be 3x103, you’ll be testing 4 effector:target ratios in sextuplicate and you’ll need targets for the Min/Max, you’ll need at least 2.1 x 105 target cells.

For the Day 20 T cell line to be tested, use 30:1, 10:1, 3:1 and 1:1 ratios. That means 30 effectors to 1 target, 10 effectors to 1 target, etc. See Assay Map (pg. 4) for visual aid. Since the number of targets/well will be 3x103 and all there are 2 target lines set up in sextuplicate, you’ll 2.244 x 106 effector cells total.

Preparing targets:

You’ll need the K562 Parental and K562-tCD19 cell lines to use as targets.

Count the target cell lines. Use the attached worksheet to record counts and calculations.

Aliquot the appropriate number of target cells into clean 14 mL round-bottom tubes, bring up to 10 mL with fresh LCL medium, and centrifuge at 1500 rpm for 5 minutes at 4ºC.

Resuspend target cells in 1 mL fresh LCL.

Chromate (a.k.a. radiolabel with 51Cr) the K562 targets for 2 hours. Try to use the freshest 51Cr that the lab contains. For each 1 ml of targets to be labeled, use 50 l of 51Cr. If the 51Cr is over 2 weeks old, use 100 l per 1 ml.

Preparing effectors during the 51Cr incubation:

See Sample Assay Map (pg. 4) for assistance.

See the worksheet for details on the number of effector cells needed for each effector:target (E:T) ratio.

Count the effector cell line and determine what volume of cells is necessary for each E:T ratio. Record counts and calculations on the attached worksheet.

Place the calculated numbers of cells each in their own 5mL FACS tube, bring up to ~4 mL with fresh LCL medium, and centrifuge at 1500 rpm for 5 minutes at 4ºC.

Resuspend effectors in 1.7 mL LCL medium and transfer each one to its own well in a labeled 12-well plate. From highest to lowest E:T ratio, this produces concentrations of 9x105 cells/mL, 3x105 cells/mL, 9x104 cells/mL, or 3x104 cells/mL.

Using 3 tips on a multi-channel pipettor, aliquot 100 uL effectors for the 30:1 E:T into all the appropriate wells of a clean 96-well plate, as indicated on the Sample Assay Map (p.4).

Using a new set of tips for each effector ratio, repeat the above step for the remaining effectors.

When finished with the effectors, aliquot 3 mL of LCL medium into two clean consecutive wells of the 12-well plate.

Using 6 fresh tips on a multi-channel pipettor, aliquot 100 uL of the LCL Medium into each of the 24 Min wells of the 96-well plate, as indicated in the Sample Assay Map (pg. 4).

Place the plate in the incubator until the target cells are ready.

Washing targets and setting up assay:

After two hours have elapsed, wash targets with 6 ml of RPMI-HEPES and centrifuge at 1400 rpm for 7 minutes at 4ºC.

Gently pour off the supernatant into a half-filled sand bottle in the hood.

Back on the bench, run each tube across the top of the rack 2-3 times to agitate the cells back into suspension.

Wash cells again with 7 ml RPMI-HEPES and centrifuge at 1400 rpm for 7 minutes at 4ºC.

Gently pour off the supernatant into a half-filled sand bottle in the hood.

Perform one more wash with 7 ml RPMI-HEPES and centrifuge at 1400 rpm for 7 minutes at 4ºC.

Gently pour off the supernatant into a half-filled sand bottle in the hood.

Back on the bench, resuspend each cell pellet in 7.0 mL of LCL medium (final concentration: 3x104 cells/mL per target). For each target, aliquot cells evenly between two clean, consecutive wells of a labeled 12-well plate (= 3.5mL per well).

Using 6 tips on a multi-channel pipettor, aliquot 100uL of the chromated K562-Parental line into all of the appropriate wells of the 96-well plate containing the targets, as indicated on the Sample Assay Map (pg. 4).

Repeat the above step with the chromated K562-tCD19 line.

When finished aliquotting the targets, aliquot 3 mL of the Nonidet P-40/Trypan Blue solution into two consecutive, clean wells of the 12-well plate.

Using 6 fresh tips on a multi-channel pipettor, aliquot 100 uL of the Nonidet P-40/Trypan Blue solution into the 24 Max wells, as indicated on the Sample Assay Map (pg. 4).

Create a balance plate using water and a clean 96-well plate available in the hotlab, balancing it with your plate on the scale.

Centrifuge the plates for 2 minutes at 1200 rpm with brake off (to get the targets and effectors interacting quickly).

Incubate the plate at 37 C in the hotlab incubator for 4 hours.

Harvesting the assay:

Carefully transfer the plate from the incubator to the bench. If the plate gets jostled or the cells resuspended accidentally, centrifuge the plates for 2 minutes at 1200 rpm with brake off to ensure that the cells are pelleted at the bottom of the wells.

Harvest the CRA by removing 30 l of culture supernatant from the top of each well into its corresponding well on a LUMA plate (which has been properly labeled with initials and date), making sure the orientation of the harvested supernatant matches that of the CRA. Be careful not to disturb the plate at any time and/or take up any of the cells at the bottom of the wells.

Dry the plates overnight or bake them in the oven if data is needed sooner. When baking the plates, be careful to not let the oven go above 50 C because hotter temperatures can melt the plates. Also place the plates on the second or third shelf to avoid putting them too near the heating element.

***It is very important to dry the plates completely before obtaining a reading on the Topcount because the scintillator in the LUMA Plate can only detect radioactivity on dried samples.**

Obtaining data on the Top Count:

Place a clear Topseal cover over the top of the plate and wipe with antistatic wipes before placing in the Topcount. Excess static on the covers can cause spurious results.

Carefully follow the instructions to the right of the Topcount. Select the “51CR Master” protocol. DO NOT MODIFY any protocols because they were designed to work optimally for the protocol for which they were named.

The data will be printed when the Topcount has finished reading all the plates. The data can also be saved to a disk and calculated with Excel (recommended).

Notes on hot lab usage and etiquette:
  1. Please follow all hot lab safety requirements. Always wear your lab coat and badge when working with radiation.
  2. Radioactive tape with the date, initials of user, and isotope (51Cr) must be placed on the targets after labeling AND on the plates during incubation.
  3. After using the benches for setting up/harvesting assays, be sure to empty the pipet bins if they are more than ¾ full.
  4. Survey all areas where radioactivity has been used: after labeling targets in the hood and on the bench where the assay was set up and harvested.
  5. Discard assay plates within 2 days of the assay date and make sure to deface the radioactive tape.
  6. If large radioactive waste disposal box is full, please close and label according to the instructions given in the hot lab orientation.
  7. If you see that a supply is low or if you are using an unusually large amount of supplies, please let the lab manager know so that replacements can be ordered.
Nonidet P-40/Trypan Blue Solution

5 mL Nonidet P-40 (Calbiochem?)

5 mL Trypan Blue (Sigma T8154)

500 mL Water

Sample Assay Map

This is a 96-well plate set up for the CRA described above.

K562-Par K562-tCD19
30:1
10:1
3:1
1:1
K562-Par Min/Max
K562-Par Min/Max
K562-tCD19 Min/Max
K562-tCD19 Min/Max
Min Wells Max Wells

Prot 109 — xCELLigence eSight Live Microscopy / Impedance Assay
Word22.7 KB· Yeh MD, Albert C· edited 2025-02-04

Assesses killing of a myeloid cell subset by CD4+CD57+CD27− T cells after CMV peptide stimulation. Uses CD29 tethering reagent and E-Plate View 96.

⬇ Download Word · 22.7 KB

Source & citation

Document record

Document author: kstankey
Last edited by: Yeh MD, Albert C
Created: 2021-01-08
Last modified: 2025-02-04
Revisions: 5
Company: FHCRC

The protocol, step by step

Live Microsocpy and Impedence Assay

Purpose: To assess killing of myeloid cell subset by CD4+/CD57+/CD27- T-cells when stimulated with CMV peptide.

Reagents:

CD29 Tethering Reagent

Tethering buffer

E-Plate View 96

Sorted classical monocytes (CD45+/CD3-/CD19-/CD56-/HLA-DR+/CD11c+/CD123-/CD14+/CD16-)

Sorted T-cells (CD45+/CD3+/CD4+/CD57+/CD27-)

RPMI 1640 media w/ 10% FCS

GM-CSF (final conc 50ng/mL); stock solution 1ug/mL

IL-2 (final conc 5IU/mL)

eAnnexinV Red (final conc 0.5ug/mL); stock solution 50ug/mL

CMV peptide (pp65, IE1, IE2)

Steps:
  1. Coat E-plate 96 with diluted CD29 Tethering Reagent (1:125 in tethering buffer, 50uL per well) for 3 hours (final conc should be 500ug/mL) at RT.
  2. Wash 2x with 200uL PBS, add 50uL media/well (see 1a), incubate at 36C for 1 hr and take background impedance measurement once. Media composition below:
    1. 10% FCS in RPMI – NOTE we will be adding 50uL media here, but final volume closer to 100uL after adding in cells. Concentration of reagents below should be calculated for 100uL
    2. GM-CSF (50ng/mL) – note stock solution is 1ug/mL
    3. IL-2 (5IU/mL) – note stock solution is 50,000IU/mL
    4. eAnnexinV Red (0.5ug/mL) – note stock solution is 50ug/mL
  3. Add cells – for tumor cells they rec 50-100k, but for primary human monocytes, I cultured up to 200k as they don’t expand (this fills up the plate). LEAVE AT 30 MINUTES RT TO LET CELLS SETTLE BEFORE MOVING TO 37C
  4. Can also set up image acquisition as well concurrently.
  5. MONITOR for up to 24 hours (or just wait 2-3 hours) before plating Effector cells.
  6. Plate sorted CD57+ T-cells split between 2 of the wells (i.e. A, C), such that amongst the 4 wells, we have all 4 combinations of +/- peptide and +/- T-cells.
  7. Continue with data acquisition to monitor target cell attachment and proliferation.
Live Microscopy Assay (For culturing monocytes and T-cells)
Reagents:

Sorted classical monocytes (CD3-/HLA-DR+/ CD14+/CD16-)

Sorted T-cells (CD3+/CD4+/CD57+/CD27-)

96-well flat-bottom plate (glass preferred, but plastic is OK)

RPMI 1640 media

GM-CSF (final conc 50ng/mL); stock solution 1ug/mL

IL-2 (final conc 5IU/mL)

eAnnexinV Red (final conc 0.5ug/mL); stock solution 50ug/mL

CFSE (final conc 500nM); stock solution (5mM)

Poly-Lysine (final conc 50 ug/mL)

FACS buffer

Steps:
  1. Coat plates in poly-lysin and let sit overnight (50ug/mL). Next day, wash 1x with FACS buffer, than suction off fluid and let dry.
  2. Sort monocytes / T-cells into RPMI. Centrifuge at 900G x 5 min, decant.
  3. Coat monocytes at 50-100k cells/well (for hybridomas, we use ~50k/well). We want final volume to be 100uL RPMI / well (about the volume post-decanting from a FACS tube). Once monocytes have been added, can dilute and add GM-CSF (final conc 50nmg/mL) as well as eAnnexin V Red (final conc 0.5ug/mL). Note that sorting with PE antibodies does NOT interfere with microscopy.
  4. Mix well by pipetting up and down 1-2x 50uL (avoid bubbles).
  5. Incubate in eSight (or tissue culture), can start image capture if desired.
  6. CFSE-treat T-cells (see Prot 207, in vitro concentration used). Resuspend so we end up adding ~50uL RPMI per well.
  7. Take out plate, add T-cells in well, and for wells without T-cells, add equivalent RPMI.
  8. After adding T-cells, add 5IU/mL IL-2 in each well (dilute so only add ~1-2uL).
  9. Incubate in eSight (or tissue culture), start image capture desired.

IEL / LPMC Biopsy Digest Worksheet

Bench worksheet for surveying lymphocytes in IEL + LPMC fractions extracted from pinch or punch biopsies, with fill-in fields for each experiment.

The protocol, step by step

EXPERIMENT: _________ Date

  • Survey lymphocyte _________ from biopsies of _____________ donors
    • ________________________ _________________(Specific details of survey) in IEL+LPMC extracted from pinch (or punch) biopsies

Set up before the staining

THAW biopsies, digest for IEL/LPMC enrichments

Set up before the thaw & digests
  1. Prepare enough digestion buffer for a total of 10mL per biopsy (single or multiples)

You will add 9mL of 1.1x enzymes to 1mL of biopsy + freezing medium (7%DMSO/FBS)

  • Determine how much overall dissociation medium you will need at a minimum
    1. The base medium can be made well ahead and stored for a few weeks cold.
    2. Add all enzymes on day of digestion
    3. Heat medium to 37oC before applying to tissues
  • Determine how much collagenase (C’aseI) you would need for at least that volume
  • Measure powdered C’aseI into 50mL tube to at least that minimum mass you calculated in the previous step
  • Calculate how much DNase you will be adding to the total fluid volume to have a final of 0.01% in the dissociation medium
  • Calculate how much fluid you will have at the end based on how much C’aseI you tapped out into the 50mL tube.
  • Add some RPMI 5% Ca++, Mg++ to the powder, invert gently to suspend the C’aseI into solution, add the desired amount of DNase, mix again, then bring fluids up to final desired total volume.
  1. 9mL X_____ samples + extra = make about ______ X 9mL= ______mL dissociation buffer:
    1. RPMI 1640
    2. 5% FBS
    3. 25mM Hepes
    4. PSG
    5. 1mM each MgCL2, CaCl2
  2. [150U/ml]final Collagenase type I (our current stock is at 340U/mg)
    1. 1.1x150U/mL = 165U/mL working solution to make
    2. 340U/mg / 165U/mL = 2.27x factor for our current lot enzymatic activity
    3. calculate about how much you’ll need _________mL / 2.7 = ____mg of C’aseI
    4. pour powdered C’aseI into 50mL tube to at least that amount
    5. 2.7 x _____mg C’aseI actual in tube = ________mL final dissociation medium
  3. DNase (our current stock is 2% kept in 66% glycerol/RPMI)
    1. 1.1 X 0.01%/ 2% stock X ______mL = _____mL DNase stock to add
  1. Prepare RPMI 10%FBS + PSG+ HEPES (R10) for washing & homogenizing
  2. Put things in the biosafety cabinet for biohazard work
    1. Small biohazard bag, medium sharps container, box of gloves, disinfection bucket
    2. Fully charged pipettor, pipettes
    3. Labelled 15mL and PS 5mL tubes for digesting biopsies

Samples used donor biopsies

THAW biopsies, digest for LPMC/IEL enrichments

In general, handle tissue-derived cells more gently than you might PBMC; they are more fragile having been through digest and physical disruptions. Centrifugations should be no more than 1000K in a standard Beckman bench top model. I typically run 900-970rpm for 8-10 minutes. These cells can tend to be clumpy even after filtration, so additional straining may be desired downstream.

  1. Thaw vial of tissue quickly by swirling in 37oC water bath until the only solid is small frozen ball
  2. Wipe vial with 70% ethanol to sanitize exterior
  3. In BSC, transfer just barely thawed cells from cryovial to 15mL tube by pouring
  4. Add 9mL RT 1.1x collagenase all at once
  5. Incubate 30 minutes on high speed 37oC shaker
    1. MultiTherm shaker @1300rpm, 40.1oC = 37oC fluids temperature
  6. Spin down cells at 1000rpm for 8-10min
  7. Suspend biopsies in 3mL cold R10 with 18g needle on 3cc syringe
  8. Draw tissue with the liquid up & down in the syringe, ala homogenizing for RNA extractions
  9. Filter through 70uM filtercons into FACs tubes
  10. Spin down cells at 1000rpm for 8-10min
  11. Aspirate fluids from pellet
  12. Proceed to assay of choice

REAGENTS USED for cell suspensions:

FCS Hyclone SH30072.03, lot AJA9537 56oC 30 minutes heat denatured

RPMI1640 Life Technologies 11875-093, lot 1707307

HEPES Corning 25-060-3I, lot 25060116

PSG; Corning 30-009-CI, lot 300009042. Penicillin 10KIU/mL / streptomycin 10Kug/mL, L-glutamine 29.2mg/mL

Magnesium chloride Sigma

Calcium chloride Sigma

Collagenase type I Gibco 17100-017, lot 1371008

DNase Sigma DN25-1G, lot 070M7032V

50mL tube

Bottle for preparing digestion medium

15mL tubes

Polystyrene 5mL tubes

3cc syringes

18g 1-1.5” needles

70uM filter units (BD or Miltenyi filtercon or cell strainers or screens, any style are OK)

Harvest of bone marrow and splenic T cells from donor mice using the MACS (Miltenyi) system, here with positive selection for CD4. Covers spleen and lymph node dissection, long-bone flushing, straining and Gey's RBC lysis.

Created: 2019-05-06

The protocol, step by step

[Prot 201] Mouse Transplant Donor Prep (BM and Spleen)
Last updated 5/15/19 by Albert Yeh
Notes:

This protocol describes the procedure to harvest bone marrow and splenic T-cells from donor mouse using MACS (Miltenyi) purification system. In this case, we are doing positive selection for CD4.

Materials:
  • Sterile forceps, scissors, 6cm culture dishes, 70-100uM strainers
  • 70% EtOH
  • Cell culture media
    • Red dot = RPMI + 2% FCS (use this to keep tissue in dish)
    • Blue dot = RPMI no FCS\
  • Gey’s Buffer, L15 Liebovitz media, MACS Buffer (can use FACS buffer)
  • CD4 (L3T4) microbeads (130-049-201); MACS system
Protocol:
  1. Sacrifice mice humanely
  2. Harvesting T-cells
    1. Dissect spleen using mid-abdominal cut.
    2. Asides from spleen, can increase T-cell yield by obtaining axillary, brachial, popliteal lymph nodes. If getting axillary/brachial nodes, better to do upper-abdominal cut.
    3. Collect cells in 6cm dish
  3. Harvesting BM:
    1. Asides from femur/tibia, can increase T-cell yield by obtaining hip, humerus
    2. Cut ends of each long bone, flush.
    3. Collect cells in 6cm dish
  4. Attached 70uM strainer to labeled 50cc Falcon tube. Use commercial 70uM strainer for harvesting – easier to mash up particles with plunger. Filter cells from 2) and 3) to new tube.
  5. LYSE RBCS: For spleen cells, need to lyse RBCs:
    1. Centrifuge, decant
    2. Add 1mL Gey’s buffer, incubate 1min at RT, top up (e.g. 9mL media)
    3. Centrifuge, decant
  6. Perform cell count
  7. Centrifuge cell suspension at 300g for 10 minutes, pipette off supernatant.
  8. Magnetic labeling
    1. Resuspend cells. For next step (adding CD4 MACS beads), note that we use 60% of the recommended amount of buffer/beads as we assume that 30% of splenocytes are CD4, and we use twice that.
    2. After calculating 60% of final spleen count, add 90uL buffer per 10M cells and 10uL CD4 MACS beads per 10M cells.
    3. Mix, incubate for 15 minutes in fridge or on ice.
    4. Rinse by adding 1-2mL of buffer per 10M cells, centrifuge at 300g x 10min, decant.
    5. Resuspend cells in 500uL buffer.
  9. Magnetic separation
    1. Place column in magnetic field
    2. Use MS column if <200M cells (mini); use LS column if < 2B cells (midi)
    3. Prepare column by rinsing with buffer (500uL for MS, 3mL for LS)
    4. Apply cell suspension.
    5. Wash through with buffer 3x (3x500uL for MS, 3x3mL for LS) – the effluent contains unlabeled cells.
    6. Remove column on separator and place on labeled collection.
      1. Put 1mL buffer in for MS column and 5mL for LS column.
      2. Use plunger to flush out the labeled cells.
  10. Count cells twice to get average.
  11. Do FACS (including CD4+ and viability dye) to get % viable for final count.
  12. Use Motoko’s transplant excel sheet to calculate fraction of cells needed to resuspend in L15 (Liebovitz) media. Make sure there is NO ALBUMIN! Note that final L15 media count is represented by either of the BM or T-cell fields, so no need to double. Final injection is 250ccs per mouse. Make sure to include ~1 extra mouse for every 4 or so sample. If can’t have this, increase injection volume to 350ccs to allow for more buffer.
  13. If saving cells for sequencing, process immediately (for TCR seq, use DNA mini kit – takes about 1 hr and requires you to resuspend cells into 200uL PBS). If do not process immediately, freeze down unused CD4+ cells.
  14. Since our plan is to do deep sequencing once on the donor cells, save 500K T-cells for DNA isolation.
  15. If saving other organs / skin, harvest as appropriate.
Notes on yield:

5/7/19: 80M B6.45.1 spleen cells (before Gey’s buffer) -> 100% CD4 MACS -> 6.17M/mL (1mL) final

7/31/19: 245M B6.wt spleen (2x) cells (before Gey’s buffer) -> 214M B6.wt spleen (2x) cells (after Gey’s buffer) -> 100% CD4 MACS ->14.75M final (average about 7.375M CD4+ per spleen)

PMID: 25938923

The protocol, step by step

[Prot 202] Mouse Whole Skin Digestion – Last updated 6/22/23 by Albert Yeh
Adapted from Andrew Wilkinson (12/16/15)
Notes:

This protocol preserves the CD4 epitope more effectively compared to the Miltenyi whole skin protocol (human protocol minus enzyme P)

Materials:
  • Collagenase Type 3 / Worthington Biochem (in 4C fridge, with other chemical reagents)
  • DNAse-1 / Sigma (in -20C fridge, aliquoted by Shu; also in 17B3)
  • Hanks buffer, FCS, EDTA, RPMI (Red dot buffer)
  • HEPES
  • Hair removal cream, forceps, scissors, shaking water bath
Protocol:
  1. Kill mouse.
  2. Shave back, apply hair removal cream
  3. ~5min later remove with tissue and cut skin
  4. Weigh skin for relative count (whole back will be ~700mg)
  5. Cut depilated skin into small pieces. Smaller the better. Into a 50ml tube.
  6. Digest (in shaking water bath) for 2h @ 37C in 7ml of:
  7. Hanks [2% FCS, 1% Pen/Strep] (Can omit Pen/Strep); with 10mM HEPES (addition per Motoko!)
  8. 7mg collagenase
    1. Lyophilized powder may be stored at 4°C. Reconstitute with sterile ddH2O. Aliquot and store at 4°C. Reconstituted product is stable for 6 months at 4°C or -20°C. Source 2: After reconstitution, it is recommended that any remaining solubilized collagenase be aliquoted into desired volumes and then frozen at < -20°C for up to 1 to 2 months.
  9. 100ul Dnase (@ 1mg/ml)
    1. 10 mg/mL solution of DNAse I in 0.15 M NaCl may lose <10% of its activity when stored for a week in aliquots at −20 °C. The same solutions stored in aliquots at 2-8 °C can lose approximately 20% activity.
  10. After 2h stop with 600ul EDTA (0.1M) for 5minutes (or 120uL of 0.5M).
  11. Filter through a 70-100uM nylon mesh. Push through.
  12. Wash with 13mL RPMI 2% FCS and count. Pool cells if need be.
  13. Block with Fc block before staining for flow cytometry.
  14. Filter directly before putting on FACS. Clumps soon after filtering. Book extra time to clean/fix blockages.

The protocol, step by step

[Prot 203] Mouse GI Harvest

This protocol describes the procedure to harvest mLN and gut from a mouse. Based on Motoko’s protocol modification of Miltenyi’s commercial protocol.

Gut Digestion (scaled for 4 samples):

For each sample, 12.5 ul Enzyme A + 100 ul (can use 50 uL to save reagent) Enzyme D + 50 ul Enzyme R + 2.35 ml digestion solution

For 4 samples, 50 ul (A)+ 400 ul (D)+ 200 ul (R) + 9.4 ml digestion solution
Preparations

Prepare HBSS (Ca-Mg free) with 10mM HEPES

Re: HBSS (Ca-Mg-free) *Note if it doesn’t say Ca/Mg free, than it’s not.

Predigestion solution 180mL; Room temperature

HBSS (Ca-Mg free) + 10mM HEPES + 5mM EDTA + 5% FCS + 1mM DTT (can omit DTT)

= 167.4mL HBSS + 1.8 ml HEPES (1M) + 1.8 ml EDTA (0.5M) + 0ml FCS

Digestion solution 10ml; Room temperature

HBSS (including Ca+Mg) + 10mM HEPES + 5% FCS

= 9.4ml HBSS + 0.1 ml HEPES (1M) + 0.5ml FCS

Method
  1. Remove mesenteric lymph nodes
  2. Take out intestine without caecum and stomach and place in cold PBS
    1. Note typically harvest terminal 1/3rd of ileum
  3. Remove fat tissues (fatty tissue lysis can decrease viability of lymphocytes)
  4. Open small intestine longitudinally with scissors and “wash” gently, changing cold “PBS” 2-3 times
  5. Wash in PBS and cut them into 3-5 mm pieces (for efficiency, can fold in half and cut)
  6. Move into 50 ml Falcon tube
  7. Repeat below steps for a total of 2 cycles:
    1. Add 15 ml “Predigestion solution” and incubate for 20 minutes at 37C (use MACSmix Tube Rotator if possible).
    2. Vortex for 30 seconds and filter through 100 um filter – note flow through contains intraepithelial lymphocytes (IELs). Can keep as “predigestion flow through” if needed.
  8. Add 15-20 ml HBSS (Ca-Mg Free with 10mM HEPES) and incubate it for 20 minutes at 37C (use MACSmix Tube Rotator).
  9. Separately, aliquot exact volume of ”Digestion solution” and pre-heat at 37C for 15 minutes.
  10. Vortex for 30 seconds and filter through 100 um filter. Combine with “predigestion flow through”. If keeping this flow through, leave on ice for >10min and take supernatant for IELs (will have to filter through 70 um before downstream application).
  11. Add Enzyme D + R + A into the tube where “Digestion solution” was pre-heated and mix gently.
  12. Transfer the intestine tissue into MACS “C” tub ewith 2.45 ml (originally 2.512 ml) of “Digestion solution + enzyme mix”.
  13. Incubate sample for 30 minutes at 37C using MACSmix Tube Rotator.
  14. Tightly close C tube and attach in gentle MACS dissociator and run program “m_intestine_01”.
  15. Detach C tube and spin down briefly with 300g
  16. Collect sample from bottom of tube and resuspend with 5 ml FACS buffer and filtrate with 70 um filter. Wash the 70 um filter with 10 ml FACS buffer.
  17. Spin down flow through at 300g x 10 minutes, then resuspend in FACS buffer for downstream use.

Prot 204 — Mouse TCD and BioMag T (combined)

Combined T-cell depletion of bone marrow and BioMag T-cell enrichment. Expect ~15M T cells per 100M splenocytes (~2.5 h); TCD of BM ~50% (~4 h).

Document author: Motoko Koyama

Created: 2019-10-15

Company: QIMR

The protocol, step by step

Murine transplant – T cell depleted (TCD) BM and biomag T Motoko (26-1-2017)

Expectations:

  1. Biomag T: expect ~15M T cells per 100M splenocytes (Take~2.5 hours)
  2. TCD: for BM, expect 50% (Take ~4 hours)

Firstly, always make sure

  1. Read again the transplant sheet (word document one). Check the strains (what kind of, how many groups and how many mice per stain)
  2. Label the tubes and lids (describe the strain and organs) (! To avoid the contamination, please label the lids too)
  3. Pick up new media with and without FCS from walk-in fridge. Put the bottle of no-FCS media (blue dot) in incubator (37 degree)
  4. All tubes in any process must always be put in order (left to right) on the racks.
  5. Before use centrifuge, always check the speed and time (1500 rpm and 5min)

Then

  1. Sacrifice strain A.
  2. Take spleen (+ lymph nodes if required) and bone with muscles.
  3. Mash spleen first. (! Spleens prefer to be mashed shortly after sacrifice)
  4. Mash LN if in that case.
  5. Scrape and flash BM.
  6. Then move strain B. (! To avoid any mixed-up, the next stain would be started once the precious strain was processed.)
  7. Repeat 6-10.
  8. Count BM (50 ul in labelled FACS tube) and keep the tube on ice (! Later you can use it as control for TCD purity check)
  9. Start BM incubation in calculated volume of TCD master-mix (!!Make sure not to grab the bottle of “Biomag master-mix”) on ice (30 mins, up to 40 mins okay)
  10. Lyse spleens (leave one minute and top-up with media to dilute)
  11. Spin down spleens (check 1500 rpm, 5 min setting), suck supernatant
  12. Resuspend roughly (don’t need to use pipette boy, just estimate with tube side-labels) 10 ml media /one spleen and take sample for count
  13. Spin down (as it takes 5 mins) and meantime, count Sp on coulter
  14. Suck supernatant
  15. Resuspend the pellet with calculated volume (based on the count ) of Biomag T master-mix (make sure not TCD master-mix) and incubate on ice (20 min)
  16. When the remaining time of TCD master-mix incubation becomes < 15 min, prepare complement with no-FCS media (!Do not prepare complement too early).
    1. Each complement pellet should be dissolved in 8mL (so ½ pellet = 4mL)
  17. When 30 min incubation is completed, fill up BM tube with no-FCS media and spin (!check always 1500 rpm, 5 min)
  18. Suck supernatant, voltex the pellet and wash again with 30 or 40 ml no-FCS media (! This is just second wash, thus it don’t need to be accurate resuspension or volume. So voltex and somehow significant volume of media to fill up is good enough)
  19. Resuspend BM pellet with complement made with no-FCS media. (If there is extra volume which you need to discard, you can increase the using volume by <10% ish (ex. 5 ml in calculation 5 to 5.5 ml)
  20. Incubate BM in incubator (37 degree) for 60 mins. !! Flick the tube every 10 mins.
  21. Measure Biomag beads based on calculation into yellow top 10 ml tube and leave the tube on magnet for 5 min (! If the volume is more than 7 ml, separate the volume into two tubes as equal. The magnet is not high enough for > 7 ml.)
    1. Suck supernatant of bead tube carefully on magnet, then add roughly equivalent volume of FACS buffer. Close the lid, flick (to resolve the beads) and apply to magnet for 5 min again or keep it on ice until the spleen incubation is completed.
  22. Once Biomag T antibody mastermix incubation is completed (20 min), fill up the tube and spin down
    1. Suck supernatant and vortex. Fill up 30-40 ml of media with FCS for second wash.
    2. Spin down
  23. When starting centrifuge (5 min), apply the bead tube ( beads in FACS buffer) to magnet (for 5 min)
  24. When 5 min spinning done, sack the supernatant above spleen pellet and supernatant of tube on magnet.
    1. Resuspend the spleen pellet with exactly required volume of FACS buffer and MIX them thoroughly into beads tube (which we just removed the supernatant in the step above.
    2. Vortex well the cells and beads in the tube
    3. Incubate it on ice for 20 min (beads with cells)
    4. Flick the tube of spleen and beads every 5 min
  25. When 40 min complement & BM incubation is completed, fill up the tube with no-FCS media (! This can be media with FCS, but no-FCS is sufficient)
  26. Spin, suck supernatant and wash BM pellet with appropriate (30-40 ml) volume of media for the second wash
  27. Resuspend BM with RPMI with FCS using pipette & pipette boy (usually I will use 10 ml/ pellet from Max. 2 spleens.)
  28. When 20 min biomag beads incubation competed, apply the tube to magnet for 3-5 min first (! You will apply one more time, so if you rush, 3 min is okay for first application)
  29. Take supernatant into new yellow-top tube
  30. Apply it to magnet for 5 min (! In this time, make sure 5 min.)
  31. Take supernatant into the final tube (! If you separated one > 7 ml sample into two tubes, now you can combine into one collection tube) and check the rough volume (! It is likely around 0.4 ml reduction compared to the first volume in my experience)
  32. Count TCD BM and Biomag T in FACS tube twice (! This is the final counts and must be accurate, so that twice and take average. If there is >1 million difference between duplicates, prepare another fresh sample and count again twice)
  33. Stain TCDBM, whole BM (counting tube), and biomag T for FACS (! I avoid FITC channel as we often have GFP or YFP marker on the cells. I like CD3 PE, CD4 or CD8 APC and 7AAD. 7AAD will help to exclude dying cells after TCD prep)
  34. TCD sample ideally has <1 % CD3+ cells.
  35. Biomag T often have 80-90% purity of CD3+ cells.
  36. Calculate concentration of Biomag T (coulter count x %CD3+ / 100)

<Make excel file>

  1. Open one excel file of previous similar transplant sheet
  2. Save it as a new experiment number
  3. Update the experiment number and date
  4. Make clear wells for “concentration”
  5. Review the transplant sheet (word document). Which groups can be combined to prepare each graft tube.
  6. Check group name (eg. Gp1+2 , Gp3+4), graft source (TCD BM or Biomag T, WT or ko etc. Sometimes it is sorted cells or CD4 (or 8) MACS purified cells.)
  7. Update the recipient number
  8. Update the number for “setup # mice” (!basically, 1 extra for 5 mice)
  9. Check the cell number/ mouse (!BM number is variable depending on experiments such 5 million or 10 million. T cell number is more variable, ie. 0.2 million, 1 million or 2 million.)
  10. Add the concentration of TCD BM and Biomag T (!! Here Biomag T concentration must be the one calculated with coulter count and FACS purity)
  11. Check L 15 volume (! Sometimes we need to use 350 ul/mouse. Thus here you need to check L 15 volume does not include mistake.)
  12. Print it out and thoroughly check all calculations are correct on paper. Usually I confirm then using the calculator on bench. Re-check transplant word document (for example, if group1 is really WT BM and T cells? not KO T cell? Not TCD? Recipient number is correct?)

<Prepare graft tube>

  1. Check which size of tube can include the total volume after combined BM and BiomagT. Prepare and label the appropriate size of tubes such as “Exp xxxx, Gp1”. (! Often there are multiple experiments are on-going. In order not to mix up with the others, put experiment number on tube.)
  2. Prepare empty labelled tubed in order (Gp 1, Gp2…) on the rack
  3. When you measure, for example, WT TCDBM, you should open the lids only of the appropriate groups. Mix well the TCDBM tube and measure the volume with pipette boy (! wherever possible, I prefer to use pipette boy and pipette tubes, rather than p1000…)
  4. After finishing BM aliquot, move on T cells… open the appropriate lids only for that strain of T cells. Close everything inappropriate.
  5. When finish the measurement, check again if labelled tubes (Gp1 , 2, …) are in order, if the total volume (BM and T cells are mixed) looks as expected (ex. BM 7 ml and T cell 1.5 ml, should look as 8.5 ml. To avoid missing component)
  6. Spin down
  7. Prepare required volume of L15 into injection tube with pipette boy (!! Don’t use p1000 if you can avoid it. inaccurate!!)
  8. Suck supernatant and resuspend with measured L15 using transfer-pipette. (! Firstly resuspend pellet well with < 2 ml volume and thoroughly move the remaining volume into the spun down tube. )
  9. Once mixed thoroughly with the total volume, move it into injection tube.
  10. L15 does not include any protein except cells. Thus, the finally resuspended cells with L15 should be injected into the recipients as soon as possible.

Prot 205 — Mouse BM T-Cell Depletion

Standalone TCD of bone marrow with the TCD master mix and complement step; keeps a pre-depletion aliquot as the purity-check control.

The protocol, step by step

[Prot 205] Mouse BM TCD Protocol

Notes: TCD: for BM, expect 50% (Take ~4 hours)

** Use TCD calculation sheet.
  1. Count BM (50 ul in labelled FACS tube) and keep the tube on ice (! Later you can use it as control for TCD purity check)
  2. Start BM incubation in calculated volume of TCD master-mix (!!Make sure not to grab the bottle of “Biomag master-mix”) on ice (30 mins, up to 40 mins okay)
  3. When the remaining time of TCD master-mix incubation becomes < 15 min, prepare complement with no-FCS media (!Do not prepare complement too early).
    1. For complement, reconstitute 8mL for 1 vial, or 4mL for half vial.
    2. Make sure to use syringe filter!
    3. Warm in incubator prior to use.
  4. When 30 min incubation is completed, fill up BM tube with no-FCS media and spin (!check always 1500 rpm, 5 min)
  5. Suck supernatant, vortex the pellet and wash again with 30 or 40 ml no-FCS media (! This is just second wash, thus it don’t need to be accurate resuspension or volume. So vortex and somehow significant volume of media to fill up is good enough)
  6. Resuspend BM pellet with complement made with no-FCS media. (If there is extra volume which you need to discard, you can increase the using volume by <10% ish (ex. 5 ml in calculation 5 to 5.5 ml)
  7. Incubate BM in incubator (37 degree) for 60 mins!! Flick the tube every 10 mins (this method results in less death than using the MACS rotator below).
    1. If have MACS rotator, can incubate for 40 mins without need for flicking.
  8. When 40 min complement & BM incubation is completed, fill up the tube with no-FCS media (! This can be media with FCS, but no-FCS is sufficient)
  9. Spin, suck supernatant and wash BM pellet with appropriate (30-40 ml) volume of media for the second wash
  10. Resuspend BM with RPMI with FCS using pipette & pipette boy (usually I will use 10 ml/ pellet from Max. 2 spleens.)

Prot 206 — Mouse BioMag T-Cell Enrichment

▼

Spleen lysis through BioMag bead enrichment. To size the starting splenocyte input, divide by 0.7 (purity) and 0.2 (T-cell fraction), i.e. ~7.14-fold.

The protocol, step by step

[Prot 206] Mouse Biomag-T Protocol

NOTE to calculate amount of starting spleen cells, divide by 0.7 for purity and 0.2 for T-cells (basically x 7.14 fold or 0.14)

  1. Lyse spleens (leave one minute and top-up with media to dilute)
  2. Spin down spleens (check 1500 rpm, 5 min setting), suck supernatant
  3. Resuspend roughly (don’t need to use pipette boy, just estimate with tube side-labels) 10 ml media /one spleen and take sample for count
  4. Spin down (as it takes 5 mins) and meantime, count Sp on coulter
  5. Suck supernatant
  6. Resuspend the pellet with calculated volume (based on the count ) of Biomag T master-mix (make sure not TCD master-mix) and incubate on ice (20 min)
  7. Measure Biomag beads based on calculation into yellow top 10 ml tube and leave the tube on magnet for 5 min (! If the volume is more than 7 ml, separate the volume into two tubes as equal. The magnet is not high enough for > 7 ml.)
  8. Suck supernatant of bead tube carefully on magnet, then add roughly equivalent volume of FACS buffer. Close the lid, flick (to resolve the beads) and apply to magnet for 5 min again or keep it on ice until the spleen incubation is completed.
  9. Repeat step 8
  10. Once Biomag T antibody mastermix incubation completed (20 min), fill up the tube and spin down
  11. Suck supernatant and vortex. Fill up 30-40 ml of media with FCS for second wash.
  12. Spin down
  13. When starting centrifuge (5 min), apply the bead tube (beads in FACS buffer) to magnet (for 5 min)
  14. When 5 min spinning done, sack the supernatant above spleen pellet and supernatant of tube on magnet.
  15. Resuspend the spleen pellet with exactly required volume of FACS buffer and move them thoroughly into beads tube which has been removed the supernatant.
  16. Vortex well the cells and beads in the tube
  17. Incubate it on ice for 20 min (beads with cells)
  18. Flick the tube of spleen and beads every 5 min
  19. When 20 min biomag beads incubation competed, apply the tube to magnet for 3-5 min first (! You will apply one more time, so if you rush, 3 min is okay for first application)
  20. Take supernatant into new yellow-top tube
  21. Apply it to magnet for 5 min (! In this time, make sure 5 min.)
  22. Take supernatant into the final tube (! If you separated one > 7 ml sample into two tubes, now you can combine into one collection tube) and check the rough volume (! It is likely around 0.4 ml reduction compared to the first volume in my experience)

⬇ Download Word · 15.9 KB

The protocol, step by step

[Prot 207] Violet proliferation dye protocol and CFSE
Material used:

Tag-it Violet Proliferation and Cell Tracking Dye (BioLegend) Product #425101 – excitation 395nm, emission 455 (use 421 readout, BV421/PB channel)

CFSE (stock 5mM, 1C2)

Reconstitution in DMSO:
  1. Spin down lyophilized reagent/product from each vial
  2. Prepare 5mM stock solution by reconstituting 1 vial of dye in 50uL anhydrous DMSO
  3. Aliquot and freeze at -80C, can be stored up to 6mo. Aliquots can be thawed and refrozen up to 2 times.
Violet Dye Staining (in vivo):
  1. Wash cells in PBS 1x
  2. Resuspend cells in 50M/mL in PBS
  3. Target is 5uM working solution – so dilute 1uL of 5mM stock per 1mL cell suspension (PBS or blue dot media NO FCS!). May be better to put dye first then add cell suspension for better immediate mixing. Note you want to disperse dye immediately, so can actually use 500uL cell suspense and dilute 1uL of 5mM dye in 500uL solution before combining the two.
  4. Vortex immediately to disperse dye
  5. Incubate at 10min at RT
  6. Wash with 9x the original volume with media + FBS/FCS (red dot media)
  7. Repeat step 6 two additional times (total 3x wash)
  8. Resuspend, count with flow

CFSE labeling (in vivo or in vitro – 1C2)

1. Resuspend cells at 30x10^6/ml in RPMI without FCS.

2. Stock CFSE 5mM (-70 C, 2B2) dilute 1/10 in RPMI no FCS (10ul CFSE stock + 90 ul media)

3. Add diluted CFSE at 4ul/1ml (for in vivo) cell suspension. May use 1ul/1mL for in vitro.

4. Incubate 10min/37C/CO2 (mix occasionally)

5. Wash in complete media (with FCS)

Eye-bleed and terminal blood handling with Gey's lysis. A healthy mouse gives ~4M WBC/mL, i.e. ~400k from a 100 µL bleed.

The protocol, step by step

[Prot 208] Mouse Peripheral Blood Processing

  1. If counting cells, count directly from the purple tube – the cell counter takes up ~11uL liquid.
  2. For eye bleeds (~100uL if not terminal), transfer blood directly to 5mL FACS tube and add ~2ml Gey’s (up to red mark of tube holder).
  3. Gey’s incubation for 1-2 minutes; while incubating, intermittently vortex.
  4. Wash twice after Gey’s incubation – unless going to FOXP3 staining kit, can just wash once.

**Note that average healthy mouse should give you ~4M/mL of WBC, or ~400k for 100uL. For IC staining, you should use the entire tube for this purpose.

The protocol, step by step

[Prot 209] Tamoxifen Reconstitution for ERT2 reporters

TamoxifenMP215673891, Mp Biomedicals Inc (Supplier Fisher Scientific), 1g

Tamoxifen is in medicine bin in the deli fridge along with sterile 100% EtOH

Due to viscosity, and need to use a needle with dead space, need to make up several “spares” worth - e.g. for 13 mice, make enough for 18

Calculate enough for 6 days of injections (schedule is for 5 days)

Use 100ul of a 10mg/ml Tamoxifen solution per mouse, per day (i.e. 1mg/day)

  • weigh out 18 mice x 6 days = 108mg Tamoxifen (in a 50ml tube) add 1080ul F/S sterile 100% EtOH
  • Vortex to mix (takes approx. 30min)
  • Add 9.72ml F/S sterile canola / corn oil to Tamoxifen/EtOH Vortex to mix
  • Aliquot 1.8ml into sort tubes x 6, cover in foil, store in -20◦C

The protocol, step by step

[Prot 210] Antibiotic Cocktail

General notes:

There is the 4-abx cocktail (gent, vanc, cefoxitin, metronidazole) and 3-abx cocktail (gent, vanc cefoxitin). Per Motoko, we used the 4 antibiotic cocktail because Zeiser lab found that it reduces bacteria dose by 3-4 logs. Motoko has used 3 antibiotic treatment for her experiments and that does not seem to make a difference with respect to MHC II expression compared to the 4 antibiotic treatment. The metronidazole does make it more logistically difficult as it has to be dissolved in sucralose solution otherwise the mice do not drink it (and would rather die) – and it becomes necessary to change the antibiotic water every 12 hours and make new stock ~2x/week.

For the 3-cocktail solution can change 2-3x / week (per Motoko no difference in efficacy).

Stock solutions:

Gentamycin, Vancomycin, and Cefoxitin stocks are made up in sterile autoclaved RO water (100mg in 1ml)

  • Weigh 100mg of powder/1ml sterile autoclaved RO water. Aliquot into 1ml cyrovials, and store at -20dC

(Metronidazole cannot be made up ahead, so must be made up fresh)

Working solutions (use 100ml/water bottle):

  • Gentamicin + Vancomycin +/- Cefoxitin (depending if making 3- or 4-cocktail solution):
    • Take a 100mg/1ml frozen aliquot of each antibiotic. Make up to 100ml in sterile autoclaved RO water (final concentration is 1g/L)
  • Metronidazole + Cefoxitin:
    • Make 100ml of a 30% sucralose solution from the sterile MediDrop sucralose and autoclaved RO water
    • Weigh out 100mg of Metronidazole. Add small volumes of sucralose solution, vortexing to dissolve, then make up to 100ml
    • Take a 100mg/1ml frozen aliquot of Cefoxitin, and add in to above solution

Collection of murine fecal samples for 16S rRNA sequencing.

The protocol, step by step

[Prot 211] Mouse microbiome / 16S ribosome RNA sequencing.

General notes:

This protocol is for collection of murine fecal samples for 16S rRNA sequencing.

Materials:

Sterile 1.5mL Eppendorf tubes

Dry ice bucket

Empty beaker (for temporary placement of mice)

Sterile empty baker

Procedure:
  1. Pre-label “sterile” (autoclaved) Eppendorf tubes (one per mouse)
  2. Scruff mouse, hold sterile eppendorf tube under under anal canal and collect sample.
  3. Put mouse in empty beaker / temporary storage
  4. Snap freeze tube at -80C in dry ice bucket
  5. Transfer to -80C.

**Note microbiome content varies over the course of the day. (PMID 25417104)

**Note fecal DNA extraction should be either all fresh or all frozen to avoid storage artifacts. Freezing fecal samples can influence the ratio of Firmicutes to Bacteroidetes. (PMID 22325006)

Additional Resources:

PMID: 34179250

For Fecal DNA extraction, can use QIAamp® Fast DNA Stool Mini Kit. Note that they recommend collecting fecal samples into soil grinding SK38 2mL tubes, which can then be subject to bead-beating using Precellys 24 bead homogenizer (cost ~$10,000!)

FOR PCR:

SFB gene amplification was performed starting from 150 ng of genomic DNA, in a final volume of 30 μL. Amplification was carried out in the presence of 1X buffer (10 mM Tris-HCl pH 8.8, 1.5 mM MgCl2, 50 mM KCl, 0.1% Triton X-100), 33 μM dNTPs, 0.5 μM forward and reverse primers (IDT Integrated DNA Technologies, Coralville, Iowa, USA), 2U of DyNAzyme II DNA Polymerase (Finnzymes, Espoo, Finland) and ultrapure water. Primers features are summarised in table 1. Each reaction was subjected to an initial denaturation step of 3 min at 96°C.

The 40 PCR cycles used were as follows: denaturation, 15 s at 95°C; annealing, 30 s at 58°C; elongation, 20 s at 72°C. The amplification was performed using Gene Amp PCR System 9700 thermal cycler (Applied Biosystems, Foster City, California, USA). PCR products were analysed by 2.5% agarose-gel electrophoresis, using ChemiDoc MP System (Bio-Rad), image acquisition and analysis was performed using Image Lab Software V.4.0 (Bio-Rad). The molecular weight marker used was GeneRuler 50 bp DNA Ladder (Thermo Scientific) designed to sizing a large wide range of double-stranded DNA.

**Note for the SFB and 16S Bac ribosome PCR, annealing temp of 58C works well

**For the CBir1 and A4 PCR, I tried 61C which gave a lot of non-specific bands – will try 65C next.

Prot 212 — CD11c Mouse Splenocyte Isolation (DC gradient)
Word20.4 KB· Yeh MD, Albert C· edited 2022-08-05

Dendritic-cell gradient procedure with underlay/overlay technique and interface disruption before centrifugation.

⬇ Download Word · 20.4 KB

Source & citation

Document author metadata vanessaR, with “ZP notes”; edited by Albert Yeh. Note: the number 212 is used twice in this collection.

Document record

Document author: vanessaR
Last edited by: Yeh MD, Albert C
Created: 2022-08-05
Last modified: 2022-08-05
Revisions: 2
Company: Qld Institute of Medical Research

The protocol, step by step

DC Gradient Procedure (with ZP notes)
  1. Spin down samples and resuspend the pellet in 5mL of gradient mix in a 15mL falcon tube
  2. Using a 5mL pipette, slowly underlay 5mL of gradient mix
  3. Using a transfer pipette, overlay 1mL EDTA/FCS
  4. Using a glass pipette, disrupt the gradient/gradient interface and the gradient/EDTA interface
  5. Centrifuge on the following settings:
    1. 2720 RPM (around 1800g)
    2. 15 min
    3. 4º C
    4. Brake off (with the brake off, this spinning step takes approximately 30 mins)
  6. Remove the material from the 15mL falcon, starting at the top interface where a cloudy band of DC’s will be seen
  7. Using a transfer pipette, remove the entire top layer (down to 4mL marking on the tube)
  8. Transfer the removed cell suspension to a 50mL Falcon tube and top up to 40mL with RPMI
  9. Centrifuge for 10 mins at 2000 RPM (around 1000g), 4ºC.
  10. Perform CD11c MACS selection (use around 10ul CD11c microbeads per 1 spleen)
  11. Check purity on FACS: CD11c+IAIE+
DC Gradient

Prot 212 — Mouse TCR Transgenic Knock-ins (CRISPR RNP)
Word25.0 KB· Yeh MD, Albert C· edited 2021-11-10
▼

T-cell activation and RNP electroporation for CRISPR knock-in/knock-out in mouse T cells. Document asks that permission be requested from Ashley Thelen or Leah Schmidt before sharing.
