  • “CRISPR RNP protocol adapted from:” — stated in the document

Document record

Document author: Yeh MD, Albert C
Last edited by: Yeh MD, Albert C
Created: 2021-11-04
Last modified: 2021-11-10
Revisions: 15

The protocol, step by step

T cell activation and RNP electroporation for CRISPR KO (Mouse)

Please request permission from Ashley Thelen or Leah Schmidt before sharing.

Mouse splenocyte isolation/activation protocol from Leah Schmidt.

CRISPR RNP protocol adapted from:

  • Nussing and Parish et al. Efficient CRISPR/Cas9 Gene Editing in Uncultured Naive Mouse T Cells for In Vivo Studies Journal of Immunology 2020 doi:10.4049/jimmunol.1901396
  • Roth and Marson et al. Reprogramming human T cell function and specificity with non-viral genome targeting. Nature 2018 doi:10.1038/s41586-018-0326-5
  • Oh and Rutz et al. Ribonucleoprotein Transfection for CRISPR/Cas9-Mediated Gene Knockout in Primary T Cells. Current Protocols in Immunology 2019 doi:10.1002/cpim.69

Materials

  • Lonza 4D Nucleofector
  • Lonza P3 kit (96 well format, snap out 16 strip cuvettes)
  • crRNA resuspended in IDT duplex buffer (RNAse free) to a final concentration of 160uM (ordered from Dharmacon, Edit-R Modified synthetic crRNA desalted/deprotected)
  • tracrRNA resuspended in IDT duplex buffer (RNAse free) to a final concentration of 160uM (ordered from Dharmacon)
  • Streptococcus pyogenes Cas9 40uM stock conc. (from Berkeley MacroLab)
  • low retention eppendorf tubes and pipette tips (for working with RNP)
  • IL-2 (50,000 U/mL stock, Proleukin)
  • PGA or ssODN enhancer (optional, but recommended)
  • Mouse T cell media
  • RPMI 1640 (1L)
  • 25mM HEPES (25ml)
  • 1% P/S - L-Glut (10ml)
  • 1% Glutamax (10ml)
  • 10% FBS (100ml)
  • 1:1000 (30uM) BetaME (1mL)
  • 1% Sodium pyruvate (10mL)
  • 1% NEAA (10mL)
  • P14 mice (P14 TCRVα2Vβ8) – mice carry re-arranged TCR specific for LCMV peptide P14 and a Tcra KO that does not develop endogenous mature TCR a/b cells.

T cell isolation and activation

  • Harvest T cells by smashing P14 spleens through a 40um filter using a sterile syringe plunger. Rinse and transfer to a 15 or 50 mL tube.
  • Spin T cells down at 240 xg for 5 min at 25C. Remove supernatant and disrupt the pellet.
  • Resuspend the spleens in 2ml/spleen ACK red blood cell lysis buffer, let sit at room temperature for 2-3 minutes.
  • Dilute out the ACK Buffer with 2x volume of T cell media. Spin T cells down @1000 rpm (240 xg) for 5 min at 25C. Remove supernatant and disrupt the pellet.
  • Resuspend cells in T cell media (at least 6.6mL per spleen). If you want a purified T cell population, use an untouched isolation kit at this step.
  • Add 1ug/ml of anti-CD3 and anti-CD28 (20ul of Ab per 10 mL of splenocytes).
  • 1:500 dilution
  • Add 50 IU/mL of IL-2 (10ul of Ab per 10 mL splenocytes) 1:1000 dilution
  • Add other cytokines/reagents for various conditions at appropriate concentrations depending on T cell population (CD8s, CD4s etc.)
  • Transfer splenocytes/purified T cells to flasks (one for each virus/condition), label with the date, donor information (ie. P14 Th1.1/1 male), cytokine condition (ie. IL-2+21), virus(es) to be infected with (ie. TCRmsln), total volume of media, and your name and place in 37C incubator overnight.

RNP Electroporation

  • Note: Use low-retention pipette tips and eppendorf tubes for all steps involving RNP (minimizes loss of RNP, important since volumes are typically small)
  • If resuspending guide RNAs, first spin down tubes at max speed in table top centrifuge for 5 minutes. Resuspend to a final concentration of 160uM in sterile IDT duplex buffer (RNAse free) at room temperature in TC hood. Let sit for 5 minutes in hood and vortex thoroughly. Then aliquot out into PCR strips and store unused aliquots at -20C, these should be good for at least 1 year.
  • Below steps are directions for 1 well of 96 well plate, with 1-2.5 x10e6 mouse splenocytes.
  • Prepare 20 μl P3 buffer per electroporation reaction by adding 3.6 μl Supplement 1 to 16.4 μl P3 Primary Cell Solution (Lonza 4D Nucleofector kit) in a 1.5 ml micro tube. Mix by vortexing and keep at room temperature until use. Buffer P3 should be freshly prepared for each experiment.
  • For sgRNA/Cas9 RNP complex formation, combine crRNA and tracrRNA (both are 160uM stocks in IDT buffer) in a thin-walled 0.2mL PCR tube. Incubate 30 min at 37C in thermal cycler.
  • 3:1 molar ratio of sgRNA:Cas9 (modified from human T cell protocol) use 0.9375uL crRNA and 0.9375uL tracrRNA per well (150 pmol sgRNA)
  • Optional but highly recommended: add either PGA (125mg/mL) or ssODNenhancer (100uM), 1uL/well. This stabilizes RNP complex and helps prevent Cas9 precipitation. Let sit with sgRNA for 1 minute before adding Cas9.
  • Very slowly pipette in 1.25uL Cas9 protein (40uM stock conc., =50pmol) to tube with annealed sgRNA. Gently mix sgRNA/Cas9 by pipetting and make sure mixture is not cloudy (Cas9 precipitates). Spin down briefly using a microfuge to collect drops from the sides of the tube. Incubate at 37C for 15 minutes in thermal cycler.
  • During complex formation, count T cells and spin down enough for 1 -2.5 x10e6 per condition. Important: do not resuspend in P3 buffer until immediately before the electroporation - leaving cells in P3 buffer for an extended period of time significantly reduces viability.
  • Turn on the Lonza X unit and select the appropriate wells using the DN100 pre-configured program.
  • Remove supernatant from cell pellet by aspirating. Ensure that all remaining liquid is removed from the cell pellet to prevent dilution of the RNP. Steps below should be completed quickly and carefully to minimize cell exposure to Buffer P3.
  • Resuspend cell pellet in warm P3 buffer (20uL/well) by pipetting up and down gently.
  • Transfer cells in P3 buffer into the PCR tube containing sgRNA/Cas9 RNP and mix gently by pipetting up and down. Try not to introduce bubbles, you do not want bubbles in the electroporation cuvette.
  • Transfer the cell/RNP mixture to one well of the Lonza nucleofector strip. Be careful to not create any bubbles or there will be arcing and the nucleofection will not work (you will know, the Lonza X unit shows a green X if successful, or yellow/red X if arcing occurred)
  • Put the lid on the cuvette in order to transfer out of TC hood to electroporator (lid for one “strip” = 16 wells does not come with the 96 well P3 Kit from Lonza. You have to order the strips to get the lid, unfortunately.)
  • To electroporate the cells, place the 16 well strip into the Lonza nucleofector (orientation matters, top tabs should have smaller side on the left). Select the appropriate wells that were loaded with cells, and select the Pulse DN100 pre-configured program.
  • As soon as possible after electroporation, gently drip in 80 ul of 37°C pre-warmed media (without antibiotics) to each well containing electroporated cells. Do not pipette up and down or disturb cells in any way!
  • Put the lid back on the electroporation cuvette and rest the cells for 15 minutes in the incubator.
  • Resuspend the cells in the electroporation well by pipetting up and down, and transfer the cell suspension into one well of a 24 well plate containing 900uL media (without antibiotics) and IL-2 (final conc. 100U/mL for the first 48 hours, then cut down to 50U/mL)
  • Supplement with IL-2 every 48 hours.
  • 4-5 days post-electroporation, check KO efficiency by flow or other method of choice.

Note: for combining CRISPR KO with retroviral transduction, we’ve seen the best results activating splenocytes for 24 hours and electroporating with RNP, followed by a 1 hour rest (15 minutes isn’t enough in this case), then transduction.

For the transduction, add the 100uL of cells transferred from cuvette directly to 2mL of filtered retroviral supernatant in a 24 well plate with 1:1000 polybrene and centrifuge 90min at 30C, 2000rpm. After spin, aspirate 1mL media from top and replace with fresh media (antibiotic free), add IL-2 to final conc. of 100U/mL. After 48 hours, supplement with 50U/mL IL-2 and fresh media if needed (with antibiotics).

Meeting with Ashley Thelen – 11/4/21

Take same TCR into CD8, take it into CD4 along with CD8. Mouse cells – having trouble transducing CD4s with TCRs. Historically, use P14 – overexpresses particular TCR, that is easy to outcompete (limited by amount of CD3) – to express TCR. There are similar lines for CD4s, but lab has not had a lot of success.

So, start with Black 6, knockout endogenous TCR, than transduce – have success with that. KO CRISPR, then retroviral delivery of TCR. Concern about CD4s. Culture conditions always skew towards CD8s. At end of expansion cycle, (after bulk), have >99% bulk CD8s. Haven’t found an efficient way to culture CD4s. Leah Schmidt is more on mouse side. Mark Jenkin’s lab – over at Minnesota. Hypothesis: strong signaling through CD4s is more detrimental than CD8s. CAR work – harder to transduce CD4s that are tonically signaling.

CD3/28 activation. Transduction is best 24 hours after activation (if too soon, goes down). CRISPR editing doesn’t really matter when you do it. Activate T cells, transduce them with TCR first, then CRISPR. Codon optimized constant region of TCR. Issue has been culturing CD4s. Historically always CD8 centric. When initially optimizing TCR ko, would start from bulk splenocyte population, mash up B6 spleen, activate for 24 hours/48 hours. As long as you look early on, CD4s are still around. KO are efficient for both CD4s/CD8s. (Gene editing is easy). Keeping them alive is difficult.

CD8 – REP cycles (up to 2 times). REP a week apart – 2-3 weeks survival. Wouldn’t go any longer. The more you expand them, the more they become differentiated cycle. For human side, 2 REP cycles is pretty standard. At least for CD8s, when you put in transgenic TCRs, and expand them on cognate peptide, CD8s thrive. However, if they’re very stimulated, they seem to do less well.

Get REP protocol. Use irradiated splenocytes – peptide pulse with peptide. Don’t sort on tetramer positive. Just do enrichment step, peptide pulse APCs. Extensively test ratio of APCs to T-cells. Can also expand non-selectively, but sort with tetramer first. For human – can use surface.

If take bulk population – CD4s.

Edited CAR into TRAC is better than lentiviral. TCRs are also limited by CD3.

CAS9 - have to pay licensing fees. CAS9 isn’t the best nuclease, just first one that we found. For academics, CAS9 is cheapest and easiest.

Meeting with Cody, Ashley, and Leah – 11/9/21

Can’t REP – just keep dying.

Go with transduction efficiency.

3500 rads; CD4 – donor mouse Tg strain.

Fold expansion – non-existent. Viability, usually pretty good – 30%-50% after transduction.

Before transduction -

Spleens ~70M cells – 10-16M CD4s. stimming CD3/CD28.

CD8s – usually get 5-10x.

Spleen –

If isolating cells on a column – getting rid of splenocytes; need to have feeder cells to bind to

antibodies. Put spleen at 1:1 ratio.

Have cells every day for the first 4 days / cytokines every day. Half fresh media every other day

with fresh cytokine. Take off half of media.

12 well plates – max for spinning.

Rowling lab – Treg therapy at UW. – culture in rapamycin – inhibits growth of everything except

the Treg.

Prot 213A — 15-plex BD Cytokine Bead Array (mouse Flex Set)
Word20.0 KB· Yeh MD, Albert C· edited 2023-04-10
▼

BD multiplex CBA for mouse. Standard stock 25,000 pg/mL at −80°C (3A4), built from individual Flex Set cytokines; IL-4 must be spiked in separately.

⬇ Download Word · 20.0 KB

Document author: Takahashi, Shuichiro

The protocol, step by step

BD Multiplex Cytokine Bead Array for mouse

Reagent

- Standard stock: 25,000 pg/ml (stored at -80C, 3A4). This stock standards are made from individual flex set cytokines combined to give a single 5x stock. Note IL-4 is stored separately.

- Diluent is in big BD box in the Deli Fridge

* Standard stock contains IL-1b (beads location; E5), IL-3 (A8), IL-5 (A6), IL-6 (B4), IL-9 (B5), IL-10 (C4), IL-12p70 (D7), IL-13 (B8), IL-17A (C5), IL-17F (D6), IFNg (A4), TNFa (C8), MCP-1 (B7), GM-CSF (B9), G-CSF (D4), IL-2 (A5).

* If IL-4 (A7) is needed, you should add IL-4 standard to standard stock

* IL-21 (B6) and IL-23 (D7) are reconstituted individually and frozen down at 10,000pg/ml for top standard (stored at -80C, 3A4). You don’t need to dilute them, but IL-23 cannot be multiplexed because of the same bead position to IL-12p70.

- V-bottom 96-well plate

- 1.5ml Eppendorf tube

Calculate the volume of the beads and diluent beforehand!
To collect serum:
  1. Bleed mice (if terminal bleed, can get ~500uL) using heparinized tubes into eppendorfs.
  2. Let sit on ice for 30 min.
  3. Spin on small rotator 8000RPM x 20 minutes, should have enough for 2 plates
Protocol
  1. Add 40ul (If IL-4 is needed, add 30ul) assay diluent (pink liquid) to the first well and then 20ul to the next 10 wells in the bottom lane of 96-well. (Note this is a total of 11 contorls)
  2. Add 10ul of 25,000pg/ml standard stock to the first well (If IL-4 is needed, add 10ul IL-4 standard as well). This dilution is the top standard (5000pg/ml)
  3. Next pipette well to mix and transfer 20ul of the diluted top standard to the next well. And repeat like this to make serial dilution

* You don’t add the dilution to the last well (blank)

1st well: 5000pg/ml-> 2nd well:2500pg/ml->3rd well: 1250pg/ml…..->10th well: 9.7pg/ml->

11th well: blank

  1. Pick up the required capture beads and detection antibody for each cytokine
  2. Take required volume of capture beads and detection antibody diluent and add them to 1.5ml Eppendorf tube separately

* The required volume for these should be calculated with excel sheet

  1. Mix the capture beads (Blue) well (vortex 15sec). Take required volume of beads for each cytokine and add capture beads diluent.

* The required volume should be the same with that of detection beads

  1. Mix the detection antibody (Red) well (vortex 15sec). Take required volume of beads for each cytokine and add detection antibody diluent.
  2. Combine beads and antibody in one tube to make master mix
  3. Thaw serum samples
  4. Transfer 5ul standard samples and serum to the well

* Standard samples should be located at the top line of 96-well. Add them reversed order

  1. Add 10ul of master mix to each well and pipette well
  2. Incubate the plate for 2 hours at room temperature with foil wrap
  3. After incubation, add 150ul wash buffer or FACS buffer to each well
  4. Spin the plate for 3-5 minutes at 1000rpm
  5. Flick the plate to discard supernatant (use quick wrist motion to flick; dab on paper towel)
  6. Resuspend the pellet in 100ul wash buffer or FACS buffer per well (pipette well). Note don’t need to pipette as symphony can mix it (you can set how much mixing – usually we do 3).
  7. Run the samples using the most recent Flex set template

*See detail of HST Experiment set up

  1. After running samples, conduct cleaning using cleaning template
  2. Turn off the switch of HTS and put back the sheath line and nozzle the way they were
  3. Analyze the data with BD FCAP v3.0 software
HTS Experiment set up
  1. Shut down BD FACS Diva software
  2. Connect the sheath line to HTS
  3. Exchange the nozzle (short one) and connect the sample line
  4. Turn on HTS and switch the mode from normal mode to HTS mode
  5. Make sure turn on Run low, not stand by mode before priming starts, otherwise sucking valve will be broken.
  6. Click [HTS]->[Prime] to start priming.
  7. After priming, make a new experiment layout using Flex set template (You can choose the newest template in the experimental template)
  8. Double click the tab named “96-well V-bottom” to open plate view

* Originally set standard as specimen 1, and samples as specimen 2

  1. Select well for standard and samples and adjust load setting as below

Plate format: 96 well V bottom, Throughput mode: Standard

Right click to show plate view.

In loader settings window

Sample Flow Rate: 1.5, Sample volume: 80, Mixing volume: 60, Mixing speed: 200
Number of Mixes: 3, Wash volume: 400
  1. Optional (Add beads-PE master mix to the blank well to see P1 gate is appropriately located and run properly)
  2. Right click on the worksheet to show up plate control panel
  3. Select the first well and click run plate tab or select wells for analysis and click run well
Cleaning wells:
  • 4 wells top row with FACS clean; 44 wells H20 on next row. Connect waterline to plate!!
Data Analysis:
  • When exporting data, export singlets only!!
Making up standards

Each cytokine comes with two vials containing lyophilised protein as a standard. These are to be reconstituted with other analytes to allow for multiplexing, except for IL-21 and IL-23.

  1. Open the vials of the lyophilised standard.
  2. Pool all the spheres into a 15ml Falcon tube (do NOT pool in the glass vial!!)
  3. Reconstitute the standards with 400µL of Assay Diluent. Allow for reconstituted standard to calibrate for at least 15mins. Do NOT vortex or mix vigorously.
  4. Store aliquots of 15µL at -70˚C
  5. If making up the IL-21 or IL-23 standards, reconstitute to 10,000pg/ml and store aliquots of 50µL – remember this aliquot is your top standard.
Software

BCAS

Login pwd; bmt

  1. Open Create blue tubes (standards) and red tubes (samples)
  2. Manually gate samples to label (e.g. “A4”, “A5”, etc….)

The protocol, step by step

[Protocol 204] Organ harvesting for CMV experiments

Updated 10/29/24 by Albert Yeh

This is a pipeline to obtain frozen tissue for PFU analysis as well as FFPE samples for histology.

Frozen tissue collection: spleen, liver, lung, salivary glands

Histology: skin, liver, ileum, colon

**Note for all frozen tissue collection, it is important to weight the tissue mass!
  1. Prep all tubes (for snap frozen, can use Eppendorf; for histology, can use 12-well plates with formalin)
  2. Collect blood (eye bleed if desired)
  3. Shave skin, take off small bit of skin, put in FORMALIN for FFPE.
  4. Flip mouse over, make long midline excision up to neck,
    1. Harvest salivary glands, SNAP FREEZE (see Fig 1, weigh tube before and after)
    2. Harvest right middle (or entire left) lung lobe, SNAP FREEZE (see Fig 2, weigh tube before and after)
    3. Harvest left lateral liver lobe, SNAP FREEZE HALF OF SAMPLE (see Fig 3, weigh tube before and after), take OTHER HALF and put in FORMALIN for FFPE
    4. Harvest spleen, SNAP FREEZE
    5. Flip over, take ileum and colon and make rolls, put in FORMALIN for FFPE.

Figure 3 – Liver LobsFigure 3 – Liver LobsFigure 1 – Salivary GlandsFigure 1 – Salivary Glands

Figure 2 – Lung LobesFigure 2 – Lung Lobes

The protocol, step by step

Sample for Monash (Purcell lab) take down 2-7-2024, wed.

Naïve vendor mice

(Albert: Marilyn into Taconic B6N Male: TCD vs BM+Marilyn, D7 takedown as post BMT samples)

All Male, age-matched (arrive on 1/31/2024, Wed): total 25 mice

Gp1: B6N JAXx 5

Gp2: B6N CR x 5

Gp3: B6N Tac x 5

Gp4: B6N Hill x 5

Gp5: WildR B6N x 5

Material

2ml Low protein bind Eppendorf tube

PBS

Change foreceps/scissors across each group

Procedure: Keep instrument clean by 80%EtOH

  1. Pre-chill LoBinf eppendorf in dry ice
  2. Distal 0.5 cm T. Ileum (including contents) and T. colon (0.5cm near rectum) and feces for Shotgun seq sample: likely 12- 22 mg snap freeze
  3. Remaining ileum and colon (to Monash): longitudinally open Rinse in ice-cold PBS cut to 3-4 mm2 and put into LoBind eppendorf on ice (up to 2 hours until freezing) after all samples collected, snap freeze on dry ice store in LN2
  4. Ileum contents (acquired when longitudinally opening ileum) for Shotgun seq sample: collect in eppendorf (normal one?) snap freeze. Can also collect colon contents in same manner.

The protocol, step by step

Dendritic Cells Activation Protocol

Rui 2025.7.1

  1. Thaw mice DC cells (mice spleen on day 7 after Flt3 IP) from -80C freezer
  2. Spin down @300xg 5min, resuspend 4M cells in 2ml total RPMI (Penicillin 100 U/ml, Streptomycin 100 mg/ml, L-glutamin 2 mM, 2-mercaptoethanol 50 mM, and10% heat-inactivated and filtered 0.22 mm, Milli-pore, FCS), split the cells into 96-well TC-treated plates, 100ul per or 12 well TC-treated plate, 1ml per
Time/concentation0ug/ml0.1ug/ml0.5ug/ml1ug/ml2ug/ml
16h
24h
40h
48h
Total need00.05ug0.25ug0.5ug1ug

Stock: 10ug/ml, 500ul per

2ug/ml group per well = 0.2ug 20ul per

1ug/ml group per well = 0.1ug 10ul per

0.5 ug/ml group per well = 0.05ug 5ul per

0.1 ug/ml group per well = 0.01ug 1ul per

  1. Add LPS, treat for 18–24 hr at 37°C
  2. After 18-24 hrs, add 100ul culture media in each well, scratch the cell plate bottom, pipette up-and-down several time, make sure cells are fully resuspend.
  3. Test the cell No.
  4. Prepare the master mix of antibodies @300xg 5min
  5. Spin down
  6. Add the antibodies, and
  7. Run cell flow

The protocol, step by step

4AB water exchange schedule
Start Date: 10/28/19 (3x IRR mice)
End Date: 11/11/19

There are 2 sets of antibiotic cocktails: these are exchanged twice per day (or once per day on weekends/public holidays)

(Both sets of antibiotic cocktails are replaced with new stock Mon/Fri (~every three days))

  • Gentamicin + Vancomycin (in normal water) (G+V)
  • Metronidazole + Cefoxitin (in 30% sucralose water) (M+C)
Mon 10/28/19 STARTTue 10/29/19Wed 10/30/19Thu 10/31/19Fri 11/1/19 ReplacedSat 11/2/19Sun 11/3/19

AMONTimeONTime
PMONTimeONTime

4AB H2O:

Stock solutions:

Gentamycin, Vancomycin, and Cefoxitin stocks are made up in sterile autoclaved RO water (100mg in 1ml)

  • Weigh 100mg of powder/1ml sterile autoclaved RO water. Aliquot into 1ml cyrovials, and store at -20dC

(Metronidazole cannot be made up ahead, so must be made up fresh)

Working solutions (use 100ml/water bottle):

  • Gentamicin + Vancomycin:
    • Take a 100mg/1ml frozen aliquot of each antibiotic. Make up to 100ml in sterile autoclaved RO water (final concentration is 1g/L)
  • Metronidazole + Cefoxitin:
    • Make 100ml of a 30% sucralose solution from the sterile MediDrop sucralose and autoclaved RO water
    • Weigh out 100mg of Metronidazole. Add small volumes of sucralose solution, vortexing to dissolve, then make up to 100ml
    • Take a 100mg/1ml frozen aliquot of Cefoxitin, and add in to above solution

Miltenyi Correspondence — Skin Lymphocyte Isolation
Word12.9 KB· ay072· edited 2019-03-06
▼

Verbatim 3/4/19 correspondence with Miltenyi technical support on isolating T lymphocytes from mouse skin, including their caveat that the Whole Skin kit was developed for human skin and their unvalidated mouse feedback protocol.

⬇ Download Word · 12.9 KB

Source & citation

Miltenyi Biotec technical support, 3/4/2019, to Albert Yeh.

Document record

Document author: ay072
Last edited by: ay072
Created: 2019-03-06
Last modified: 2019-03-06
Revisions: 1

The protocol, step by step

3/4/19 Correspondence with Miltenyi re: isolation of T-lymphocytes from skin

Dear Albert,

Leaving our Enzyme P will likely reduce the yield of Langerhans cells, but I suspect that reduced enzymatic dissociation will not impact the recovery of T cells as much.

Unfortunately, we do not have a ready-to-use solution for mouse whole skin. The Whole Skin Dissociation Kit was specifically developed for human skin and we lack of information if the kit works also for mouse skin. Of course this can be tested, but we cannot guarantee for the outcome. We would suggest to leave out enzyme P for the initial experiment.

Alternatively, we have a customer feedback for the dissociation of mouse skin which might be of use. Please be aware that we haven´t validated this protocol in house.

mouse skin & epidermis

1h incubation with enzymes (37°C and mixing)

add 2ml medium

program B (gentleMACS Dissociator)

Amount of tissue: 0,15g/tube

Medium: RPMI1640

Enzymes:

4mg/ml Collagenase IV (Sigma)

2mg/ml Hyaluronidase (Sigma)

100 U/ml DNase I

Unfortunately, we didn´t received any additional details on this protocol, I´m sorry.

We recommend using Collagenase or Liberase with a low tryptic activity, as markers like CD4 and CD8 are easily cleaved off, if the enzyme has a high tryptic activity.

Hope this helps, and let me know if you have any questions.

Kind regards,

Jeff

The protocol, step by step

Miltenyi Whole Skin Dissociation Protocol – Adapted for Mouse

Before starting:

  • Note that the concentration of enzymes are used as follows (same across all kits):
    • For every 1mL of Buffer (different kits use different total buffer volume):
      • 25uL of Enzyme P
      • 5uL of Enzyme A
      • 100uL of Enzyme D
    • Human epidermis dissociation kit uses 1mL buffer (up to 5 punch biopsies)
    • Mouse epidermis dissociation kit uses 4mL buffer (up to 1.5g tissue)
    • Human whole skin kit uses 500uL buffer (up to 3 punch biopsies)
    • Mouse whole skin kit – if we want to use 500ul buffer, than use 1/8th of the tissue from mouse epidermis kit? (= ~0.2g).
  1. Whole Skin Dissociation Protocol
    1. Wash skin tissue sample in appropriate buffer or cell culture medium
    2. Carefully scrape off the subcutaneous fat using a scalpel. Store samples in appropriate buffer or cell culture medium until needed.
    3. Transfer 435uL Buffer L and 12uL Enzyme P into the gentleMACS C Tube and mix carefully.
      1. Note that if wanting to preserve CD4/8 epitopes, do NOT use Enzyme P.
    4. Add 50uL of Enzyme D and 2.5uL of Enzyme A into the C tube and mix carefully (keep buffer at the bottom of the tube).
      1. Note that Enzyme A and D can be pre-mixed before adding into the C tube. Do not premix Enzyme P with Enzyme A or Enzyme D.
    5. Transfer one sample of skin tissue (4mm human) into the C tube containing the enzyme mix and tightly close it
      1. Close C tube tightly beyond the first resistance.
      2. Up to 3 samples can be processed per C Tube is an overnight incubation is chosen in the next step.
    6. Incubate sample in a water bath at 37C for 3 hours or overnight
      1. Longer incubation time increases yield.
    7. After incubation dilute the sample by adding 0.5mL of cold cell culture medium
    8. Tightly close C Tube and attach it upside down into the sleeve of the gentleMACS Dissociator.
      1. Close C tube tightly beyond the first resistance.
      2. It has to be ensured that the sample material is located in the area of the rotor/slator.
    9. Run the gentleMACS Program h_skin_01
      1. Note that the ECM is not completely dissociated after the dissociation step but DO NOT repeat the step as it reduces yield.
    10. After termination of the program, detach C Tube from the gentleMACS Dissociator.
    11. Perform short centrifugation step to collect the sample material at the tube bottom.
    12. Apply cell suspension to ta Pre-Separation Filter, 70um, placed on a 15mL tube
      1. Dissociated tissue can be removed from the closed C Tube by pipetting through the septum-sealed opening in the center of the cap of the C Tube. Use ART 1000 REACH 1000uL pipette tips.
    13. Wash the filter with 4mL of cold cell culture medium
    14. Discard the filter and centrifuge cell suspension at 300g for 10 minutes at 4C. Aspirate supernatant completely.
    15. Resuspend cells with medium or an appropriate buffer to the required volume for further applications.
    16. Process cells immediately.

Mouse Skin Digest (original Wilkinson version)
Word14.0 KB· ay072· edited 2019-05-07
▼

The original whole-back skin digest — shave, depilate, weigh (a whole back is ~700 mg) and digest. Prot 202 is the current lab version.

⬇ Download Word · 14.0 KB

Source & citation

Document author metadata: Andrew Wilkinson (created 12/16/2015); later edited by ay072.

Document record

Document author: Andrew Wilkinson
Last edited by: ay072
Created: 2015-12-16
Last modified: 2019-05-07
Revisions: 5

The protocol, step by step

Skin digest

Kill mouse.

Shave back

Apply hair removal cream

~5min later remove with tissue and cut skin

Weigh skin for relative count (Scales in Ch22)(whole back will be ~700mg)

Cut depilated skin into small pieces. Smaller the better. Into a 50ml tube.

Digest (in shaking water bath) for 2h @ 37C in 7ml of:

Hanks [2% FCS, 1% Pen/Strep]

7mg collagenase

  • Lyophilized powder may be stored at 4°C. Reconstitute with sterile ddH2O. Aliquot and store at 4°C. Reconstituted product is stable for 6 months at 4°C or -20°C. Source 2: After reconstitution, it is recommended that any remaining solubilized collagenase be aliquoted into desired volumes and then frozen at < -20°C for up to 1 to 2 months.

100ul Dnase (@ 1mg/ml)

  • 10 mg/mL solution of DNAse I in 0.15 M NaCl may lose <10% of its activity when stored for a week in aliquots at −20 °C. The same solutions stored in aliquots at 2-8 °C can lose approximately 20% activity.

After 2h stop with 600ul EDTA (0.1M) for 5minutes.


Filter through a 100uM nylon mesh. Push through.

Wash with RPMI 2% FCS and count. Pool cells if need be.

Block with Fc block before staining for flow cytometry.

Filter directly before putting on fortessa. Clumps soon after filtering. Book extra time to clean/fix blockages.

Andrew Wilkinson 16/12/15

Tamoxifen Reconstitution (earlier draft)

Revisions: 2

The protocol, step by step

Tamoxifen

MP215673891, Mp Biomedicals Inc (Supplier Fisher Scientific), 1g

Due to viscosity, and need to use a needle with dead space, need to make up several “spares” worth - e.g. for 13 mice, make enough for 18

Calculate enough for 6 days of injections (schedule is for 5 days)

use 100ul of a 10mg/ml Tamoxifen solution per mouse, per day (i.e. 1mg/day)

weigh out 18 mice x 6 days = 108mg Tamoxifen (in a 50ml tube) add 1080ul F/S sterile 100% EtOH

Vortex to mix (takes approx. 30min)

Add 9.72ml F/S sterile canola oil to Tamoxifen/EtOH Vortex to mix

Aliquot 1.8ml into sort tubes x 6, cover in foil, store in -20◦C

USING HALO (for image analysis):
We are permitting remote access to HALO workstations. You can use the instructions below:

Please see the following instructions for remote access to HALO:

  1. Use VPN access to connect to the Fred Hutch computer network.
  2. Use iLab (Link) to schedule your HALO workstation.
  3. Use the “Microsoft Remote Desktop” app (included in Windows or available for Mac from the App Store) to access a specific HALO analysis module as listed in the chart below. Be sure to enter your user name as FHCRC\XXXXXXX. (If already on Hutch computer, can ignore this step, just log into the computer name directly as below).
EH HALO station ID:Computer name:
HALO 1SR-HW5FGK2
HALO 2SR-HW5DGK2
HALO 3SR-11GLSW1
HALO 4SR-MJ06YNES
HALO 8SR-MJ0AAGHN
  1. Log into the computer station using your Hutch ID and password as normal.
  2. Log into iLab and start your session as normal.
  3. Start HALO and use as normal.
  4. “Finish” your iLab session as normal.
  5. Sign out of the remote Windows session. Use the window icon at the bottom left of the screen and the user icon to “Sign out”.
  6. Close your remote session window.
As always, access to HALO Link is available with just a VPN connection.

HALO workstations 1 and 3 are also available for in-person use in room DE-480.

Please let us know if you have any questions or issues.

Mtg 8/8/23 – Stephanie
Log into HALO – changes here are reflected on HALO link.
Handy stuff:
File-> Logout

Tools->Figure Maker -> easy to make powerpoint-like figure! Can lock boxes and select images to show. Can label, draw, etc… Can save at appropriate DPI for publication (e.g. 300 dpi).

Tools->Manual Click Counter
Tools->Fuse Images -> Pseudo-multiplexing (take off stain, re-stain)

Help->User guide->Algorithms – Used Indica Labs – FISH-IF v1.1 module (nuclear segmentation and cell counting in IF module with probe counting in FISH module).

Sub-bars:
TMA (Tissue Microarrays) – Need a separate session / training.

Annotation – Make new Layer, named “Training” – can draw shape, and designate only to run analysis on this area.

Brush Tool – customizable ways to select area; Scissors Tool – excludes

Bucket fill – finds an area using color / texture to draw an area (i.e. nice distinct tumor)

Classifiers -> Classifier Actions – load (did one “Classifier_AYeh_Int-Adip_sw”) in 1488-test-run folder. Intestine vs. adipose.

Classify -> Real Time Tuning – can use stack of papers to turn on/off markup.

Build new one using “Classifier Actions”->New. Random Forest works most of the time. Random Forest V2 really designs well for 1 persons model. “Click to Add New Class”. Create one called “mucosa”, “submucosa”, and “glass”. Using color and texture (not AI). HALO-AI, easy to teach. They are getting Vizio Farm (different analysis platform – can be used for things HALO can’t, but harder to learn, also has AI capabilties).

Pick spots – use real time tuning – than pick areas that are wrong and re-classify. Can also change Minimum Object Size (e.g. for mucosa / submucosa – can choose large object size). For resolution – for large areas, did better at lower resolutions. Key is to pick areas that are distinctly like one thing but not the other. Stephanie use this method to exclude the yellow stain from adipose tissue.

Analysis Algorithm – Can stick the Classifier back into the Analysis Algorithm!

Analysis – Can load default or saved settings. Default we saw was “Indica Labs – FISH-IF v2.2.5”.

Analysis Magnification – defaulted at 40x, it was having a lot of trouble, so used 20x (Can use image zoom parameter). Default Image Zoom is 1.

Dye selection – don’t count autofluorescence – so we have 2 dyes. IF Dye 1 DAPI, IF Dye 2 690 (CD3), FISH Probe 1 Opal 570.

Nuclear detection – this is what AI is for – has really improved nuclear segmentation. Things to make it better: 1) Is it over or under-detecting? (Can change nuclear size to capture the smaller guys; nuclear density). Changing the Image zoom did make them better.

Membrane and Cytoplasm detection – can set max cytoplasm radius (average number of pixels of different pigment in cells around it). It looks for average for IF.

DAPI – skip (don’t care are DPI +)

Opal 690 – only have limited # of parameters – in IF alone, have other options (like what % of radius is +). Sometimes don’t get what you want if didn’t segment well.

Opal 570 (Probe) – More parameters (has minimum intensity but also spot size and copy intensity). Can change spot size. Indica call – they doubled the magnification so they could see the magnification.

If run into trouble, can reach out to Paul, Tracy, Stephanie, can call out to Indica labs too. Stephanie will send me webinar.

FISH Scoring – 1+, 2+, 3+, 4+ - doesn’t matter for me (used mostly for clinical).
Phenotype – can change phenotype name.
Advanced – can bring in classifier that was built.
Store Object (Cell) Data – will save data for each cell!

If run analysis – it’s local. Can also run on cluster – go through “Studies” instead of “Analysis”.

After done, go to “results tab”.
Look at probe intensity and cutoffs – and can manually set this up.
Once you have the algorithms and classifiers set up, can run it from HALO link.
Annotation Layers – Can make per slide. Can also make it in HALO link.

Prot 302 — Bacterial Maxi Prep
Word18.4 KB· Yeh MD, Albert C· edited 2022-09-13
▼

Large-scale plasmid preparation from bacterial culture, with the LB/agar and ampicillin sourcing the lab actually uses.

⬇ Download Word · 18.4 KB

Source & citation

In-document header: “Detailed Maxi-prep Overall Procedure (by Steven Chang, UCSD) — Modified 03.23.2022 by Albert Yeh”.
  • “Modified 03.23.2022 by Albert Yeh” — stated in the document

Document record

Last edited by: Yeh MD, Albert C
Created: 2022-03-23
Last modified: 2022-09-13
Revisions: 4

The protocol, step by step

Detailed Maxi-prep Overall Procedure (by Steven Chang, UCSD)
Modified 03.23.2022 by Albert Yeh
Materials:
LB media (Corning, obtain from media room)
LB broth with gar (Miller’s) – Sigma Millipore – L3147-1KG
Ampicillin: -20C top rack
Step 1: Transformation

(Two days before maxi-prep. Starting at late afternoon. Duration: ~2 hours)

  1. Get the ice bucket and keep DH5a chemical competent cells (from the -80°C freezer) in ice for 5~10 minutes for them to thaw.
  2. Turn on the heat shock machine to low setting at 42°C. Add water to the surface to make a water bath for even heat-up.
  3. Put LB plate with the appropriate antibiotics at 37°C incubator to warm up.
  4. Add 1µL DNA to DH5a, keep the vial in ice for 30mins.
  5. Put 950µL LB (or SOC) media to round bottom tube (14mL) next to fire to ensure sterilization.
  6. Heat shock the vial of DH5a for 45sec at 42°C. If the thermometer reads a higher temperature, add some water to cool it down first.
  7. Cool down in ice for 2mins.
  8. Add DH5a to LB media, incubate in shaker at 37°C for 1 hour. (In the warm room)
  9. Add 50µL of the resulted LB media with DH5a to the center of the plate.
  10. Soak the spreader in ethanol. Start gas fire and sterilize the spreader using fire 2 to 3 times.
  11. Cool down the spreader by letting the spreader to touch the periphery of the LB plate. Let it cool before spreading the DH5a cells.
  12. Spread the DH5a cells using the spreader
  13. Put the plate upside down in the incubator for overnight (16~18 hours)
Step 2: Growing Bacteria

(One day before maxi-prep, starting around noon)

  1. Next to a fire, put 5mL LB with the appropriate amount of antibiotics in a round bottom tube. (For ampicillin, use 1:1000 dilution i.e. 5µL. For kanamycin, use 1:500 dilution i.e. 10µL.)
  2. Pick one medium sized colony by using a 10 or 200µL pipette tip to touch the bacteria colony and put the tip into the prepared LB media.
  3. Incubate in shaker at 37°C for 8 hours.
  4. Be sure to have a sterile 250mL LB medium prepared in a 1L or 2L Erlenmeyer flask.
  5. Check if the LB medium becomes cloudy by swirling the tube and see if there are things floating inside. If not, wait longer.
  6. If yes, put 0.5mL of the culture into 250mL LB with the appropriate amount of antibiotics (see dilution ratio above) next to a fire.
  7. Incubate in shaker at 37°C for 12~16 hours.
Step 3: Maxi-prep using Hispeed plasmid maxi kit.

(Duration 2.5~3 hours. Procedure can also be found in the kit booklet.)

  1. Turn on the centrifuge and put in the big rotor to cool it down. Put the P3 buffer in the fridge to pre-chill it.
  2. Pour the incubated medium with bacteria cells into a 500mL centrifuge bottle. Prepare another bottle to balance the weight.
  3. Harvest the bacterial cells by centrifugation at 6000 rpm for 15 minutes at 4°C.
  4. Discard the liquid. Resuspend the pellet in 10mL P1 buffer. (P1 buffer is in the 4°C fridge.)
  5. Add 10mL P2 buffer and vigorously invert the bottle in room temperature for 5 minutes. Mixture should turn blue.
  6. Add 10mL chilled P3 buffer and mix immediately by vigorous inverting. Make sure all the blue color is gone.
  7. Centrifuge at 6000rpm for ~2minutes. Turn off the centrifuge after done.
  8. Assemble the cartridge without the plunger. Pour the supernatant to the cartridge and wait for 5 minutes. Don’t pour in the solid.
  9. During this time, assemble and equilibrate the column with 10mL QBT.
  10. Remove the cap of the cartridge and using plunger to allow the liquid to flow through the column.
  11. Wash the column with 60mL QC. (The liquid level should reach to the “A” of QUAGEN)
  12. Elute DNA with 15mL QF to a new clean tube (the plastic 50mL centrifuge tube).
  13. Add 10.5mL isopropanol to the eluted DNA. Incubate for 5 minutes.
  14. During the incubation, remove the plunger from the 30mL syringe and attach QIA precipitator maxi module (white part to the syringe). Prepare some 70% ethanol.
  15. Transfer the mixture to the syringe and push with plunger through the filter. Discard the liquid.
  16. Detach the QIA precipitator first before pull out the plunger and re-attach afterwards. NEVER PULL OUT THE PLUNGER WITH THE QIA PRECIPITATOR ON THE SYRINGE.
  17. Transfer 2mL 70% ethanol to the syringe and push with plunger through the filter.
  18. Push air through several times to remove residual ethanol. Dry the tip of the QIA precipitator on a paper each time.
  19. Detach the QIA precipitator from the 30mL syringe and attach it to the 5mL syringe (remove the plunger first!).
  20. Set the QIA precipitator with the 5mL syringe on a 1.5mL collecting vial. Add 1mL of TE to the syringe. Push the plunger to elute the DNA.
  21. Use a pipette to collect the eluted TE with DNA, re-add it to the syringe, and push the plunger to elute the DNA again.
  22. Measure the DNA concentration. (Dilute it 100 times in molecular water and enter a dilution factor of 100 to the machine when measuring.)

Prot 303 — Cell Transduction (Primary Cells)
Word24.7 KB· Yeh MD, Albert C· edited 2022-04-17
▼

Day 0 onward retroviral transduction of primary cells: poly-L-lysine coating, Plat-E plating to 60–70% confluence, transfection and spinoculation.

⬇ Download Word · 24.7 KB

Source & citation

Lineage recorded in-document: Shivani Srivastava (modified 1-17-18) → Ping Zhang (Aug 20 2020) → Albert Yeh (3/23/22).
  • “Shivani Srivastava, modified 1-17-18” — stated in the document
  • “Albert Yeh, modified 3/23/22” — stated in the document

Document record

Last edited by: Yeh MD, Albert C
Created: 2022-03-23
Last modified: 2022-04-17
Revisions: 3

The protocol, step by step

Shivani Srivastava, modified 1-17-18

Ping Zhang, modified Aug – 20 – 2020

Albert Yeh, modified 3/23/22

Day 0 (Sunday - Preparation of transfection)

  • Pre-coat 6-well plate with .001% Poly-L-Lysine (Sigma; dilute 0.1% stock 1:100 dilution in PBS; 2 ml/well) for 30 mins at RT.
  • about 20 h before transfection: transfer 0.7 – 1x106 Plat-E cells in 3 ml culture medium (CM) into a tissue culture-treated 6-well to yield 60-70% confluence on the day of transfection, incubation: 37 °C, 5% CO2, 95% humidity

Day 1 (Monday - Transfection)

  • - prepare 150 μl of 250 mM calcium chloride solution containing 18 μg DNA and add 150 μl transfection buffer drop-wise while vortexing constantly (*add DNA to H2O first, then add 2M CaCl2, then 2X HBS) as below:

H2O 131.25ul – X

DNA X (18ug, can use significantly less)

2M CaCl2 18.75ul

2X HBS 150ul

  • - incubate 15 min (solution becomes opalescent) and add the 300 μl precipitate- containing solution to one well of 6-well with Plat-E cells drop by drop using a p200 pipet
  • - after 6 h incubation, replace medium carefully with 3ml fresh warm CM and incubate for additional 42 h
  • - Dilute anti-CD3 and anti-CD28 antibodies 1:1000 in PBS to get a final concentration of 1ug/ml each. Coat a 24-well plate with 1ug/ml anti-CD3 and 1ug/ml anti-CD28 in PBS (1ml/well). Seal plate with parafilm and incubate overnight at 4C. (Coat enough wells to stim ~3x106 T cells per well)
  • Day 3 (Wednesday - Isolation of spleen cells)
  • - Isolate T cells and adjust concentration to 1 – 3x106 cells/ml IMDM and add rhIL-2 to final concentration of 200 U/ml
  • - Aspirate PBS solution from CD3/28 coated plate and add 1 ml cell suspension to each coated well. Incubate at 37C for 24h
  • - Coat a non-TC 24-well plate with 400ul/well of 12.5ug/ml Retronectin. Incubate at 4C overnight (coat 1 well per million cells to be transduced)
  • Day 4 (Thursday - First transduction of T cells)
  • - Remove retronectin solution
  • - Block the first RN-coated plate with 500ul/well 2% BSA in PBS (incubate 30 mins, 37C). Rinse wells with PBS. (seems not necessary)
  • - Harvest virus supernatant and filter through 0.45 μm filter.
  • - Add 1-2ml virus supernatant to each well of RetroNectin-coated plate and coat plates by centrifugation (2500 g, 1.5 – 2 h, 32 °C).
  • - Harvest T cells from previous day, spin down and adjust concentration to 1x106 cells/ml mTCM + 200 IU/ml rhIL-2 + 1x106 CD3/28 dynabeads/ml (pre-wash dynabeads and resuspend in IMDM, 1 million beads = 25ul) (bead to cell ratio should be 1:1)
  • - Discard virus supernatant and transfer 1 ml bead/cell suspension onto pre-coated wells (can add more virus if excess), spinoculate at 1000 g, 30 min, 32 °C, incubate cells over night at 37C.

Day 6 (Saturday - T cell culturing)

  • - Harvest T cells, spin down (400 g, 5 min), adjust concentration to 1x106 cells/ml mTCM + rhIL-2 (100U/ml) + 10 IU/ml IL-15 (50ng/ml) and plate in appropriate TC vessel.

Day 8+ (Monday and following days - T cell culturing)

  • - Harvest T cells and remove Dynabeads by placing in magnetic holder for 2 minutes. Collect supernatant into new 15ml conical, spin down (400 g, 5 min), and adjust concentration to 1x106 cells/ ml in mTCM + 50ng/ml IL-15
    1. - Take a small aliquot of T cells to check transduction by flow. You can further enrich transduced cells by sorting or using magnetic isolation kits targeting a transduction marker
    2. - Transfer cells in vivo and/or setup in vitro assays between Mon-Wed (earlier is better, as mouse T cell decline quickly once beads have been removed)
    3. - If keeping cells in culture longer, resuspend in IMDM at 1x106 c/ml + 10U/ml IL-15
Reagents

Culture medium (CM): Dulbecco’s modified Eagle’s medium (DMEM, Invitrogen), 10% heat-inactivated fetal calf serum (FCS; Biochrom AG) and 100 IU/ml penicillin, streptomycin (Invitrogen)

CalPhos Mammalian Transfection Kit: Clontech, #631312

Calcium phosphate buffer (2.5 M): 36.75 g calcium chloride (Sigma Aldrich), add 100 ml H2O

Transfection buffer: 1.6 g NaCl, 74 mg KCl, 50 mg Na2HPO4, 1g HEPES (all Sigma Aldrich), add 100 ml H2O, pH 6.7

The protocol, step by step

Transduction of primary mouse T cells

Shivani Srivastava, modified 1-17-18

Ping Zhang, modified Aug – 20 – 2020

Albert Yeh, modified 3/23/22

Materials:

- 58 alpha-/beta- T-hybridomas (NFAT-GFP)

- Retronectin (stock solution at -80C 1mg/mL, dilute in PBS) – 4th shelf 2nd drawer, C1

- Already harvested virus

Step 1: Preparation of plate:

  • Coat a non-TC 24-well plate with 400ul/well of 12.5ug/ml Retronectin. Incubate at 4C overnight (coat 1 well per million cells to be transduced). May also coat 2hr @ RT or 37C.

Step 2: Transduction:

  • Remove retronectin solution
  • Block the first RN-coated plate with 500ul/well 2% BSA in PBS (incubate 30 mins, 37C). Rinse wells with PBS. (seems not necessary) – May avoid BSA step
  • Add 1-2ml virus supernatant to each well of RetroNectin-coated plate and coat plates by centrifugation (3000 g, 1.5 – 2 h, 32 °C).
  • Suction part of viral media out, can leave a bit behind
  • Add 1M/mL hybridoma
  • spinoculate at 800 g, 1-30 min (1 minute ok); make sure to set acceleration to “4” so keep cells from flinging to edge of wells, 32 °C.
  • Next day, change media or split depending on confluency

Prot 305 — eSight Hybridoma Screen and MLR
Word22.9 KB· Yeh MD, Albert C· edited 2023-08-27
▼

Hybridoma screening and mixed lymphocyte reaction on the Agilent xCELLigence eSight, using retronectin-coated plates and APCs.

⬇ Download Word · 22.9 KB

Source & citation

In-document header: “Albert Yeh, modified 4/17/22”.
  • “Albert Yeh, modified 4/17/22” — stated in the document

Document record

Last edited by: Yeh MD, Albert C
Created: 2022-08-09
Last modified: 2023-08-27
Revisions: 10

The protocol, step by step

Albert Yeh, modified 4/17/22

Materials:

- 58 alpha-/beta- T-hybridomas (NFAT-GFP)

- Retronectin (stock solution at -80C 17C1 1mg/mL) – 4th shelf 2nd drawer, top – dilute to 12.5 ug/ml (typically add 4mL PBS)! (in PBS)

- APCs

- Agilent xCELLigence Esight

- Peptide (stock peptide stored at 5mg/mL; working concentration should be ~5ug/mL.

- Note that SFB Peptide (DVQ) is stored in dH20 while CBir1 peptide (YSN) is stored in DMSO. Stock stored in -20C fridge next to Deli Fridge

Basic Hybridoma Screen Protocol

Step 1: Preparation of plate:

  • Coat a TC-treated 96-well plate with 100ul/well of 12.5ug/ml Retronectin. Incubate at 4C overnight (coat 1 well per million cells to be transduced). May also coat 2hr @ RT.

Step 2: Plating T-hybridomas

  • Remove retronectin solution
  • Add 50k T-hybridoma cells (in 100uL solution)
  • Spinoculate at 800 g, 1min; make sure to set acceleration to “4” so keep cells from flinging to edge of wells, 32 °C.

Step 3: Adding APCs

  • Optimal T-hybridoma to APC ratio appears to be ~1:20
  • Make separate solution at 1M APCs/100uL (note if need to incubate APCs with antigen, this has to be worked out – for first experiment, we used splenocytes as target (CD19).
  • Add 100uL APCs into each desired well in 96-well plate; add in “drop-wise” fashion to not disturb T-hybridomas

Step 4: Running Esight

  • Can begin measurements immediately
  • Set GFP exposure time to 300-500ms
  • Note NFAT-GFP reporter should be detectable ~4-8 hr (4hr if manually look at images, 8hrs is when Esight really should start picking up a signal on image processing)

Notes:

First run, I used CAR19 transfected T-hybridomas

MLR with Intestinal Epithelial Cell
  • Sort IELs (CD326+/CD45.2-) from small intestine of female mice. (See Motoko’s notes below from her 2019 Immunity paper)
  • Can culture up to 1M APCs/100uL into plated hybridomas per basic screen protocol above
  • Pulse peptide at 5ug/mL (note stock peptide stored at 5mg/mL.
  • Note don’t need to add rIL2 if using hybridomas.
  • Per Motokos, IELs start to die pretty quickly right after being sorted.

Per Motoko’s Immunity paper:

Carboxy fluorescein diacetate succinimidyl ester (CFSE) labeling was performed as described (Koyama et al., 2015). In round-bottom 96-well plates, sort-purified CFSE-labeled Marilynluc+ cells (CD90.1+) were stimulated with sort-purified intestinal epithelial cells (IEC) (CD326+CD45.2neg) from the small intestine of female or male B6.WT mice which had undergone TBI 20h before, in the presence of rhIL-2 (100 U/ml) (Zhang et al., 2017). Seven days later, Marilynluc+ T cells (CD90.1+CD4+CD45.2+) were assessed for CFSE dilution and expression of CD69 by flow cytometry. For CD80 blocking, purified CD80 mAb (clone 16-10A1, BioLegend) or its isotype control (Armenian Hamster IgG, BioLegend) was added to cultures at 1 mg / ml.

Prot 306 — TCR Cas9 RNP Knockout

Day-by-day schedule for knocking out the endogenous TCR in transduced CD8 T cells (1e6 per construct) using Cas9 RNP.

⬇ Download Word · 18.2 KB

Last modified: 2022-04-18
Revisions: 3

The protocol, step by step

Protocol from Tamer Shabaneh on 4/18/22

Thursday:

Transduce 1e6 CD8 T cells per construct.

Friday:

# cultures to CRISPR

  • Warm up IL-2/mTCM
    • Thaw P3 buffer (15uL / rxn; ~150uL)
    • Aliquot some SF RPMI1640
  • Constitute TRAC and TRBC at 120uM with buffer IDT. (e.g. 1.5nmole in 15uL IDT)
  • Prep Cas9 by dilution 1:1 with IDT buffer. 10uL Cas9 + 10uL IDT buffer.
  • On ice, mix:
    • 10uL TRAC + 10uL Cas9
    • 10uL TRBC + 10uL Cas9
  • Prep RNPs in PCR tubes:
    • Anneal RNPs at 37C for 15’
    • Place RNPs on ice; mix the TRAC and TRBC.
    • Transfer 4uL / PCR tube. # tubes total. Set aside.
  • Turn on Lonza X unit and select program:
    • Select the wells; chose mouse T cell unstim (DN100). Open to have ready.
  • Prep T cells in Eppendorf tube then PCR tubes:
    • Harvest T cells and debead.
    • Transfer the cells to an Eppendorf or a falcon tube. Spin 400rcf 5min.
    • Aspirate sup and resuspend in 200uL SF RPMI. Transfer to a new, sterile PCR tube strip. Spin 400rcf 5min on a PCR rack.
      • Meanwhile, transfer each bead sample in 900uL IL2mTCM to respective 24 well plates.
    • Use multichannel to remove most of the sup from the PCR tubes. Then return with P20 to remove remainder of sup, so that only the cell pellet remains.
    • Resuspend in 15uL P3 buffer. With multichannel if possible. Immediately proceed
  • Transfer T cells to the PCR tubes containing RNPs. R/S. avoid bubbles.
    • Immediately transfer to mini-cuvettes and electroporate.
    • Drip 80uL prewarmed IL-2mTCM. Do not resuspend cells.
    • Rest cells in 37C incubator for 15’
    • Resuspend cells and transfer to the 24-wells containing 900uL IL-2mTCM/beads.

Saturday:

Culture in twice the volume with IL-15mTCM; per mouse T cell culture SOP.

Prot 307 — Transfection of Plat-E Cells (retrovirus)

Two methods side by side: Cal-Phos (note that TAE buffer interferes — store plasmid DNA in dH₂O) and Xfect.

⬇ Download Word · 27.7 KB

The protocol, step by step

Transfection of Plat E cells for generation of retrovirus

Method 1: Using Cal Phos (note TAE buffer of DNA can interfere with results, use dH20 to store plasmid DNA!)

Method 2: Using XFect (Tamer from Riddell lab prefers this method – more expensive, but less “finnicky” than Cal Phos.

METHOD 1: Cal Phos

From Thermofisher (https://www.thermofisher.com/order/catalog/product/K278001#:~:text=The%20calcium%20phosphate%20transfection%20method,enters%20the%20cell%20by%20endocytosis): The calcium phosphate transfection method for introducing DNA into mammalian cells is based on forming a calcium phosphate-DNA precipitate. Calcium phosphate facilitates the binding of the DNA to the cell surface. DNA then enters the cell by endocytosis. The method was first developed by Graham and van der Ebb and was later modified by Wigler. The procedure is routinely used to transfect a wide variety of cell types for transient expression or for producing stable transformants. The DNA is mixed directly with a concentrated solution of CaCl2, which is then added dropwise to a phosphate buffer to form a fine precipitate. Aeration of the phosphate buffer while adding the DNA-CaCl2 solution helps to ensure that the precipitate that forms is as fine as possible, which is important because clumped DNA will not adhere to or enter the cell as efficiently.

Shivani Srivastava, modified 1-17-18

Ping Zhang, modified May – 13 – 2021

Albert modified 5/10/2022

Day 0 (Preparation of transfection)

  • Pre-coat 6-well plate with .001% Poly-L-Lysine (Sigma; dilute 0.1% stock 1:100 dilution in PBS; 2 ml/well) for 30 mins at RT.
  • about 20 h before transfection: transfer 0.7 – 1x106 Plat-E cells in 3 ml culture medium (CM) into a tissue culture-treated 6-well to yield 60-70% confluence on the day of transfection, incubation: 37 °C, 5% CO2, 95% humidity

Day 1 (Transfection)

  • - Prepare 300ul transfection mix for each well as below
  • 1) Add 18ug DNA to H2O, then 2M CaCl2 while vortexing (probably not necessary) to 150ul, with CaCl2 at 250mM;
  • 2) Add 150ul 2X HBS to the above mixture drop-wise while vortexing gently.

H2O 131.25ul – X

DNA X (18ug, can use less)

2M CaCl2 18.75ul (final dilution 1:16, i.e. 125mM)

2X HBS 150ul

  • - Incubate 15 min and add the 300μl precipitate- containing solution to one well of 6-well with Plat-E cells drop by drop using a p200 pipet
  • - After 6 h incubation, replace medium carefully with 3 – 4ml fresh warm medium

Day 3 (1st Harvest)

  • - Harvest viral supernatant 42 hours after transfection

Day 4 (2nd Harvest)

  • - Harvest viral supernatant after another 24 hours
  • - Filter 0.45um, snap freeze (crushed dry ice or cold ethanol).
  • - Check expression of hEGFR on Plat E cells
Reagents

Culture medium (CM): (Complete DMEM) Dulbecco’s modified Eagle’s medium (DMEM, Invitrogen), 10% heat-inactivated fetal calf serum (FCS; Biochrom AG) and 100 IU/ml penicillin, streptomycin (Invitrogen), HEPES and Glutamine.

Sample cDMEM Recipe

1,000 DMEM

25mL 1M HEPES

100mL FBS

10mL 200mM L-glutamine

10mL 10,000U/mL Pen/strep

CalPhos Mammalian Transfection Kit: Clontech, #631312

Calcium phosphate buffer (2.5 M): 36.75 g calcium chloride (Sigma Aldrich), add 100 ml H2O

Transfection buffer: 1.6 g NaCl, 74 mg KCl, 50 mg Na2HPO4, 1g HEPES (all Sigma Aldrich), add 100 ml H2O, pH 6.7

METHOD 2: XFect

From Takara: (https://www.takarabio.com/products/gene-function/transfection-reagents/plasmid-transfection-reagents/xfect-reagent) The Xfect reagent is a biodegradable transfection polymer, screened from a pool of 2,300 candidates for its very low cytotoxicity profile and high transfection efficiency. The protocol is far simpler than with most commercially available transfection reagents, it is performed in a single tube, in the presence of serum, and with no requirement for optimization of plasmid to polymer ratios.

The Xfect reagent also outperforms popular competitor transfection reagents in Jurkat cells, which are notoriously difficult to transfect. Xfect Transfection Reagent produced >40-fold higher transfection efficiency than Product L. This transfection reagent can also be used to transfect human adult stem cells. We used the Xfect reagent to transfect human adipose-derived stem cells (hADSCs) with a transfection efficiency of 92%.

XFect:

7/22/22: Tamer: The 2 wells I transfected for each plasmid were 6-well-plate wells of platEs. They were plated the day before at 0.75e6 / 3mL cDMEM. We start Xfect transfections in the afternoons and leave them overnight before refreshing the media and then collecting sup after ~24 hours.

  • Monday
    • Plate PlatE in 6 well (TC-treated) dish (0.75e6/3mL cDMEM)
  • Tuesday
    • Change media fresh 3mL
    • Make 100ul mix (of 18ug plasmid + water/reaction buffer), vortex well
    • Add 2.25ul Xfect polymer (vortex stock solution well)
    • Vortex mix with polymer and solution very well.
    • Incubate 10min at room temp
    • Add dropwise to respective PlatE well
    • Place in incubator overnight
  • Wednesday
    • Refresh media
  • Thursday (our transduction day)
    • Collect and filter virus (0.45uM filter), capture retrovirus on RN coated plate…
    • Filter 0.45um, snap freeze (crushed dry ice or cold ethanol).

Prot 309 — Vector Sequencing (MP71)

Verification of common vectors, with the MP71 murine expression vector primer pair pF TS41 / pR TS42 and PCR program.

⬇ Download Word · 18.2 KB

Created: 2023-01-03

The protocol, step by step

Vector Sequencing

Contains protocol for verifying common vectors:

  1. MP71 (murine expression vector)
    1. forward primer pF TS41 (TCTTGTCTGCTGCAGCATCGT)
    2. reverse primer pR TS42 (TCCCATAAAATGTAAATGATTGCCCCACC)
    3. PCR program:
      1. 96C – 1minute
      2. 25 cycles of:
        1. 96C – 10 sec
        2. 50C – 5 sec
        3. 60C – 4 min
      3. 4C – hold
    4. Materials:
      1. 400ng template – so 4uL if 100ng/uL
      2. 2uL BigDye mix
      3. 0.5uL DMSO (at 5-10% final conc) – used to reduce secondary structures that can inhibit progress of polymerase!
      4. 0.5uL Primers (@10uM)
      5. ddH20 until 10uL

Prot 311 — qPCR (SYBR Green, Roche LightCycler 480)
Word19.0 KB· Yeh MD, Albert C· edited 2023-02-23
▼

SYBR Green qPCR on the Roche LightCycler 480 in the Immune Monitoring Core.

⬇ Download Word · 19.0 KB

Source & citation

Author metadata Shivani; edited by Albert Yeh.

Document record

Document author: Shivani
Last edited by: Yeh MD, Albert C
Created: 2023-02-22
Last modified: 2023-02-23
Revisions: 9

The protocol, step by step

Using SYBR Green, Roche Lightcycler 480

Materials:
SYBR Green Reagent (SsoAdvanced Universal SYBR Green Supermix [BIO-RAD])

Roche Lightcycler 480 (Immune Monitoring Core)

Forward and Reverse primers

DNA (I used 5ng)

Nuclease-free water

  1. Total volume: 20uL
    1. 10uL SYBR green
    2. Fwd primer (20uM): 0.75uL
    3. Rev primer (20uM): 0.75uL
    4. 8.5uL total of:
      1. DNA: 5ng
      2. dH20 (nuclease free): make up 8.5uL with DNA
  2. Cycles: 40
    1. 95C x 3min
    2. Repeat 40x:
      1. 95C x 30sec
      2. Annealing temp x 30 sec
      3. 72C Extension x 60 sec
    3. 95C x 10 sec
    4. Melting curve 95->65C at increments of 1C
  3. Analysis:
    1. Abs quant/fit curve (adjust noise band if reads off)
      1. Note Ct/Cp: every 3 cycle difference is ~10x difference in quantity
    2. Melting temp analysis
  4. Note:
    1. Include positive AND negative controls!

PCR Primer (slide deck)
PowerPoint1.2 MB
▼

Background slides on PCR primer design.

⬇ Download PowerPoint · 1.2 MB

Source & citation

Slide deck of external origin.

Document record

No embedded metadata (legacy binary format).

Slide by slide

Slide 1 · Polymerase
  • Chain
  • Reaction
  • PCR
Slide 2 · What is PCR?
  • An in vitro process that detects, identifies, and copies (amplifies) a specific piece of DNA in a biological sample.
  • Discovered by Dr. Kary Mullis in 1983.
  • A technique that has revolutionized modern molecular biology.
Slide 3 · "Beginning with a single molecule of genetic material DNA, PCR can generate 100 billion similar molecules in an afternoon. The reaction is easy to execute. It requires no more than a test tube, a few simple reagents and a source of heat. The DNA sample can be pure, or it can be a minute part of an extremely complex mixture of biological materials. The DNA may come from a hospital tissue specimen, from a single human hair, from a drop of dried blood at the scene of a crime, from the tissues of a mummified brain or from a 40,000-year-old wooly mammoth frozen in a glacier.“
  • -Kary Mullis, Scientific American
Slide 4 · PCR Requires the following:
  • Template DNA to be amplified
  • Pair of DNA primers
  • Thermostable DNA polymerase
  • dNTPs
  • Buffer to maintain pH and to provide Magnesium Ions
  • Thermal cycler
Slide 5 · Reaction Components
  • www.accelrys.com
  • 1. Template DNA
  • A sequence of DNA that is to be copied. Also called target DNA.
  • Can amplify (copy) a piece of DNA ~50 to ~4000 base pairs long (maybe more, depending on ingredients).
  • DNA must be isolated from an organism before it can be copied (remember Cell lysis, Protein denaturation, DNA precipitation)
Slide 6 · Reaction Components
  • 2. A Pair of DNA primers
  • In the cell (in vivo), primers are short RNA strands that serve as a starting point for DNA replication
  • In a PCR reaction (in vitro), Primers are short synthetic strands of single stranded DNA that exactly match the beginning and the end of the DNA fragment to be amplified.
  • Reverse Primer
  • Forward Primer
  • Target DNA length
  • 3’
  • 5’
  • 3’
  • 5’
  • 5’
  • 5’
  • Genomic DNA
  • 3’
  • 3’
Slide 7 · Reaction Components
  • 3. DNA polymerase
  • Polymerase builds a new DNA strand in the 5’ to 3’ direction.
  • The newly-polymerized molecule is complementary to the template strand and identical to the template's partner strand.
  • DNA polymerase
Slide 8 · DNA polymerase must be Thermostable (Heat–stable) because of high temperatures used in PCR
  • Called Taq polymerase because it is isolated from the bacteria Thermus aquaticus (they live in hot springs)
  • Brock & Freeze, 1969
Slide 9 · Reaction Components
  • 4. dNTPs
  • dNTPs (deoxynucleosides) are the building blocks in the PCR Reaction.
  • They are the monomers that DNA polymerase uses to form DNA…..the A’s, T’s, C’s and G’s that will build the new strand of DNA.
  • A
  • A
  • A
  • A
  • A
  • A
  • A
  • T
  • T
  • T
  • T
  • G
  • T
  • T
  • C
  • C
  • C
  • C
  • C
  • C
  • C
  • G
  • G
  • G
  • G
  • G
  • G
  • G
Slide 10 · Reaction Components
  • 5. Buffer
  • To work properly, Taq needs mg++
  • The concentration of magnesium ions needs to be optimized with each target and primer combination.
  • Too little magnesium could equal little or no PCR product, too much could mean unwanted product….a fine line.
  • Buffer also maintains pH
Slide 11 · How Does PCR Work? A Three-Step Process Each step happens at a different temperature
  • Step 1: Denaturation
  • Step 2: Annealing
  • Step 3: Extension
  • 1 cycle
Slide 12 · How Does PCR Work?Step 1: Denaturation
  • Heat over 90ºC breaks the hydrogen bonds of DNA and separates double-stranded molecule into two single strands
  • Double Stranded DNA target
  • Denatured single strand
  • Denatured single strand
Slide 13 · How Does PCR Work?Step 2: Annealing - Primer Binding to Targetalso called Hybridization
  • Temperature is reduced ≈ 50-65ºC
  • (Annealing temperature depends on primer length and G-C content. )
  • 5’
  • 3’
  • 3’
  • 5”
  • 5’
  • 3’
  • Template Strand #1
  • Reverse Primer
  • Template Strand #2
  • Forward Primer
  • 3’
  • 5’
Slide 14 · How Does PCR Work? Step 3: Extension
  • 5’
  • 3’
  • 3’
  • 5’
  • 5’
  • 3’
  • Taq
  • Taq
  • Template Strand #1
  • Template Strand #2
  • Temperature is increased≈ 72ºC
  • (taq’s ideal temperature)
Slide 15 · Equipment
  • Thermal cycler
  • Is needed for PCR
  • Thermal cyclers have metal heat blocks with holes where PCR reaction tubes can be inserted. The thermalcycler then raises and lowers the temperature of the block at each step (denaturation ~95 ͦC, annealing ~55 ͦC and extension 72 ͦC)
Slide 16 · PCRa CHAIN reaction
  • At the end of the first PCR cycle, there are now two new DNA strands identical to the original target
  • Multiple Cycles (30-40)
  • Exponential Growth
  • # of Copies =2n
  • (Where n is the number of cycles)
Slide 17 · Exponential Amplification of the Target DNA Sequence
Slide 18 · BEWARE !
  • Other DNA can contaminate the PCR reaction
  • Sources:
  • The person who is setting up the reaction
  • The tubes
  • The enzymes, buffers or water used in the reaction
  • VALIDATION
  • Do a negative control (no DNA) to validate that the PCR product is amplified from the intended DNA, not some other source of DNA.
  • A positive control using DNA with good primers validates that the reaction conditions and thermal cycler work properly.
Slide 19 · PCR: Analysis
  • At the end of a PCR reaction, there is a A LOT more of your target DNA than before the reaction started…billions of copies!
  • Now the sample is large enough to be seen on a gel and analyzed.
Slide 20 · Analysis of PCR ResultsGel Electrophoresis
  • 100 bp
  • 200 bp
  • PCR Target Band
  • A DNA Ladder of known size is run along with the samples. This allows analysis of the size of the piece of DNA amplifed by PCR.
Slide 21 · PCR Product – An End and a Means
  • An end PCR can be the final step in analyzing or answering a question. An example is food samples. PCR can identify a piece of DNA that indicates genetic modification, or contamination by bacteria.
  • A means PCR can amplify a gene for further study, or for cloning.
Slide 22 · PCRA powerful, versatile tool
  • There are many uses for PCR in a endless array of scientific fields:
  • DNA sequencing
  • DNA profiling (fingerprinting)
  • Making recombinant DNA for GMOs
  • Detecting foreign organisms in food
  • Salmonella, E. coli
  • Detecting the cause of an infection or disease
  • Lyme Disease, Strep throat, STDs, etc., etc.
  • Agriculture Molecular biology
  • Archaeology Botany
  • Medicine Cell biology
  • Forensics

Prot 401 — Bacterial Agar Plates with Ampicillin
Word21.4 KB· Yeh MD, Albert C· edited 2022-12-29
▼

Pouring LB-agar plates from powder (Sigma L3147): 20 g in 0.5 L, boil to dissolve, autoclave 15 min at 121°C, then add antibiotic.

The protocol, step by step

Creating Agar Plates from Powder

Sigma Millipore – LB Broth with agar – L3147-1kg ($293.00)

https://www.sigmaaldrich.com/US/en/product/sigma/l3147

1. Suspend 20 g in 0.5L of distilled water. (40g/1L)
2. Heat to boiling while stirring to dissolve.
3. Autoclave for 15 minutes at 121°C.
4. Cool to 50°C prior to adding final concentration of 50ug/mL ampicillin, dispensing into sterile petri dishes.

5. Each dish can put ~25mL liquid

Prot 402 — Sterile Lysates / Bacterial Growth Curves
Word37.1 KB· Yeh MD, Albert C· edited 2026-01-01
▼

Bacterial growth curves by OD₆₀₀ on the NanoDrop One C, feeding into sterile lysate preparation. This is the current version (revision 23).

⬇ Download Word · 37.1 KB

Source & citation

Document author metadata: Saranya Rani Chakka (Markey Lab); edited by Albert Yeh.
  • “Make sure grow the culture in appropriate volume based on OD intervals” — stated in the document

Document record

Document author: Chakka, Saranya Rani
Last edited by: Yeh MD, Albert C
Created: 2022-07-25
Last modified: 2026-01-01
Revisions: 23

The protocol, step by step

Bacterial Growth Curves using a Spectrophotometer (Nanodrop one C)
  • Prepare sterile broth or media that will be used for inoculation. When ready to use, make sure the temperature is not too hot. The media container should be comfortable to the touch.
  • Power on a spectrophotometer and allow it to warm up, preferably for several minutes before use.
  • Make sure grow the culture in appropriate volume based on OD intervals
  • Set the wavelength of the spectrophotometer to 600 nm (or another appropriate setting). Add 0.5 mL of uninoculated sterile media to a clean cuvette and blank the machine by setting it to 0 with this sample. This step standardizes the turbidity of the media without any cells in it, so that further calculations can quantitate growth due to changes in the turbidity.
  • Inoculate the primary media from which samples will be taken.
  • Immediately after inoculating, take a 0.5 mL sample of the inoculated media and pipette it into a clean cuvette. Place it in the blanked spectrophotometer, and record the OD reading. This reading should be recorded at time “0”.
  • Repeat the previous step at preferred intervals (based on bacterial strain) until the absorbance no longer increases.
  • Plot the readings on a graph with Time as the X-axis and OD as the Y-axis.
Preparing Sterile Lysates by Sonication (Fisher scientific, sonic dismembrator-Model 100, Immune Monitoring Team lab, 5th floor)1 2
  • Once the bacterial culture reaches log phase, pellet the bacterial culture at 5000rpm for 5min, resuspend in PBS.
  • Turn on the machine and set it on remote mode.
  • Prepare 70% ethanol and milliQ water in 50ml falcon tubes to dip and wipe the probe before and after use(can also turn the machine on for 2sec to clean any debris present on the probe
  • Set the power to 10
  • Keep the bacterial culture on ice (to prevent heat during sonication) and immerse the clean probe and press the foot switch on the probe as necessary
  • Repeat it every second with few seconds for 10 times pause to prevent over heating of bacterial culture
  • Bacterial culture will be disrupted now, leave it on ice 5min, centrifuge it for 7500g for 5min, sterilize the supernatant by using a 0.2 µm syringe filter, take 5uL and culture it and check for growth. There shouldn’t be any growth.
  • Determine protein concentration by Bradford assay or qubit and store lysates in aliquots at -80°C until use.
Preparing Sterile Lysates by Thermal lysis (freeze-thaw method) 3
  • NOTE THAT SONICATION IS MUCH BETTER! (I tried thermal lysis vs. thermal lysis followed by sonication – the latter is far superior, get yields 10-100x more)
  • Once the bacterial culture reaches log phase, pellet the bacterial culture at 5000g for 5min.
  • Freeze the pelleted bacterial culture by placing in dry-ice bath for 2min. The sample then thawed by placing in water bath for 8min. Repeat this step 5 times.
  • Resuspend the pellet in PBS, spin down cell wall debris 7500g x 5 min and obtain supernatant. filter sterilize using 0.2 µm filter take 5uL and culture it and check for growth. There shouldn’t be any growth.
  • Determine protein concentration by Bradford assay or store lysates in aliquots at -80°C until use.
Preparing lysate from stool samples
  • Take feces from stool samples and put in sterile centrifuge tube
  • Put ~500uL to 1mL fluid (PBS sterile) and mash with pipette tip
  • Use sonication protocol above
  • Spin down pellet debris @ ~5000G x 5 min, filter sterilize using 0.2 µm filter take 5uL and culture it and check for growth. There shouldn’t be any growth.
  • Determine protein concentration by Bradford assay or store lysates in aliquots at -80°C until use.
Protein Quantification by Bradford Assay (Prometheus cat: 18-442 [Genesee Scientific])
  • Kit provides Bradford Protein Assay Reagent and BSA standard.
  • Remove the Bradford Protein Assay Reagent from the refrigerator and gently mix it by inverting the bottle several times. Do not shake the bottle vigorously.
  • Remove the required volume of the dye reagent needed and equilibrate it to room temperature (RT) before use.
Preparation of Diluted BSA Protein Standards Dilute the supplied 2mg/ml BSA standards
Tube No. Buffer/Diluent Volume (μl) BSA Standard (2mg/ml) Volume (μl) Final Protein Concentration
Blank8000
10802000 ug/ml
220601500 ug/ml
340401000 ug/ml
45030750 ug/ml
56020500 ug/ml
67010250 ug/ml
7755125 ug/ml
879125 ug/ml
9796410 ug/ml
1079825 ug/ml
1179912.5 ug/ml
*Note can just pick an interval desired (nanodrop defaults to 7 intervals)

Standard Test Tube Protocol (Working Range: 100-1500μg/ml)

  • Pipette 50 μl of each standard or unknown sample into appropriately labeled tubes.
  • Add 1.0 ml of the Bradford Protein Assay Reagent to each tube and mix it well.
  • Incubate the tubes for 10 minutes at room temperature (RT).
  • Set the spectrophotometer at 595nm and zero it, using a cuvette filled only with water and measure the absorbance of all the tubes. Note can also just use nanodrop (2uL).
  • Subtract the average 595nm measurement for the Blank replicates from the 595nm measurements of all other individual standard and unknown sample replicates.
  • Prepare a standard curve by plotting the average Blank-corrected 595nm measurement for each BSA standard Vs its concentration in μg/ml. Use the standard curve to determine the protein concentration of unknown samples.
Pooling bacterial cultures together:

Once the protein concentration is quantified, resuspend all the strains of same concentration in respective volume so that the concentration will be uniform, mix them together and store it in -80.

References:

1. Suarez, N., Ferrara, F., Rial, A., Dee, V. & Chabalgoity, J.A. Bacterial Lysates as Immunotherapies for Respiratory Infections: Methods of Preparation. Front Bioeng Biotechnol 8, 545 (2020).

2. Varian, B.J., et al. Microbial lysate upregulates host oxytocin. Brain Behav Immun 61, 36-49 (2017).

3. Johnson, B.H. & Hecht, M.H. Recombinant proteins can be isolated from E. coli cells by repeated cycles of freezing and thawing. Biotechnology (N Y) 12, 1357-1360 (1994).

Prot 403 — CD4 Antigen Detection Assay
Word24.2 KB· Yeh MD, Albert C· edited 2024-04-04
▼

Live-cell assay to detect antigen-specific CD4+ T cells using anti-CD154 (PE), anti-CD137 (APC) and anti-CD107a (FITC).

⬇ Download Word · 24.2 KB

Source & citation

Yeh Lab. Based on Chattopadhyay PK et al., Nature Protocols 2006;1(1).
  • “Based off of Chattopadhyay PK, et al, Nature Protocols 2006;1(1)” — stated in the document

Document author: Yeh MD, Albert C
Last edited by: Yeh MD, Albert C
Created: 2023-05-02
Last modified: 2024-04-04
Revisions: 8

The protocol, step by step

Live-cell assay to detect antigen-specific CD4+

Based off of Chattopadhyay PK, et al, Nature Protocols 2006;1(1)
Reagents:

Anti-CD154 (PE) - (Clone TRAP1) (BD #555700)

Anti-CD137 (APC) - (Clone 4B4-1) (BD #550890)

Anti-CD107a (FITC) - (Clone H4A3) (BD #555800)

Monensin (note this assay does NOT use Brefeldin) (BD #554724)

Staphylococcus enterotoxin B (SEB), Toxin Technologies Inc (Staphylococcal Enterotox 1mg; 1/EA NC9442400)

Dilute SEB to 0.1mg/mL in PBS, store in 100uL aliquots.

Co-stimulatory antibodies (If not using DC co-culture):

Anti-CD28 (BD #555725)

Anti-CD49d (BD #555501)

If using DC coc-tulre

  1. Plate 100uL DCs at 10k/100uL
  2. Add lysate/petpide (8-12ug/mL), incubate for 2.5 hrs
  3. Plate 100uL T-cells at 100k/100uL
  4. Add titrated Anti-CD154 PE (0.3 to 20uL per 200uL, determine beforehand).
  5. For positive control, add 2uL SEB solution (SEB to 0.1mg/mL in PBS, store in 100uL aliquots)
  6. Add Monensin – default 2uM, can titrate.
  7. Stimulation: 6-24 hours (37C) – no appreciable loss of signal for CD154 when stimulated as long as 24 hrs.
  8. Staining - downstream


If NOT using DC co-culture

  1. Suspend T-cells in 96 well-plates at a concentration of 10M/mL (so about 2M / well if using 200uL of cell suspension for 96-well paltes).
  2. For peptide stim, add ~0.2ug peptide per well.
  3. Also for each well, add 0.2ug of anti-CD28 and anti-CD49d
  4. Add titrated Anti-CD154 PE (0.3 to 20uL per 200uL, determine beforehand).
  5. For positive control, add 2uL SEB solution.
  6. Monensin – default 2uM, can titrate.
  7. Add 2uL SEB solution – dilute SEB to 0.1mg/mL in PBS, store in 100uL aliquots.
Stim conditionExperimental medium (with monensin)
Peptide CostimPeptide or whole antigen
Anti-CD28
Anti-CD49d
Anti-CD154
Costim only (Neg control)Peptide diluent
Anti-CD28
Anti-CD49d
Anti-CD154
SEB (Pos control)SEB
Anti-CD154
  1. Stimulation: 6-24 hours (37C) – no appreciable loss of signal for CD154 when stimulated as long as 24 hrs.
  2. Staining - downstream

Prot 403b — CD4 Antigen Detection with DC Co-culture
Word23.5 KB· Yeh MD, Albert C· edited 2025-03-05
▼

CD154-based variant run with dendritic-cell co-culture. Note this version uses monensin, not brefeldin.

⬇ Download Word · 23.5 KB

Source & citation

Yeh Lab. Based on Chattopadhyay PK et al., Nature Protocols 2006;1(1).
  • “Based off of Chattopadhyay PK, et al, Nature Protocols 2006;1(1)” — stated in the document

Document record

Document author: Yeh MD, Albert C
Last edited by: Yeh MD, Albert C
Created: 2024-04-05
Last modified: 2025-03-05
Revisions: 6

The protocol, step by step

Live-cell assay to detect antigen-specific CD4+ with DC co-culture

Based off of Chattopadhyay PK, et al, Nature Protocols 2006;1(1)
Reagents:

Anti-CD154 (PE) - (Clone TRAP1) (BD #555700)

Monensin (BD #554724) (note this assay does NOT use Brefeldin)

LCL Media

Staphylococcus enterotoxin B (SEB), Toxin Technologies Inc (Staphylococcal Enterotox 1mg; 1/EA NC9442400). Dilute SEB to 0.1mg/mL in PBS, store in 100μL aliquots.

  1. In a 96-well, plate 100μL DCs at 10k/100μL per well using LCL media
  2. Add lysate/peptide (note final concentration will be 8-12μg/mL in 200μL; adjust concentration so that up to 20μL of peptide), incubate for 2-3 hrs
  3. After incubation, plate 100μL T-cells at 100k/100μL (10:1 ration of T:DC) per well using LCL media
  4. If using REPPED T-cells, baseline CD154 expression is elevated; will need DC co-culture for 24 hours to downregulate CD154 expression prior to proceeding to next step / adding CD154 Ab.
  5. In each experimental well, add:
    1. 10μL anti-CD154 PE
    2. 2μM Monensin (typical stock is 1:1000; may need to dilute stock to manage volume, can make master mix with CD154 antibody)
  6. For positive control, add 2μL SEB solution (SEB at 0.1mg/mL in PBS, stored in 100μL aliquots).
  7. DC Co-culture: 8-24 hours at (37C) – no significant loss of signal for CD154 when stimulated as long as 24 hrs. Most of my experiments have been overnight co-cultures for ~18 hours.
  8. After this incubation, can proceed with downstream analysis
