Prot 404 — Topical Application of S. epidermidis
Word25.5 KB· Yeh MD, Albert C· edited 2023-05-05
▼

Skin colonisation model from glycerol stock through tryptic soy broth culture to application. Budget 30–45 min per cage of 5 mice.

⬇ Download Word · 25.5 KB

Source & citation

In-document header: “Taken from Y. Belkaid lab” (NIAID).
  • “Taken from Y.Belkaid lab” — stated in the document

Document record

Document author: Yeh MD, Albert C
Last edited by: Yeh MD, Albert C
Created: 2023-05-05
Last modified: 2023-05-05
Revisions: 8

The protocol, step by step

Taken from Y.Belkaid lab

Materials:
Staph epi culture -80C glycerol stock, Box 1C3

Budget ~30-45 minutes per cage of 5 mice! Get help to speed things up!

  1. Take glycerol stock aliquot (keep frozen on dry ice)
  2. Use 5ml of tryptic soy broth per mouse (5/gp + 1 extra/gp) x 3groups
  3. Grow 18hrs at 37C; label all items with the date and our contact details)
  4. Spec. culture: an O.D. (600nm) = 0.8 is required. Dilute in tryptic soy broth, if required
  5. Make 5ml aliquots (in 10mL tubes, labeled with group/mouse number) x number of mice to use for rubbing
Application:
  1. New gloves are to be used before and after handling S.epi associated mice. Preferably double glove.
  2. Each mouse in groups with + S. epi is associated by placing 5 ml of S. epidermidis-conditioned medium across the entire surface using sterile cotton swab every other day (day -8, -6, -4, -2).
    1. Need to rub swab against the grain of the hair to get skin wet, otherwise drops will run off.
    2. Can use 1mL syringe to apply more liquid after initial rubbing to speed up process.
    3. Use heat lamp to dry mice. Will need to rotate mice twice to allow some time to dry before able to use up 5mL media (this is if using the syringe technique)
  3. Change stations and biosafety cabinets will be decontaminated before and after handling S.epi associated animal cages.
  4. Template below

Prot 405 — Growing E. faecalis and Lachnospiraceae

Anaerobic culture at 37°C: fastidious anaerobe agar with 7% sheep blood for colony picking; BHI broth for enterococcus, Schaedler anaerobe broth for Lachno. Media must sit in the anaerobe chamber ≥2 days to de-oxygenate.

The protocol, step by step

Taken from Markey Lab (Saranya Chakka)

I streaked the initial glycerol stock in fastidious anaerobe agar with 7% defibrinated sheep blood (neogen #NCM0014a) and picked individal colonies.

For enterocoocus I used BHI broth (RPI#B11000-500.0) and for lachno I used Schaedler anaerobe broth (Oxoid#CM0497).

I used anerobic conditions at 37 degrees to grow both bugs in our anaerobe chamber. I keep the media in our anaerobe chamber at least two days ahead of time to remove any oxygen present in it before growing the bacteria.

Prot 406a — Phospho-Western Blot, Bacterial Lysate

The protocol, step by step

Phopho- Western Blot Protocol – Bacterial Lysate

Reagents:

Laemmli SDS sample buffer (reducing 6X) – ThermoFisher Cat #J61337 - $138.49 for 25mL

Immun-Blot BVDF/Filter Paper Sets – BioRad Cat #162061 - $334 for 20

Trans-Blot Turbo 5x Transfer Buffer – BioRad Cat #10026938 - $132 for 1L

EveryBlot Blocking Buffer – BioRad Cat #12010020 - $136.80 for 500mL

Clarity Max Western ECL Substrate – BioRad #1705062 - $368 for 100mL

BLUelf Prestained Protein Ladder (5 to 245 kDa) – Bio-Helix Cat #PMB13-0500 – $224.62 for 500uL

Antibodies:

  • For positive control Ab (E.Coli), we use RNA polymerase beta (MA1-25425, 1mg/mL, mouse IgG2b Ab, 1:1000, Invitrogen, ~150kDa).
  • Other antibodies tried: Maltose Binding Protein Ab (MA5-27544, 1mg/mL, mouse IgG2b Ab, 1:1000, Invitrogen, ~42kDa)
  • Secondary Ab: HRP-Goat anti-mouse IgG2b (M32407, Goat Ab, 1:10,000)

Prepare Bacaterial lysate

  • Please see “[Prot 402] Sterile Lysates protocol” for prepping bacterial lysate. Note that we do not need to filter in this case for sterility and in fact don’t want to filter as that may lose proteins!
  • For reference, prior lysates (from 50mL bacteria in growth phase) have been retrieved at concentrations ~200-450 ug/mL.

Normalize lysate to total protein

  • Perform BSA assay according to manufacture protocol
  • Normalize all samples to ~2ug/uL final concentration after reducing buffer is added (or lowest concentration sample)
  • Add 6X Reducing buffer
  • Heat samples a 70 degrees for 10 minutes (or 95 deg for 5 minutes)
  • Centrifuge to collect evaporants

*Pause: Samples can be stored at -80 at this point*

Western Blot

  • If samples are frozen, thaw on ice
  • Write out plan for loading gel, 15-well or 12-well are available **avoid a symmetric gel, put 1 ladder on one side 2 ladders on the other**
  • Set up gel tank if odd number of gels being run put in blank
  • Prepare gels, remove green tape from the bottom of the gel, pull comb straight out to avoid bending wells
    • Hold the gel upside down and inspect wells, if the walls have collapsed or are bent, use a P20 pipette tip to fix wells
  • Clamp gel into holders, fill between the gels with SDS running buffer (10X stock dilute in diH2O), use P1000 to pipette running buffer into wells to get rid of bubble
  • Fill tank with SDS running buffer to the 2-gel line
  • Pipette 10uL of ladder or sample into each well (max 12uL sample into well of 15well gel or 20uL sample into well of 12well gel)
  • If 3+ gels are being run, fill tank to 4 gel line
  • Run 200V for 30 min
  • When ~10 min is left soak membrane in Methanol, and 2 stacks per gel of transfer stacks in transfer buffer – (in 4 degree fridge, stock solution in bottles in fridge)
  • After 30 min stop power source
  • Remove tray from transfer machine
  • Place stack of transfer stacks to drawer (metal box), flatten out any bumps with roller
  • Use forceps to remove membrane from methanol, dip in transfer buffer, and then place on prepared stack – roll to remove bumps/bubbles
  • Open gel with tool at indicated cites, cut off wells and along the bottom of the gel
  • Lay gel on the membrane, again flatten gel to remove bubbles
  • Lay second stack on top of gel, roll
  • Press lid onto drawer and lock – transfer drawer to machine
  • Run “Turbo” transfer program, 1 mini gel (should be a 7 min program); Mixed MV 25A, 25V 7 min
  • After program runs remove membrane, transfer to Western box and pour Blocking Buffer on to membrane, enough to cover
  • Incubate, shaking for 5 min
  • Store membrane in 1X TBS in 4 degree or proceed to blotting
  • Incubate membrane with primary antibody diluted in blocking buffer (I start Cell Signaling Abs at 1:1000 then adjust as needed) – usually 10 ml antibody solution, less if can fix in small box
    • Incubate overnight, shaking in cold room
  • Wash membrane 3X 5 min in TBS-Tween (1X TBS +0.1% Tween)
  • Incubate in secondary Ab, Mouse or Rabbit-HRP, according to species of primary Ab
    • 1:10,000 dilution in blocking buffer
    • Incubate 1 hr RT shaking
  • Wash membrane 3X 5 in in TBS-Tween
  • Incubate membrane in ECL reagent (1:1 of each reagent in box) 5 min shaking RT
  • Image membrane “Chemi Blot” setting of gel imager
  • After imaging wash 1X 5min in TBST
  • If probing with additional antibodies strip membrane
    • 10 mL stripping buffer, incubate shaking RT for 7 min
  • Wash 2X 5 min TBST
  • Block, blocking buffer 5 min
  • Store membrane in TBS, 4 degrees or process with re-probing, same procedure as 1st Ab

The protocol, step by step

Phopho- Western Blot Protocol – Primary T cells

Prepare NP40 RIPA Lysis Buffer

Stock Concentration 10mL

25 mM TRIS pH8 1M 250uL

150mM NaCl 3M 300uL

1% NP40 20% 500uL

5 mM EDTA .5 M 100uL

Thermo Protease/Phosphatase inhibitor 10X 100uL/1 tablet

0.1% SDS 1% 100uL

Water 8.650 mL

Regents/materials

  • Ice-cold NP40-RIPA lysis buffer
  • Ice-cold PBS
  • RT centrifuge, ideally close to water bath
  • 37 degree water bath
  • 4 degree centrifuge (ideally close to vacuum for aspiration)

Prepare T cells

  • Spin down T cells, aim for a minimum of 2x106 cells per condition
  • Resuspend at 2x106 cells/50ul (2M cell/50uL per timepoint) in eppendorph tube
  • Transfer 50ul/2M cells to an eppendorph tube for each timepoint
  • Warm cells at 37 degrees for 5 minutes before activations

*Nalm6 stim (1:1 E:T)

  • Prepare Nalm6 similarly to T cells 2M cells/timepoint resuspended in 50uL/timepont in 1 eppendorph tube
  • Prepare antigen negative nalm6 in same way

Stimulate T cells

  • Place tubes with T cells in RT centrifuge with lids open
  • Carefully pipet 50uL of Nalm6 cells to side of eppendorph
  • Spin down tubes to bring Nalm6 in contact with T cell 800g 30sec
  • Quickly transfer tubes to water bath, incubate for length of time point
  • At end of time point add 1mL ice-cold PBS
  • Immediately centrifuge tube 800g 1min
  • Aspirate PBS/media, resuspend in 50-75uL ice-cold NP40 RIPA lysis buffer
  • After all time point collected sonicate lysate (2X 40% amplitude, transfer back to ice between sonication – I sonicate all samples 1 time, then start next round of sonication to cool between)
  • Centrifuge lysate max speed 15 min at 4 degrees
  • Transfer supernatant to clean eppendorph – there will be a small white pellet, avoid transferring this!

*Pause point: lysate can be stored in -80 at this point*

Normalize lysate to total protein

  • Micro-BSA kit 1:150 dilutiono of lysate in 10% NP40 lysis buffer (dilute in water)
  • Perform BSA assay according to manufatuce protocol
  • Normalize all samples to ~2ug/uL final concentration after reducing buffer is added(or lowest concentration sample)
  • Add 6X Reducing buffer
  • Heat samples a 70 degrees for 10 minutes

*Pause: Samples can be stored at -80 at this point*

Western Blot

  • If samples are frozen, thaw on ice
  • Write out plan for loading gel, 15-well or 12-well are available **avoid a symmetric gel, put 1 ladder on one side 2 ladders on the other**
  • Set up gel tank if odd number of gels being run put in blank
  • Prepare gels, remove green tape from the bottom of the gel, pull comb straight out to avoid bending wells
    • Hold the gel upside down and inspect wells, if the walls have collapsed or are bent, use a P20 pipette tip to fix wells
  • Clamp gel into holders, fill between the gels with SDS running buffer (10X stock dilute in diH2O), use P1000 to pipette running buffer into wells to get rid of bubble
  • Fill tank with SDS running buffer to the 2-gel line
  • Pipette 10uL of ladder or sample into each well (max 12uL sample into well of 15well gel)
  • If 3+ gels are being run, fill tank to 4 gel line
  • Run 200V for 30 min
  • When ~10 min is left soak membrane in Methanol, and 2 stacks per gel of transfer stacks in transfer buffer – (in 4 degree fridge, stock solution in bottles in fridge)
  • After 30 min stop power source
  • Remove tray from transfer machine
  • Place stack of transfer stacks to drawer, flatten out any bumps with roller
  • Use forceps to remove membrane from methanol, dip in transfer buffer, and then place on prepared stack – roll to remove bumps/bubbles
  • Open gel with tool at indicated cites, cut off wells and along the bottom of the gel
  • Lay gel on the membrane, again flatten gel to remove bubbles
  • Lay second stack on top of gel, roll
  • Press lid onto drawer and lock – transfer drawer to machine
  • Run “Turbo” transfer program, 1 mini gel (should be a 7 min program)
  • After program runs remove membrane, transfer to Western box and pour Blocking Buffer on to membrane, enough to cover
  • Incubate, shaking for 5 min
  • Store membrane in 1X TBS in 4 degree or proceed to blotting
  • Incubate membrane with primary antibody diluted in blocking buffer (I start Cell Signaling Abs at 1:1000 then adjust as needed) – usually 10 ml antibody solution, less if can fix in small box
    • Incubate overnight, shaking in cold room
  • Wash membrane 3X 5 min in TBS-Tween (1X TBS +0.1% Tween)
  • Incubate in secondary Ab, Mouse or Rabbit-HRP, according to species of primary Ab
    • 1:10,000 dilution in blocking buffer
    • Incubate 1 hr RT shaking
  • Wash membrane 3X 5 in in TBS-Tween
  • Incubate membrane in ECL reagent (1:1 of each reagent in box) 5 min shaking RT
  • Image membrane “Chemi Blot” setting of gel imager
  • After imaging wash 1X 5min in TBST
  • If probing with additional antibodies strip membrane
    • 10 mL stripping buffer, incubate shaking RT for 7 min
  • Wash 2X 5 min TBST
  • Block, blocking buffer 5 min
  • Store membrane in TBS, 4 degrees or process with re-probing, same procedure as 1st Ab

Prot 407 — Embedding Bacterial Pellet in HistoGel

Embeds a fixed bacterial pellet in HistoGel so it can be sectioned; done in an Eppendorf rather than a PCR tube depending on the cross-section wanted.

The protocol, step by step

Embedding Bacteria with Histogel

Protocol adopted from https://www.statlab.com/pdfs/ifu/emhge.pdf

The main idea is to embed fixed bacterial pellet into histogel matrix so that they can be sectioned. We will do so in a small Eppendorf vs. PCR tube to get a nice tiny pellet!

Preparation

Histogel – HistoGel Vial 12/Cs ($338.76) https://www.fishersci.com/itemdetail?catnum=22110678

Cytology specimens should be ethanol-preserved cell suspensions, either with PCR or Eppendorf tubes, depending on how large a cross section is desired.

Steps

  1. Heat up HistoGel to 60C +/- 5 either by microwave on low for 5-15 seconds (loosen cap), or boiling water bath for 3-10 minutes.
    1. Can allow cooling to 50C +/- 5 to allow gel to solidify more quickly after dispensing.
  2. Centrifuge Tube Methods
    1. Add 4-6 drops of liquified HistoGel with a STERILE pipette to cell pellet at bottom of centrifuge tube (if using PCR tubes, I used 50uL and resuspended pellet in solution).
    2. Vortex specimen for a few seconds to mix cells and HistoGel together OR let HistoGel settle at bottom of tube.
    3. Allow HistoGel to solidify by cooling to near room temp < 20C. Can use ice packs if want to speed up process.
    4. Remove HistoGel pellet containing specimen and place inside a tissue cassette.
    5. Process HistoGel cell pellet as standard histology specimen.

Prot 408 — Bacterial Glycerol Stocks: Freezing and Revival
Word4.7 KB· Yeh MD, Albert C· edited 2026-08-21

How the gavage isolates are banked and brought back: exponential-phase culture frozen at 25% glycerol final (500 µL culture + 500 µL of 50% glycerol), and revived by scraping the still-frozen vial with a sterile tip or loop into ~1 mL of media. The stock is the seed, not the inoculum — gavage cultures are grown fresh each time from an ongoing culture. Growth conditions are in [Prot 405].

The protocol, step by step

[Prot 408] Bacterial Glycerol Stocks – Freezing and Revival

Written 8/21/26 by Albert Yeh. Taken from Markey Lab (Saranya Chakka), per e-mail 4/27/26.

Companion to [Prot 405] Growing E. faecalis and Lachno, which covers the media and anaerobic growth conditions referred to below.

Notes:
  • For the anaerobic gut isolates used for gavage — Enterococcus faecalis and Lachnospiraceae — grown as described in [Prot 405].
  • Stocks are frozen from exponential-phase culture at 25% glycerol final.
  • The glycerol stock is the seed, not the inoculum. Cultures for gavage are grown fresh every time from an ongoing culture, not thawed straight from the freezer.
  • As of 4/27/26 S. Chakka was testing freezing protocols for ready-to-gavage frozen cultures. That is not standardized yet — update this protocol when it is.
Materials:
  • Exponentially growing culture (BHI broth for E. faecalis; Schaedler anaerobe broth for Lachnospiraceae; 37°C, anaerobic chamber — see [Prot 405])
  • Sterile 50% (v/v) glycerol in water
  • Cryovials, labels
  • Sterile inoculation loop or pipette tip
  • –80°C freezer; dry ice for transport
Freezing — making the stock:
  1. Grow the culture to exponential phase under the media and anaerobic conditions for that organism ([Prot 405]).
  2. In a cryovial, combine 500 µL of the exponential culture with 500 µL of sterile 50% glycerol — 1 mL total, 25% glycerol final.
  3. Mix, and label with the organism, the date, and where the culture came from.
  4. Transfer to –80°C.
Revival — growing from the stock:
  1. Keep the vial frozen. Do not thaw the stock through.
  2. With a sterile pipette tip or inoculation loop, scrape a small amount of the frozen culture.
  3. Inoculate that into about 1 mL of the appropriate media and grow under the conditions for that organism ([Prot 405]).
  4. For gavages, grow fresh every time from this ongoing culture rather than returning to the freezer.
Not specified in the source — confirm with S. Chakka before relying on:
  • The OD600 or time at which “exponential” is called, per organism.
  • Whether the 50% glycerol is autoclaved or filter-sterilized, and whether it is pre-reduced in the anaerobe chamber before use.
  • Freezer and box location for the stocks.
  • How many passages an ongoing culture is carried before going back to a fresh vial.
Source:

E-mail, Saranya Rani Chakka to Albert Yeh, 27 April 2026, “Re: protocol for growing / storing bacterial stock”: “I freeze the bacterial culture in 25% glycerol stock. Exponential culture (500uL culture in 500uL of 50% glycerol). I use a sterile tip or inoculation loop and scrape little bit of frozen culture and inoculate in about 1mL of media when thawing first and then grow it fresh every time from ongoing culture for the gavages.”

Prot 501 — TCR Design (overview)

The umbrella document for the TCR pipeline: CRISPR KO of endogenous TCRs, lentiviral TCR transduction, codon optimisation and IDT gBlock ordering, molecular cloning into the lentiviral construct, virus prep and flow titration.

Document record

Document author: Cummings, Carrie L
Last edited by: Yeh MD, Albert C
Created: 2023-04-05

Revisions: 6

The protocol, step by step

- CRISPR KO of endogenous TCRs
- TCR transduction (using lentivirus)
- TCR design, gene art codon-optimization, ordering from IDT geneblocks
- molecular cloning (from IDT geneblock to lentiviral construct)
- virus prep (concentrated virus or viral supernatant)
- virus titration (by flow)
- sorting transduced T cells/REPing/flow checks

The protocol is attached. The brief outline is here:

  • d0: digestion, ligation (~7-8 hours, but tons of long incubations) 4/17 4/18
  • d1: transform bacteria (~ 40 minutes) 4/18 4/19
  • d2: colony PCR (~3 hours, but multiple 1 hour incubations) 4/19 4/20
  • d3: submit sequencing 4/20 4/21
  • d5 (variable, happens once sequencing is back): set up cultures for maxipreps weekend (I can do) weekend
  • d6: maxiprep 4/24 4/24
Went over with Jessica Lok on 4/18/23, my notes in Red

The pRRL.PPT_MP_CBF Bracelet-RQR8 backbone vector is from Bleakley lab. RQR8 is composed of CD34-CD20-CD34 tag so you can use either to tell the gene insert apart.

TCR Cloning Protocol:
  1. Resuspend gBlocks (1000 ng, lyophilized) in 20 µLs 1x TE Buffer. Note that this is a linear construct. Gene art gives you the vector you want in a plasmid (e.g. bacterial plasmid). gBlocks is more efficient but can’t amplify.
  2. Digest gBlock inserts (x2 tubes of each) as follows: (we use 2 tubes instead of combining as the reactions work better in the smaller volumes)

gBlock (300 ng) 6 µLs

10x CutSmart Buffer (NEB) 4 µLs

SacII Enzyme (NEB) 1 µL

RsrII Enzyme (NEB) 1 µL

MBG Water 28 µLs

40 µLs

Add enzymes last

Digest for 2 hours at 37°C.

Purify insert digests using Qiagen MinElute PCR Purification Kit. Only cutting out overhangs, so don’t need gel purification.

Elute final product with 20 µLs MBG water. Use water instead of buffer, may be better for downstream ligation – same idea as what Tamer does.

Quantitate concentration of inserts using Qubit 1x dsDNA HS Assay Kit.

  1. Digest pRRL.PPT_MP_CBF Bracelet-RQR8 backbone vector as follows (x3-4 tubes): “MP” is the promoter

Vector (2.5 µg) 2 µLs

10x CutSmart Buffer (NEB) 6 µLs

SacII Enzyme (NEB) 1 µL

RsrII Enzyme (NEB) 1 µL

BstEII-HF Enzyme (NEB) 1 µL Need this to cut out the original pRRL.PPT vector insert to distinguish it from the vector insert which does not have a BstEII cut site.

MBG Water 49 µLs

60 µLs

Digest for 3 hours at 37°C.

Add 1 µL of CIAP (20 U/µL) to each tube. CIAP = alk phos to help ligation step

Incubate at 50°C for 15 minutes.

Run digests in 1% TAE Agarose Gel for 1 hour (combine tubes into as few wells as possible). Run at 100A

Cut largest bands and purify using QIAquick Gel Extraction Kit (use as few columns as possible).

Elute each column with 30 µLs MBG water and combine if necessary. Use water instead of buffer, may be better for downstream ligation – same idea as what Tamer does.

Quantitate concentration of purified backbone vector using Qubit 1x dsDNA HS Assay Kit.

Can leave at 4C

  1. Set up ligations using the following formula (start with a 3:1 insert to vector ratio and 100-200 ng of vector). Also set up a negative control reaction (digested vector only, no insert):

(ratio)

ng of vector x kb of insert x insert = ng of insert to use

kb of vector vector

Example: 200 ng x 1.3 kb x 3 = 104 ng

7.5 kb 1

5x T4 DNA Ligase Buffer 4 µLs

Insert varies

Vector varies

T4 DNA Ligase (5 U/µL) 1 µL

MBG Water varies

20 µLs

Incubate overnight at 16°C.

  1. Transform ligations into One Shot Stbl3 Chemically Competent cells as follows:
    1. Thaw Stbl3 cells on ice. May also use DH5alpha or Turbos
    2. Add 5 µLs of each ligation reaction to one tube of competent cells.
    3. Incubate cells on ice for 30 minutes.
    4. Heat shock cells in a 42°C water bath for 45 seconds, then place on ice for 2 minutes.
    5. Add 200 µLs of room temperature SOC media (comes with cells) to each tube and incubate in a 37°C shaker (200-250 rpm) for 1 hour.
    6. Plate each tube of cells on LB/Carbenicillin plates (prewarmed to 37°C) and incubate overnight in a 37°C incubator. Carbenicillin lasts longer than amp plates
  2. Remove plates from incubator and check for colonies. Ligation plates should have more colonies than the negative control plate. If the ligations failed, repeat them using a different insert to vector ratio (5:1 or 10:1).
  3. Pick 4-6 colonies from each plate and inoculate 6 mLs of LB Broth + 100 µg/mL Ampicillin (for minipreps). For each colony picked, also streak a small amount of the bacteria onto a new LB/Carbenicillin plate.
  4. Incubate the liquid cultures overnight at 37 deg C with shaking at 200-250 rpm. Incubate the streak plate at 37 deg C overnight.
  5. Remove streak plate from the incubator, wrap in parafilm, and store at 4 deg C (for future maxipreps).
  6. Perform minipreps on liquid cultures and elute with 60 µLs MBG water.
  7. Set up a diagnostic digest on minipreps as follows:

Miniprep DNA 10 µLs

10x CutSmart Buffer (NEB) 2 µLs

SacII Enzyme (NEB) 0.5 µL

RsrII Enzyme (NEB) 0.5 µL

MBG Water 7 µLs

20 µLs

Digest for 1 hour at 37°C.

Run digests in 1% TAE Agarose Gel for 1 hour and check for proper band size (positive clones will

have a band at the same size as the original insert).

  1. Set up sequencing PCR reactions on 2-4 positive minipreps from each using common forward and reverse primers as well as several primers that sit inside your insert as follows:

Miniprep DNA 1 µL

Primer (10 µM) 0.5 µL

BigDye® Mix 2 µL

DMSO 0.5 µL

MBG Water 6 µLs

10 µLs

Common Forward Primer: pRRL.PPT_JG2_BB_8775_Seq_F

Common Reverse Primer: pRRL.PPT_RQR8_Seq_R These should be in the APE file – a bit of RQR8 is sequenced as part of insert

PCR Reaction:

  1. 96°C for 5 min.
  2. 96°C for 10 sec.
  3. 50°C for 5 sec.
  4. 60°C for 4 min.
  5. Repeat steps 2-4 for 25 total cycles
  6. Hold at 4°C

Take sequencing reaction to core sequencing facility for processing.

  1. Check sequencing results for proper insert by blasting against original gBlock sequence.
  2. Choose a positive clone for each and perform maxipreps (pick matching colonies from streak plate), then sequence the maxipreps as above to verify inserts again. Proceed with virus production.

Prot 502 — Concentrated TCR Lentivirus Stock Production
Word25.2 KB· Yeh MD, Albert C· edited 2023-05-09
▼

Lentivirus production and concentration using Lipofectamine 2000 / Opti-MEM in DMEM + FB Essence.

⬇ Download Word · 25.2 KB

Source & citation

Bleakley Lab. Document author metadata: Jessica A. Lok; edited by Albert Yeh.

Document record

Document author: Lok, Jessica A
Last edited by: Yeh MD, Albert C
Created: 2023-05-09
Last modified: 2023-05-09
Revisions: 4

The protocol, step by step

Concentrated TCR lentivirus stock production

Reagents Needed:

Gibco DMEM media (Thermo Fisher Cat #11965092)

FB Essence (VWR Cat #10803-034) – other types of FBS are acceptable to substitute

Lipofectamine 2000 (Thermo Fisher Cat #11668019)

Opti-MEM I (Thermo Fisher Cat #31985062)

PEG-it Virus Precipitation Solution (System Biosciences Cat # LV825A-1)

HEPES Buffer (Available from various suppliers)

  1. (day 0 - 5/9/23) Plate 4.5 million HEK293T cells (thaw 4 days in advance to give it time for a passage, wash 1x PBS and use 0.5% trypsin) in a 10 cm dish in 10 mLs DMEM + 10% FB Essence (FBE) - NO ANTIBIOTICS! (Jessica uses LCL 20 media – basically RPMI with 20% FCS) (Note, can add back antibiotics after d1 of transfection) Allow the cells to adhere to the plate by incubating them in a 37°C, 5% CO2 incubator overnight.
  2. (day 1 - 5/10/23) Co-transfect the next day with 8 µg of psPAX2 (gag pol) plasmid, 4 µg of pMD2.G (VsVg) plasmid, and 8 µg of lentiviral vector construct using Lipofectamine 2000 (or PEI-Transferrinfection Kit; thermofisher BMS1003-A, may work better):
    1. Mix 1.25 mLs of Opti-MEM I media with 50 µLs Lipofectamine 2000. Incubate at room temperature for 5 min.
    2. In a separate tube, mix 1.25 mLs of Opti-MEM I media with all plasmid DNA (20 µg total).
      1. Optional (only if directly comparing two constructs against each other): Add enough TE buffer to your plasmid so that the 16ug of DNA are in 50uL total volume.
      2. NB: it is highly suggested you use a construct that has been maxiprepped, rather than miniprepped as this gives better results
    3. Add diluted Lipofectamine 2000 to the DNA mix. Mix gently by flicking or inverting tube (do not vortex [don’t disrupt the lipid mycells]) and incubate at room temperature for 20-30 min.
    4. Gently add Lipofectamine/DNA mix to cells. Swirl plate to mix and return cells to the incubator.
  3. (day 2 - 5/11/23) 24 hours after transfection, remove the transfection media (discard) and gently add 10 mL of fresh DMEM + 10% FBE (can be with antibiotics, if desired). Gently pipet media onto side of plate so you do not lift off cells on bottom.
  4. (day 3 - 5/12/23) 48 hours after transfection (24 hrs post addition of new media), harvest the medium containing the lentiviral virions:
    1. At 48 hours, transfer media (~10 mLs) to a 50 mL conical tube.
    2. Bleach the remaining HEK293T cells and discard them.
  5. (day 3 - 5/12/23) Centrifuge the viral supernatant at 3000 x g for 15 minutes at room temperature to pellet cell debris.
  6. (day 3 - 5/12/23) Transfer viral supernatant to a sterile 50 mL conical tube by decanting liquid and discard cell debris pellet.
  7. Concentrate viral particles with PEG-it Virus Precipitation Solution:
    1. Add 1 volume of cold PEG-it Virus Precipitation Solution (4°C) to every 4 volumes of viral supernatant. (Example: 5 mLs PEG-it + 20 mLs viral supernatant).
    2. Refrigerate overnight at 4°C (at least 12 hours). Lentivector-containing supernatants mixed with PEG-it Virus Precipitation Solution are stable for up to 4-5 days at 4°C.
    3. Centrifuge supernatant/PEG-it mixture at 1500 × g for 30 minutes at 4°C. After centrifugation, the lentivector particles may appear as a beige or white pellet at the bottom of the vessel.
    4. Transfer supernatant to a fresh 50 mL conical tube by decanting. Leave tube containing concentrated viral pellet upside down on a paper towel to allow fluid to be removed. Centrifuge residual PEG-it solution again at 1500 × g for 5 minutes. Decant fluid and discard. Leave the second tube upside down on a paper towel to allow remaining fluid to be removed for several minutes. Blot tubes by gently tapping on the paper towel to remove remaining fluid.
    5. Resuspend/combine lentiviral pellets in 1/100 of original volume using cold (4°C) DMEM containing 25mM HEPES buffer.
  8. Aliquot into cryogenic vials and store at -80°C until ready for use.

Prot 503 — PC.008 Rapid Expansion Protocol (REP)
Word27.4 KB· Yeh MD, Albert C· edited 2023-06-27

The Bleakley Lab's controlled SOP for REP set-up to expand T cells; stated scope covers all resources, personnel and equipment in that lab.

⬇ Download Word · 27.4 KB

Bleakley Lab SOP PC.008. Document author metadata: Jenny H. Lill; edited by Albert Yeh.

Document author: Lill, Jenny H
Last edited by: Yeh MD, Albert C

Revisions: 2

The protocol, step by step

  1. Purpose

The purpose of PC.008 is to detail REP set up for expansion of T cells in the Bleakley Lab.

  1. Scope & Background

SOP PC,008 is intended to cover all resources, personnel and equipment in the Bleakley Lab.

  1. Materials
ItemManufacturerCatalog No.Quantity per T25
CTL mediaN/A-make25mL
OKT3 (1mg/mL stock)MiltenyiCustom Order
see: Bleakley Lab/Ordering and Inventory/Quotes Archive for previous orders		30ng/mL (0.75uL of stock per 25mL)
TM-LCL cell lineFrom CPF5e6 Cells
Feeder PBMC (A2-)CCEH donor program3e7 cells from thaw, 2.5e7 cells if fresh
IL-2 (50IU/uL stock)Miltenyi130-097-748
  1. Safety

Proper PPE and sterile technique should be worn and followed during REP set up and maintenance.

  1. Procedure

Setting up the REP (Day+0)

  1. Count TM- LCL. Remove appropriate number of cells and centrifuge at 1200 rpm for 10 minutes. Re-suspend cells in fresh CTL medium and leave on ice until the PBMC are ready to irradiate.
  2. While LCLs are centrifuging, thaw PBMC in warm serum-containing medium. Count cells and re-suspend in CTL medium (NOTE: The amount of medium used to re-suspend the three cell populations of LCL, PBMC and T cells, should NOT EXCEED the total volume of CTL medium needed for the entire REP.)
  3. While PBMC is centrifuging prepare T cell clone either from fresh cells (i.e. from cloning plate or REP) or from frozen cells as described in Thawing Cells Protocol.
  4. Once the appropriate numbers of PBMC and LCL have been prepared, irradiate them at 3500 rads for PBMC and 8000 rads for LCL (it’s best to irradiate the cells right before you are ready to set up the cultures because the PBMC become much stickier after irradiation).
  5. Once all cells are ready, a master mix can be made which contains the irradiated PBMC, irradiated LCL, and the OKT3. The mixture can then be brought up to the final volume required for all REPs (minus T cell volume). Generally, the volume of T cells needed per flask is minute; therefore, the master mix volume can be brought to 25 ml per T25 flask or 75 ml per T75 flask.
  6. Aliquot the above master mix into each flask and add in the appropriate number of T cells/flask.
Day+1 - Adding IL-2
  1. Add 50 U/ml of IL-2 (stocks are at 50 U/uL; therefore, add either 25 μl/T25 or 75 μl /T75.
Day+4 - Wash and Feed
  1. For T25 - Transfer the total contents of the flask (medium and cells) to a 50 ml conical tube.
    1. Centrifuge at 1200 rpm for 10 minutes and aspirate the medium. Make sure to use a new aspirating pipette for each tube. Add 25 ml of CTL medium containing 50 U/ml of IL-2, resuspend cell pellet and transfer back to original T25 flask.
  2. For a T75 - Without disturbing the cells, remove 25 ml of medium from flask and transfer the remainder of medium and cells to a 50 ml conical tube.
    1. Centrifuge at 1200 rpm for 10 minutes, aspirate medium and resuspend cell pellet in 10 ml of fresh CTL medium containing 50 U/ml IL-2. Transfer the cells and medium to the original flask. Add an additional 65 ml of medium containing 50 U/ml IL-2 to each flask.
Day+7 or +8
  1. Evaluate and Count the cells. If the cells are at a concentration of 1.5x106 or greater, they should be split 1:2.
  2. If the cells are at concentration of less than 1.5x106, perform half a medium change. Aspirate 12.5 ml/T25 or 37.5 ml/T75 from flasks. Replace the removed medium volume with fresh CTL medium containing 50 U/ml IL-2.
Day+10 or +11
  1. Evaluate and count the cells. If the cells are at a concentration of 1.5x106 or greater, they should be split 1:2.
  2. If the cells are at concentration of less than 1.5x106, perform half a medium change. Aspirate 12.5 ml/T25 or 37.5 ml/T75 from flasks using a new pipette for each flask. Replace the removed medium volume with fresh CTL medium containing 50 U/ml IL-2.
Day+12 through +14
  1. Cells should be tested for function by either chromium or proliferation assays, re-stimulated and /or frozen down (see CTL/PBMC cryopreservation protocol for details)

Prot 504 — Monocyte-derived Dendritic Cells

CD14+ selection and DC maturation from monocytes; PBMC/PBSC source can be graft or CD34-depleted G-CSF-mobilized cells. Benzonase replaces DNase in all PBMC media and buffers.

⬇ Download Word · 22.0 KB

Source & citation

Bleakley Lab (in-document initials TMC, MEB; author metadata RMC); edited by Albert Yeh.

Document record

Document author: RMC
Last edited by: Yeh MD, Albert C
Created: 2023-07-20
Last modified: 2024-04-08
Revisions: 13
Company: USCA

The protocol, step by step

CD14+ cell selection and DC maturation from monocytes

(TMC, MEB)

Day 0

Note: Add Benzonase nuclease (Millipore Sigma; note this replaces DNAse) to all media and buffers for PBMC leading up to cell selection. This helps keep the PBMC less sticky and helps prevent clogging of the AutoMACs. After cell selection, nuclease is no longer necessary and is not needed if you are using fresh PBMC.

PBMC/PBSC source: can be graft or CD34 depleted (G-CSF mobilized cells)

CD14+ monocyte selection

Thaw PBMC into LCL media + Benzonase (1:1000). Spin down cells, resuspend in LCL + Benzonase and count. Set aside 0.3-0.6 M unselected cells for flow.

Spin down cells.

Resuspend in MACs buffer + Benzonase, using 20 ul of buffer per 1x107 cells.

Add 20 ul CD14 microbeads (Miltenyi) per 80uL cells.

Mix gently and incubate 15 min. at 4˚C.

Wash by adding 10-15 ml MACs buffer + 1 into

Spin 10 min. at 300g rpm, 4˚C.

Suspend cells in 1 ml MACs buffer + Benzonase

Filter through a yellow pre-separation filter into a 14 ml conical.

Rinse tube and filter with an additional 1 ml MACs buffer + Benzonase

Separate CD14+ cells using “possel” (positive selection) program on AutoMacs Pro, or use a Miltenyi manual cell separation device. Getting excellent purity is not particularly important for this protocol. Column is changed very 2 weeks.

Dilute fractions in PBS as needed and count.

Expect approximately 5-10% yield of monocytes from total PBMC.

Set aside ~0.6 M CD14+ cells for flow cytometry.

Can use adherence as selection step too

Plate PBMC/PBSCs on tissue culture dish for 2-4 hours, remove floating cells, wash 1-2 times, before proceeding.

Checkpoint flow Day 0:

Suspend cells for flow (CD14+ and initial PBMC) in MACs buffer at 0.5-1 M/ml. Aliquot 0.1 ml/tube into sample tubes and control tubes.

Controls (Use PBMC for controls):

Negative Control (Use unselected cells; can also use negative sorted cells)

Antibodies (all 2uL in 100uL suspension):

CD80 – BV711 (T-cell Co-stim marker on mature DCs)

CD86 – APC (T-cell Co-stim marker on mature DCs)

CD83 – PE (Marker for mature DCs)

CD14 – FITC

Add antibodies. Incubate 15 min. at 4˚C, in the dark.

Wash with 3 ml/tube MACs buffer. Spin.

Pour off wash buffer and re-suspend cells in remaining buffer.

Run on flow cytometer.

Expected Results:

CD14 +
CD86<1%
CD80<1%
CD83<1%
CD14>80%
DC culture initiation

Suspend monocytes in AIMV medium (Gibco) + 800U/ml GM-CSF + 1000 U/ml IL-4 (R&D Systems) to a concentration of 1.5 x 106 monocytes/ml.

Plate monocytes ~5 ml/well in a 6 well plate. Wrap plate in foil and incubate at 37˚C for at least 48 h.

Day 2 DC culture: (>48h after plating monocytes-do not add maturation cytokines sooner than 48 hours or you will end up with a partially immature phenotype)

Stimulate dendritic cell maturation by addition of cytokines.

CytokineManufacturer[cytokine] in vial Desired [ ] in mediaul/ml addedVolume of media per wellVol cytokine per well
GMCSFR&D Systems800u/ul800u/ml1ul/ml0.5 mL0.5 ul
IL4R&D Systems714u/ul (25ng/ul)1000u/ml1.4ul/ml0.5 mL0.7 ul
PGE2MP Biomedicals1000ng/ul1000ng/ml1ul/ml5.5 mL5.5 ul
IL6R&D Systems1000u/ul1000u/ml1ul/ml5.5 mL5.5 ul
TNFalphaR&D Systems10ng/ul10ng/ml1ul/ml5.5 mL5.5 ul
IL1bR&D Systems5ng/ul2ng/ml0.4ul/ml5.5 mL2.2 ul

Prepare a master mix of cytokines in 0.5 ml/well AIMV medium, then add 0.5 ml of the cytokine cocktail to each well.

Continue incubation at 37˚C.

Optional: Can repeat flow as on Day 0, however, the expected result is cells in an intermediate and variable stage of differentiation. This intermediate stage correlates with a change in morphology visible under the microscope.

Day 4-5 DC culture: Flow analysis for moDC

Harvest DCs by pipetting up and down, then combining all wells in a 50 ml conical.

Spin down, suspend cells in 10 mL CTL medium. Count.

Prepare 0.2-0.4 M cells in 0.6 mL MACs buffer and repeat flow as on Day 2.

Expect ~90% CD80+, CD83+, CD86+ and <1% CD14+ If you don’t have a mature DC phenotype consider stopping the experiment and starting over.

Mature MoDC’s can be cryopreserved in aliquots for future use.

Notes: Benzonase nuclease is an engineered endonuclease from S. marcescens that degrades all forms of DNA/RNA and no proteolytic activity.

CD83: Marker for mature DCs; Function: stabilizes MHCII, costim molecules and CD28 by antagonizing MARCH-family E3 ligases.

CD80 (B7-1): Transient marker for activated B cell, macrophages, DCs. Ligand for CD28 and CTLA-4 (provides co-stim signal). Generally more potent ligand than CD86, but CD86 is potentially more important in T-cell activation than CD80.

CD86 (B7-2): Constitutively expressed on DCs, macrophages, B-cells, and other APCs. Ligand for CD28 and CTLA-4 (provides co-stim signal)

Prot 505 — Cloning by Limiting Dilution
Word45.5 KB
▼

Cloning minor-antigen-specific cells at 1 cell/well (plus higher-density plates), using irradiated patient or TM LCL feeders — with clinical-grade OKT3 at 30 ng/mL when no patient LCL is available.

⬇ Download Word · 45.5 KB

Source & citation

Riddell Lab. In-document header: “Adapted by Albert Yeh 04.12.24”. Refers to the GVL Protocol Notebook for the CTL medium recipe.
  • “Adapted by Albert Yeh 04.12.24” — stated in the document

Document record

No embedded metadata (legacy binary format).

The protocol, step by step

Cloning by Limiting Dilution for Minor Antigen-Specific Cells

Adapted by Albert Yeh 04.12.24

Reagents and Supplies:

CTL medium-prepared fresh (for recipe, see GVL Protocol Notebook)

IL-2 stock at 50 U/ml

LCL

Depending on the situation, either patient LCL or TM LCL will be used. Make sure there are at least three confluent T75 flasks of LCL at hand when cloning. When no patient LCL is available, use TM LCL plus 30 ng/ml of clinical-grade OKT3.

Normal Donor PBMC
T cell line

we generally clone at 1 cell/well and may include plates at higher concentrations

Sterile Nunc 96-well tissue culture plates

Multi-stepper-multi-channel pipettor and tips

Sterile reservoirs

Protocol:

** The cloning form found in the “Forms” notebook or on the PC in the “Forms” folder can be used for calculations and ensuring all the necessary components are added to the master mix.

  1. Harvest the LCL that will be used for cloning and centrifuge them at 1200 rpm for 10 minutes at 4o C. Remove the supernatant and resuspend the pellet with CTL medium and count. Put cells on ice until they will be irradiated.
  2. Calculate the number of 96 well plates you will be cloning based on how many LCL there are. 1x104 LCL are needed per well (approximately 1x106 LCL per 96 well plate). For example, 80 plates can be cloned from 80x106 LCL.
  3. Knowing the number of plates being cloned, the amount of normal donor PBMC can be calculated. (See sample master mix below). 7.5x104 PBMC are plated in each well (approximately 7.5x106 PBMC/ 96 well plate).
  4. Warm at 37o C enough serum-containing medium to thaw the PBMC. Quick-thaw the vials of normal donor PBMC at 37o C, pipette the cells into the pre-warmed medium, and centrifuge at 1200 rpm for 10 minutes at 4o C.
  5. While the PBMC is centrifuging, prepare the T cell line. If the line is in culture, count cells and take out appropriate amount of cells. This cell suspension will need to be diluted quite a bit before adding it to the mix (see serial dilution example at the end of this protocol). If the T cell line needs to be thawed, warm 9 ml of serum-containing medium at 37o C, quick-thaw the line, and centrifuge the cells at 1200 rpm for 10 minutes at 4o C. Resuspend the cell pellet with 10 ml of CTL medium and count the cells. Once again, remove the cells needed for the master mix, diluting the cells accordingly.
  6. After the PBMC have completed their centrifugation, remove the supernatant and resuspend the pellet(s) with 10 ml of CTL medium and place in a 50 ml conical tube. Bring volume up to 50 ml and take a sample to count. Place the tube on ice with the LCL.
  7. Prepare the 96-well plates by unwrapping them and stacking them in groups of five, making sure not to accidentally lift off any of the lids.
  8. Irradiate the PBMC for 3500 rads and the LCL for 8000 rads. Keep cells on ice at all times before making the master mix.
  9. Once everything else is ready, make the master mix with the irradiated PBMC, irradiated LCL, IL-2, T cells, and CTL medium. Because 200 µl of the master mix will be plated per well, 20 ml of mix volume will be needed per plate (if OKT3 and TM LCL are used, the concentration of the OKT3 should be at 30ng/ml final).
  10. Plate out the 200 µl per well of the master mix into the 96-well plates. Using the multi-stepper, multi-channel pipettor helps tremendously. Once all the plates are completed, wrap them in aluminum foil in stacks of 5 and place them in the incubator. Screen the plates only after d+10 according the micro CRA protocol.


Sample master mix for 100 plates with T cells cloned at 1 cell/well

Item Amount/plate Total amount required for 100 plates

  1. Irradiated PBMC 7.5x106 750x106
  2. Irradiated LCL 1x106 100x106
  3. T-cells (1 cell/well) 100 1X104
  4. IL-2 (50 u/ml final) 20µl (of 50 u/µl stock) 2 ml
  5. CTL medium (200 µl/well) 20 ml minus volume of 2 L minus volume

other components of other components

f. OKT3 (if not patient LCL; final 30ng/mL)

__________________________________________________________________________________

Final volume 20 ml 2L

Sample dilution series for T cells cloned at 1 cell/well

  1. Getting line counts and dilution series correct are the most important parts of the cloning experiment. Most lines will be at concentrations in the 105-106 T cells/ml range. Therefore, to obtain 1X104 T cells, only 10 µl would be needed from a line at 1X106 cells/ml. Because the chance for pipetting error increases as the volume being worked with becomes smaller, the goal of these serial dilutions is to remove a larger volume from the original line and dilute through a series of 1:10 dilutions to an appropriate concentration.
  2. In this example, we will plate 100 plates at 1 T cell/well. The total number of cells needed will be 1X104 (1 cell/well X 100wells/plate X 100 plates).

2.Remove 1 ml from tube #1 (1X105 cells) and add to tube #2 with 9 ml of CTL medium. Mix well.1. Remove 1 ml of line (1X106 cells) and add to tube #1 with 9 ml of CTL medium. Mix well. Tube #11X105 cells/mlTube #21X104 cells/ml3.Remove 1 ml from tube #2 (1X104 cells) and add to tube #3 with 9 ml of CTL medium. Mix well. Use all 10 ml of this dilution for cloning.Tube #31X103 cells/ml2.Remove 1 ml from tube #1 (1X105 cells) and add to tube #2 with 9 ml of CTL medium. Mix well.1. Remove 1 ml of line (1X106 cells) and add to tube #1 with 9 ml of CTL medium. Mix well. Tube #11X105 cells/mlTube #21X104 cells/ml3.Remove 1 ml from tube #2 (1X104 cells) and add to tube #3 with 9 ml of CTL medium. Mix well. Use all 10 ml of this dilution for cloning.Tube #31X103 cells/ml

Concentrated TCR Lentivirus Stock Production (6/9/21)

The protocol, step by step

Concentrated TCR lentivirus stock production

Reagents Needed:

Gibco DMEM media (Thermo Fisher Cat #11965092)

FB Essence (VWR Cat #10803-034) – other types of FBS are acceptable to substitute

Lipofectamine 2000 (Thermo Fisher Cat #11668019)

Opti-MEM I (Thermo Fisher Cat #31985062)

PEG-it Virus Precipitation Solution (System Biosciences Cat # LV825A-1)

HEPES Buffer (Available from various suppliers)

  1. Plate 4.5 million HEK293T cells in a 10 cm dish in 10 mLs DMEM + 10% FB Essence (FBE) - NO ANTIBIOTICS! Allow the cells to adhere to the plate by incubating them in a 37°C, 5% CO2 incubator overnight.
  2. Co-transfect the next day with 8 µg of psPAX2 (gag pol) plasmid, 4 µg of pMD2.G (VsVg) plasmid, and 8 µg of lentiviral vector construct using Lipofectamine 2000:
    1. Mix 1.25 mLs of Opti-MEM I media with 50 µLs Lipofectamine 2000. Incubate at room temperature for 5 min.
    2. In a separate tube, mix 1.25 mLs of Opti-MEM I media with plasmid DNA (28 µg total).
      1. Optional (only if directly comparing two constructs against each other): Add enough TE buffer to your plasmid so that the 16ug of DNA are in 50uL total volume.
      2. NB: it is highly suggested you use a construct that has been maxiprepped, rather than miniprepped as this gives better results
    3. Add diluted Lipofectamine 2000 to the DNA mix. Mix gently by flicking or inverting tube (do not vortex) and incubate at room temperature for 20-30 min.
    4. Gently add Lipofectamine/DNA mix to cells. Swirl plate to mix and return cells to the incubator.
  3. 24 hours after transfection, remove the transfection media (discard) and gently add 10 mL of fresh DMEM + 10% FBE (can be with antibiotics, if desired). Gently pipet media onto side of plate so you do not lift off cells on bottom.
  4. 48 hours after transfection (24 hrs post addition of new media), harvest the medium containing the lentiviral virions:
    1. At 48 hours, transfer media (~10 mLs) to a 50 mL conical tube.
    2. Bleach the remaining HEK293T cells and discard them.
  5. Centrifuge the viral supernatant at 3000 x g for 15 minutes at room temperature to pellet cell debris.
  6. Transfer viral supernatant to a sterile 50 mL conical tube by decanting liquid and discard cell debris pellet.
  7. Concentrate viral particles with PEG-it Virus Precipitation Solution:
    1. Add 1 volume of cold PEG-it Virus Precipitation Solution (4°C) to every 4 volumes of viral supernatant. (Example: 5 mLs PEG-it + 20 mLs viral supernatant).
    2. Refrigerate overnight at 4°C (at least 12 hours). Lentivector-containing supernatants mixed with PEG-it Virus Precipitation Solution are stable for up to 4-5 days at 4°C.
    3. Centrifuge supernatant/PEG-it mixture at 1500 × g for 30 minutes at 4°C. After centrifugation, the lentivector particles may appear as a beige or white pellet at the bottom of the vessel.
    4. Transfer supernatant to a fresh 50 mL conical tube by decanting. Leave tube containing concentrated viral pellet upside down on a paper towel to allow fluid to be removed. Centrifuge residual PEG-it solution again at 1500 × g for 5 minutes. Decant fluid and discard. Leave the second tube upside down on a paper towel to allow remaining fluid to be removed for several minutes. Blot tubes by gently tapping on the paper towel to remove remaining fluid.
    5. Resuspend/combine lentiviral pellets in 1/100 of original volume using cold (4°C) DMEM containing 25mM HEPES buffer.
  8. Aliquot into cryogenic vials and store at -80°C until ready for use.

IDT gBlock Assembly and Ordering

How to lay out and order the synthesised gBlock fragments from IDT.

The protocol, step by step

TCR alpha and beta chain variable regions: reconstruction

*See reconstruction powerpoint

After you have reconstructed your alpha and beta chains, plug them into your Ape template. Check that you can read the chains all the way through and that you are not missing an amino acid at the start of the constant region. Use the RsrII and SacII enzymes (in the enzyme selector) to figure out what region you will want to order.

GeneArt Codon Optimization

*Codon optimization keeps amino acid sequence the same, but changes nucleotides to ensure better protein production and expression of your gene

There are many companies that do this, but GeneArt is the one we’ve tested and had good results with

https://www.thermofisher.com/order/geneartgenes/projectmgmt

New Project > Provide name and select a folder > drag “gene synthesis” box into process #1 > double click the icon you just dragged > provide sequence name, select level 1, standard cloning vector > edit sequence tab, select your restriction enzymes > optimize sequence for homo sapiens only for the region after the Kozak > sequence summary, download as fasta

*All of the ordering info can also be found in more detail in the master TCR cloning document

IDT Geneblock Design

*Since this company sends the gene as a block, rather than within a construct, you need to create a place for the restriction enzymes to “land” where they will be cutting otherwise you will get poor results!

Any ~10 base pairs on either end of the construct is fine, but I’ve used the sequences below with great success, so I usually use them to avoid issues.

caggatcgac…insert sequence here…gtcaacgtaa

Ordering:

*Note that I have only ever used the HiFi and had great results. Non-HiFi is cheaper and arrives faster, but is untested

FMS > creat requisition > marketplace > IDT punchout > Products and Services > gBlocks & gBlocks HiFi Gene Fragments > gBlocks HiFi Gene Fragments in Tubes

  • Enter the item name and sequence
  • Validate
  • Follow instructions to order
  • Will take ~10 business days to arrive
  • Will arrive lyophilized and fine at room temp until cloning

The protocol, step by step

TCR cloning


Standard TCR cloning timeline

Times are for 1-3 TCRs and may need to be scaled up for larger batches of TCRs. These times do not account for troubleshooting at any step along the way.

  • TCR sequencing by Adaptive Biotechnologies
    • 2-4 weeks from submission to sequence for TCRB
    • 4-6 weeks from submission to sequence for TCRA/D*
  • In silico TCR reconstruction
    • <1 day
  • In silico TCR construct assembly
    • <1 day
  • Ordering TCR construct as GeneArt
    • <1 day to order
    • Up to 3 weeks for GeneArt to arrive
  • Restriction digest cloning of TCR construct into backbone vector
    • 1 week
  • TCR virus production
    • 1 week
  • TCR transduction, sort, and expansion of TCR-transduced T cells
    • 3 weeks
*TCRA/D sequencing is only run quarterly so advance planning is needed!

TCRA/B sequencing overview

There are two primary ways of sequencing TCR alpha and beta chains for TCR reconstruction.

Adaptive TCR sequencing uses next-generation sequencing technologies to identify the V, D (for beta chains), and J regions as well as to resolve the CDR3 region from genomic DNA.

RACE-PCR (Rapid Amplification of CDNA Ends) allows one to PCR out an entire TCR alpha or beta chain from cDNA (introns spliced out) without knowing the sequence of the V(D)J region.

AdaptiveRACE-PCR
ProsUser-friendly and few steps TCRB sequencing available through Fred Hutch core gDNA-based (more stable vs RNA) Minimal hands-on time (at most, gDNA isolation) Can be run on oligoclonal sampleResolves complete TCR chain sequence Can be run any time
ConsDoes not resolve complete TCR chain sequence (only ~80 bp around CDR3) Cost TCRA/D runs are only done quarterly and directly through AdaptiveCan be technically challenging Multiple steps (RNA extraction, cDNA, RACE-PCR, Topo cloning, sequencing) Cost Must be run on clone (>80-90%)

Post 2020, we generally prefer Adaptive sequencing up-front as it reliably provides TCR chain sequences and requires less hands-on time. run TCR assembly is also more streamlined. The protocol for Adaptive TCRA/D and TCRB sequencing is provided below in the main protocol.

RACE-PCR may be preferred if time is short and an Adaptive TCRA/D is far off, or to resolve uncertainty from Adaptive sequencing (standard PCR with gene-specific primers based on Adaptive sequence is an alternative). Protocols for RACE-PCR are provided in the Appendix.


TCRA/B sequencing by Adaptive

For TCR alpha sequencing:

  1. Contact FH Adaptive Rep well in advance of desired sequencing since TCRA/D sequencing is only run quarterly. The calendar of runs will be added to the Bleakley lab calendar and posted on the TCR cloning channel of the Bleakley Lab Teams once available. The Adaptive REP will provide detailed instructions for how to submit samples.
    1. NB: as of 6/2020, IML will do TCRB but not TCRA/D sequencing.
  2. Isolate genomic DNA using QIAGEN miniAMP kit, elute in TE buffer, and determine concentration as well as A260/A280 ratio.
  3. Submit samples to Adaptive in plate provided. Time to results is 4-6 weeks.

For TCR beta sequencing:

  1. Can be done through Fred Hutch Immune Monitoring Lab (IML). Request service in iLab. Contact IML (as of 09/2020, contact was Mason Lai) and submit either a cell pellet from your T cell clone or genomic DNA extracted from your T cell clone. Time to results is about 2-4 weeks.
  2. TCR beta sequencing can also be submitted directly to Adaptive, i.e., included along with samples for TCR alpha sequencing as above if the timing is better.


Assembling TCR consensus sequences

You can do this using either RACE-PCR sequence or Adaptive sequence. RACE gives longer nt sequence so can increase confidence. If possible have at least 2 overlapping sequences (fwd/rev, RACE and Adaptive). Note: A detailed example powerpoint is saved on Teams (Bleakley lab>TCR cloning>Files>SOPs and examples>TCR reconstruction.pptx) and on the server (Bleakley lab>Michelle>Protocols>TCR reconstruction.pptx)

  1. Search your beta or alpha chain sequence on IMGT (http://www.imgt.org/IMGT_vquest/vquest?livret=0&Option=humanTcR).
    1. Identify appropriate V, D (if applicable), and J regions.
    2. Save IMGT output as PDF.
    3. Note V and J regions used in spreadsheet.
  2. Search for V region sequence in IMGT Gene
    1. Select "Homo sapiens", TR, "Variable" or "Joining", and "functional" then click submit.
    2. Check appropriate boxes for V or J regions (Step 3) from list that is pulled up.
    3. Then below list of genes under "IMGT label extraction from IMGT/LIGM-DB reference sequences (F+ORF+all P)" under choose label select "L-Part1+V-exon"
    4. Copy and paste into excel spreadsheet. These will be your reference V sequences.
  3. Repeat Step 2 for J regions -- make sure you are not getting introns.
  4. Align original sequence (RACE-PCR and/or Adaptive) with V- and J-regions, as well as constant region (only if RACE-PCR was used)
    1. Open Sequencher
    2. Import .ab1 files for sequenced RACE products. If reverse sequence, under View, select "Reverse & Comp". Trim Ns.
    3. Import ApE files for any reference sequences (any file type)
    4. Create new sequences for V and J region nt sequences for Adaptive sequence.
    5. Highlight all sequences and click "Align automatically". You should not need to change from standard parameters or do anything fancy.
    6. Double click on the resulting contig and click "Bases" to view at NT level.
    7. If RACE product included, delete 5' sequence up to where it aligns with the start codon of the V-region.
  5. Under "View/Translation" select protein 1st frame to see in line aa sequence.
  6. Scroll through contig to look for discrepancies.
  7. Clone sequence (RACE or Adaptive) ALWAYS trumps reference V or J sequence. Confidence in CDR3 sequence increases if you have duplication (ie fwd and reverse or Adaptive and RACE). If sequence quality is poor, confidence decreases.
  8. Edit to correct Ns and so that CDR3 is correct based on RACE/Adaptive. You may need to trim or edit V and/or J—that is okay! Be sure you DO NOT alter your CDR3 sequence.
  9. Confirm that the amino acid sequence for your CDR3 is CORRECT and that everything is IN FRAME.
  10. Make sequence from consensus and export as FASTA file. This will be the basis for your alpha or beta variable +/- constant.
  11. Save project file as Date_Clone_alpha/beta_assembly.SPF
Building a TCR insert from consensus sequences

This subprotocol assumes that sequences have already been aligned in Sequencher and consensus sequences representing the entire V(D)J region have been saved as FASTA files.

  1. Open pRRL_PPT_MP-p-TCR-B-A_RQR8_TEMPLATE.ape (found in "Bleakley-Fusion Genes/TCR sequencing" folder).
  2. Open spreadsheet with sequencing data for TCRs, including the amino acid sequence for CDR3 regions.
  3. Find and open FASTA files (saved as .txt; can be opened in ApE) of TCR variable regions in "Bleakley-Fusion Genes/TCR sequencing/TCR contigs aligned from RACE and Adaptive" folder then open sub-folders for appropriate project and T cell clone.
    1. There should be at least one alpha chain sequence and one beta chain sequence for every T cell clone. These will be named [cloneID]_alpha1_consensus.txt for alpha chain #1, [cloneID]_alpha2_consensus.txt for alpha chain #2, etc.
  4. Copy the beta 1 variable consensus sequence for the T cell clone and paste it into the beta variable region in the template ApE file (first green section). Recolor the new beta variable region and call it [cloneID]_beta1_consensus by making it a new Feature.
  5. Then do the same thing for the alpha 1 variable consensus region, pasting into the alpha variable region of the template (second green region). Also recolor and label the new alpha variable region as [cloneID]_alpha1_consensus in Features as above.
  6. Save the file as pRRL_PPT_MP-p-[cloneID]-B1A1_RQR8_not-CO.ape, substituting the clone name/ID for the section in brackets.
  7. Translate your new TCR starting from the beginning of your beta variable region (should begin with ATG and follow ccgcgg). Check that everything is in frame and that there are no stops until around amino acid 760-800.
  8. Then do the following QC checks:
    1. Make sure your beta CDR3 is in frame (use sequence from Adaptive or from RACE-PCR). CDR3 should be near the end of the consensus sequence you pasted in. Make sure the amino acid sequence in your construct is identical to your CDR3.
    2. Make sure your alpha CDR3 is in frame (see instruction for beta CDR3).
    3. Make sure RQR8 is in frame (amino acid sequence below)
    4. Make sure there are no stop codons until the end of the RQR8 sequence.
  9. If any of your QC checks fail (ie your sequence is not correct or there is a premature stop), it is likely that you have a problem at the junction between either your beta variable and constant regions or your alpha variable and constant regions or both. To fix it:
    1. try adding a single nucleotide (a) at the junction (highlight in a new color and make lower case). Translate again. If everything is in frame, you have fixed* the problem.
    2. if a) doesn't work, delete the nucleotide you added. Then delete another nucleotide from the 3' end of the variable region. Translate again. If everything is in frame, you have fixed* the problem.
    3. If your beta and alpha junctions are off, you may need to do this twice.
    4. Once you have done a) or b) and have everything in frame where it should be, you need to check again that your sequences are going to translate correctly. Repeat all of the steps in #8.
      1. In addition, translate the consensus sequence of the variable region that you needed to modify (ie, if there was a problem at the alpha junction, translate your alpha variable consensus sequence). Check to make sure that the amino acid sequence of that region in your construct perfectly aligns.
      2. Finally, go to a reference sequence (JG2 TCR, AED24.1 TCR etc), translate it, and confirm that the amino acid sequence of the constant region you modified are identical to the reference construct.
  10. Repeat steps #8 and #9 until you are satisfied that the sequences are correct. Phone a friend (Melinda, Michelle) if you are unsure.
  11. Once you are confident in your sequence, select the region between the RsrII and SacII cut sites and order as GeneArt.

*make sure that any additions or deletions do not change your sequence in either the variable or constant regions.

Ordering the TCR construct(s) as GeneArt
  1. Log into the ThermoFisher GeneArt synthesis portal: https://www.thermofisher.com/account-center/signin-identifier.html
    1. NB: the link may be changed by the company in the future so if the above link doesn’t work, perform a web search for GeneArt.
    2. Can generate your own account or use a shared one (tmcunnin@fhcrc.org, Hutch8).
  2. Click the red button that says “New Project,” enter a name and select (or create) a folder.
  3. In the new project, drag the “Gene Synthesis” icon into the dashed square. Double click the icon. The box below (Enter Sequence tab) will appear. Type in a name for your sequence. Paste in nucleotide or amino acid sequence. Make sure under Biosafety Classification that “Level 1” is checked and select “Standard cloning vector.”
    1. NB: the default cloning vector can come with either ampicillin or kanamycin resistance. Specifying ampicillin is preferred as one does not find out resistance until the construct arrives and we do not always have kanamycin plates in house.
  4. Click “Edit Sequence” tab. In this tab, choose your open reading frame, which will begin at position 13 (after SacII and Kozak sequence) and end at the last nucleotide of your sequence (the last nucleotide of your RsrII restriction site). The stop codon is not included in your GeneArt construct as it is after RQR8 in final backbone plasmid.
  5. Click “Optimize Sequence” tab. Make sure SacII and RsrII are listed under “Protect restriction sites.” Under “Motifs to avoid,” select SpeI and AscI (in pRRL.PPT backbone) and make sure SacII and RsrII are also listed.
  6. Under “Optimization” (still on the “Optimize Sequence” tab), select “Homo sapiens” in drop-down menu, then click “Optimize.” This will generate a codon-optimized nucleotide sequence.
  7. Once codon optimization is complete, you will automatically be directed to the “Sequence Summary” tab. Review the translated sequence and restriction sites to ensure that everything is correct and in-frame.
  8. Once you are satisfied with your sequence, you can either generate more constructs in the same project or follow prompts to move your project and the sequences it contains into your shopping cart. At the last step, click the button to generate a quote and forward it to the appropriate technician who will be placing the order. Separately email the technician to request the order.
  9. GeneArt can take up to 3 weeks to arrive so plan accordingly.

Notes:

  1. The CBF vector can be found in the molecular cloning boxes at -20o C. Check with JAL if stock is running low.
  2. BstEII-HF is used only in the vector digest as it cuts the CBF insert in half, thereby keeping it out of the way when the gel is run. This is for ease of band extraction and to keep the vector pure.
  3. CIAP is a phosphatase which removes phosphate groups from the end of the digested vector and prevents the vector from closing back up prior to ligation.

  1. Digest for 3 hours at 37°C.
  2. Add 1 µL of CIAP (20 U/µL) to each tube.
  3. Incubate at 50°C for 15 minutes.
  4. Run digests in a 1% TAE Agarose Gel for 70 to 80 minutes at 100V (combine tubes into as few wells as possible).
  5. Cut largest bands and purify using QIAquick Gel Extraction Kit (use as few columns as possible).
  6. Elute each column with 30 µLs MBG water and combine if necessary.
  7. Quantitate concentration of purified backbone vector using Qubit™ 1x dsDNA HS Assay Kit.
  8. Set up ligations using the following formula (start with a 3:1 insert to vector ratio and 100-200 ng of vector). Also set up a negative control reaction (digested vector only, no insert):

Notes:

  1. If your concentrations are not high enough to get the desired amount of insert and vector into the reaction, expand the total volume to 30 µLs (bump up the buffer to 6 µLs and ligase to 1.5 µLs). If you still cannot fit it within the 30 µL volume, bring down the ng of vector until the math works out.
  2. We have both Invitrogen and NEB brand ligase. Make sure you match the brand of ligase buffer to the brand of the ligase.

  1. Mix the above table components together on ice.
  2. Incubate overnight at 16°C.
  3. Transform ligations into One Shot Stbl3 Chemically Competent Cells (E. coli) as follows:
    1. Thaw Stbl3 cells on ice.
    2. Add 5 µLs of each ligation reaction to one tube of competent cells and flick tubes gently to mix.
    3. Incubate cells on ice for 15-30 minutes.
    4. Heat shock cells in a 42°C water bath for 45 seconds, then place on ice for 2 minutes.
    5. Add 200 µLs of room temperature SOC media (comes with cells) to each tube and incubate in a 37°C shaker (200-250 rpm) for 1 hour.
    6. Plate each tube of cells on LB/Carbenicillin plates (prewarmed to 37°C) and incubate overnight in a 37°C incubator.
  4. Remove plates from incubator and check for colonies. Ligation plates should have more colonies than the negative control plate. If the ligations failed, repeat them using a different insert to vector ratio (5:1 or 10:1).
  5. Pick 4-6 colonies from each plate and grow overnight in 6 mLs Miller’s LB Broth + Ampicillin (100 µg/mL final conc.) at 37°C in a shaker (200-250 rpm). For each colony picked, also streak a small amount of the bacteria onto a new LB/Carbenicillin plate. Incubate the plate in a 37°C incubator overnight.
  6. Perform minipreps using QIAGEN kit. Elute minipreps with 55-60 µLs MBG water. Remove streak plate from the incubator, wrap in parafilm, and store at 4°C (for future maxipreps).
  7. Set up a diagnostic digest on minipreps as follows:
