  3. For “Full Service” sequencing reactions:
  4. Prepare a mix of 5 µLs of miniprep DNA (see table below for recommended amounts) and 3 µLs of primer (3 µM) for each sample using the same primers from A.1) above.
Plasmid Size:Concentration:
<6 kb25 – 150 ng/µL
6 to 10 kb150 – 275 ng/µL
>10 kb275 – 500 ng/µL
  1. Take sequencing reactions to core sequencing facility for processing (choose “Full Service” option in HutchBase).
  2. Check sequencing reactions for proper insert by blasting against original gBlock sequence.
  3. Choose a positive clone for each and perform maxipreps (pick matching colonies from streak plate), then sequence the maxipreps as above to verify inserts again. Proceed with virus production.


Concentrated TCR lentivirus stock production

NB: There are many ways to make concentrated lentivirus. We have been using this one (from Carrie Cummings) successfully, but another standardized protocol could be substituted. If you are doing a one-time experiment, you may wish to skip this step and go to “Production of fresh TCR virus” in Appendix to make a small amount of virus.

Reagents Needed:
Gibco DMEM mediaThermo FisherCat #11965092
FB Essence (or other FBS)VWRCat #10803-034
Lipofectamine 2000Thermo FisherCat #11668019
Opti-MEM IThermo FisherCat #31985062
PEG-it Virus Precipitation SolutionSystem BiosciencesCat # LV825A-1
HEPES BufferAvailable from various suppliers
  1. Plate 4.5 million HEK293T cells in a 10 cm dish in 10 mL DMEM + 10% FB Essence (FBE) - NO ANTIBIOTICS! Allow the cells to adhere to the plate by incubating them in a 37°C, 5% CO2 incubator overnight.
  2. Co-transfect the next day with 8 µg of psPAX2 (gag pol) plasmid, 4 µg of pMD2.G (VsVg) plasmid, and 8 µg of lentiviral vector construct using Lipofectamine 2000:
  3. Mix 1.25 mLs of Opti-MEM I media with 50 µLs Lipofectamine 2000. Incubate at room temperature for 5 min.
  4. In a separate tube, mix 1.25 mLs of Opti-MEM I media with plasmid DNA (20 µg total).
    1. Add enough TE buffer to your plasmid so that the 16ug of DNA are in 50uL total volume.
    2. NB: it is highly suggested you use a construct that has been maxiprepped, rather than miniprepped as this gives better results.
  5. Add diluted Lipofectamine 2000 to the DNA mix. Mix gently by flicking or inverting tube (do not vortex) and incubate at room temperature for 20-30 min.
  6. Gently add Lipofectamine/DNA mix to cells. Swirl plate to mix and return cells to the incubator.
  7. 24 hours after transfection, remove the transfection media (discard) and gently add 10 mLs of fresh DMEM + 10% FBE (can be with antibiotics, if desired). Gently pipet media onto side of plate so you do not lift off cells on bottom.
  8. 48 after transfection (24 hrs post addition of new media), harvest the medium containing the lentiviral virions:
  9. At 48 hours, transfer media (~10 mLs) to a 50 mL conical tube (store at 4°C overnight).
  10. Bleach the remaining HEK293T cells and discard them.
  11. Centrifuge the viral supernatant at 3000 x g for 15 minutes at room temperature to pellet cell debris.
  12. Transfer viral supernatant to a sterile 50 mL conical tube by decanting liquid and discard cell debris pellet.
  13. Concentrate viral particles with PEG-it Virus Precipitation Solution:
  14. Add 1 volume of cold PEG-it Virus Precipitation Solution (4°C) to every 4 volumes of viral supernatant. (Example: 5 mLs PEG-it + 20 mLs viral supernatant).
  15. Refrigerate overnight at 4°C (at least 12 hours). Lentivector-containing supernatants mixed with PEG-it Virus Precipitation Solution are stable for up to 4-5 days at 4°C.
  16. Centrifuge supernatant/PEG-it mixture at 1500 × g for 30 minutes at 4°C. After centrifugation, the lentivector particles may appear as a beige or white pellet at the bottom of the vessel.
  17. Transfer supernatant to a fresh 50 mL conical tube by decanting. Leave tube containing concentrated viral pellet upside down on a paper towel for several minutes to allow fluid to be removed. Centrifuge residual PEG-it solution again at 1500 × g for 5 minutes. Decant fluid and discard. Leave the second tube upside down on a paper towel to allow remaining fluid to be removed for several minutes. Blot tubes by gently tapping on the paper towel to remove remaining fluid.
  18. Resuspend/combine lentiviral pellets in 1/100 of original volume using cold (4°C) DMEM containing 25mM HEPES buffer.
  19. Aliquot into cryogenic vials and store at -80°C until ready for use.


Lentivirus titration

Day 1:

  1. Produce concentrated virus by usual method in previous section. Set aside an aliquot of ~35 uL when making other aliquots.
    1. You can titer the same day as you make virus, but make sure to freeze and then thaw your titer aliquot before using in the experiment below.
  2. Plate concentrated virus in 96 well V bottom plate.
    1. Add 90uL media to each of 8 wells
    2. Dilutions: 30 uL virus in the first well, mix, take from first well and then serial 4-fold serial dilutions (total 7 wells of virus). Leave 8th well as a no virus well.
  3. Transfer viral dilutions to a 96 well flat bottom plate.
    1. Transfer 30uL from each well using a multi-channel pipette. Can transfer multiple rows if replicates are desired.
  4. Resuspend Jurkat 76 cells in media with polybrene.
    1. For each well, we want 100,000 cells per well, 120 uL of cell suspension per well, and 0.6 uL polybrene.
    2. Here is an example of the math for the mastermix:
1x8x (1 row)
Cells100,000800,000
LCL media (to resuspend cells in)119.4uL955.2uL
Polybrene0.6uL4.8uL
  1. Plate 120 uL/well of cell mastermix (100,000 cells/well) on top of pre-plated diluted virus.
  2. Incubate at 37C, 5% CO2, etc. overnight.

Day 2-5:

  1. Add 50 uL LCL media daily x 3 days and split into other well if necessary.
  2. Evaluate transduction by flow at 72h or 4 days (e.g., for RQR8 tag or GFP expression).

Day 4 or 5: Tetramer Staining

Step 1: Tetramer stain

  1. Plate cells (5,000 – 1M/well) in 96 well v-bottom plate
  2. Spin down at 400g for 1-5min
  3. Carefully remove supernatant. You want cells as dry as possible!
  4. Spin tetramer at 12000g for 2min at 4o before using
  5. Add tetramer mix (depends on dilution)
    1. Tetramer: 2.5 uL
      MACS/PBS + 5% FBS: 22.5 uL
  6. Gently resuspend cells in mix by pipetting (25 uL mix per well)
  7. Incubate 15 min at RT in the dark

Step 2: Antibody stain

  1. Make antibody mix
    1. Use titrations for 50uL staining volume
      1. CD34 AF700 (for RQR8 staining): 5 uL
      2. CD3 FITC: 5uL
    2. Add MACS buffer to 25uL total, rather than 50uL
      1. 15 or 20uL
  2. Add 25 uL antibody mix to each well, pipette gently
  3. Incubate 10-15min in the dark, on ice (not at 4o)
  4. Wash in 150 uL cold MACS/PBS +5% FBS
  5. Spin at 400g for 1-5 min
  6. Remove wash
  7. Wash with 200 uL cold MACS/PBS +5% FBS, spin again
  8. Resuspend in 100+uL DAPI (1:12k)
  9. Transfer to tubes and run
    1. Keep on ice
  10. Flow analysis:
    1. RQR8 staining is the standard for virus titration evaluation (other stains are helpful to gain understanding, but will not be factored into the math)
    2. You are looking for condition with ~10% transduced (well above background but not a huge amount).
    3. ~10% transduction indicates 10,000 cells transduced so 10,000 infectious doses (=particles).
    4. TU/mL = (Number of cells transduced x Percent fluorescent x Dilution Factor) / (Transduction Volume in mL)
  11. Then infect T cells with MOI 2, 5, 10 to understand correlation between Jurkat 76 and primary T cells.
    1. MOI = multiplicity of infection
  12. Virus volume = (Number of cells transduced x MOI) / (TU/mL)


TCR transduction
T cell isolation and stimulation
  1. Make CTL media (10% HS) fresh for the experiment.
  2. Isolate T cells from healthy donor apheresis PBMC according to kit protocol using Miltenyi CD8 and/or CD4 isolation kit and AutoMACS depleteS protocol (can also use deplete for this purpose).
    1. Donor ID:
    2. Deviation from standard kit protocols?
    3. Starting number of PBMC:
    4. CD8+ T cell yield:
  3. Count and re-suspend at 1M/ml=______M in ______mL CTL media
  4. Add CD28 beads 1:1 in CTL + 50 U/mL IL-2: Calculate volume of beads: Stock is 1x10e8 /mL, = 10μL beads/1M cells.
  5. Plate 2 mL/well in a 24 well plate (two wells for each TCR plus at least 1 control well).
  6. Incubate overnight (18-24 hrs) at 37C. Start of incubation = ___
T cell transduction with concentrated virus
Day 1: Transduction (using concentrated virus. For fresh virus, see Appendix.)
  1. Remove 1mL of CTL media from each well.
  2. Calculate volume of CTL media needed (1 mL/well x # wells). Make calculated volume of CTL media with 50 units/mL IL-2 plus 2x of Polybrene = 8µg/mL (will result in 4ug/mL once added on top of old media that does not contain polybrene). Polybrene stock is at 1µg/μL
  3. For control wells only, add 1 mL CTL + polybrene + IL-2 to each well.
  4. Inoculate cells in sample wells:
    1. Calculate volume of virus needed based on MOI or on experience (e.g., using concentrated virus production method above, 25 μL is appropriate to start).
    2. Put vial in water bath for ~10mins, vigorously vortex to break up the virus clump, spin down briefly.
    3. Dilute virus in CTL + polybrene + IL-2 from step 2aiii. and mix well by pipetting.

(E.g., for 2 wells and using standard concentrated virus production without MOI, add 50 μL virus to 2 mL CTL + polybrene + IL-2)

  1. Add appropriate volume of CTL + virus to each well of T cells (1 mL + volume of virus).
  1. Re-suspend cells in wells to mix all reagents.
  2. Spinoculation: 2000rpm (RCF 845xg), 32C, 90 min.
    1. Spininoculation is optional if using concentrated virus produced by method described above.
    2. If you are spinoculating, make sure to ask other lab members about using the centrifuge for so long, and be sure to allow enough time for it to come to temp.
  3. Incubate the plate at 37C overnight. Incubation started at: _________
Day 2: Add Fresh Media
  1. Add media and cytokines to each well at exactly 24 hours:
    1. Make mastermix of CTL media + 50 U/ml IL-2 (1:1000 dilution from our 50U/μl stock solution)
    2. Add 1 mL CTL media + IL-2 to each well
  2. Return to incubator.
Day 3 & 4: Partial change media
  1. Remove 1.5 ml of used media from each well.
  2. Replace with fresh media and cytokines:
    1. Make mastermix of CTL media + 50 U/ml IL-2 (1:1000 dilution from our 50U/μl stock solution)
    2. Add 2 mL CTL media + IL-2 to each well
  3. Return to incubator.
Evaluation of transduction efficiency and flow sorting
Day 5: Flow evaluation for TCR expression
  1. Stain with peptide-MHC tetramer(s) and/or CD34 (QBend mAb) (if RQR8 tag included), CD8, +/-CD4, viability dye.
  2. Note % transduced (tetramer and/or RQR8 positive) to plan sorting: ___
Day 6 or 7: Sorting
  1. Harvest, pool, and count T cells from same donor transduced with same TCR.
    1. Count: _______
  2. Spin down and resuspend to max 5e6 cells per 100 μL and divide in 100 μL aliquots in sterile v-bottom 96 well plate.
  3. Stain as above only use 2x tetramer and 2x antibody and stain in 100 μL.
  4. Sort tetramer+ cells (any sorter is appropriate). If the epitope specificity of the TCR is unknown, can sort for specific Vbeta (Beckman Coulter BetaMark IOTest kit) and/or CD34 if an RQR8 tag was included in the TCR construct. Sorting on a direct marker of TCR expression (i.e., tetramer or Vbeta) as well as transgene marker (i.e., RQR8) is highly recommended as TCR and marker expression are not always 1:1.
  5. After sorting, check purity of your sorted samples as follows: Using a sterile dropper (can be found in the flow core supplies), take a small amount of each of your sorted cells from the collection tube. Transfer into a new flow tube. Add a few drops of PBS to bring up the volume if needed. Run through the sorter and record at least 5,000 events. Repeat for each of your sorted samples.
  6. Save sort data and export PDF from sort as well as recording data into FCS file.
  7. REP at least 50,000 cells (can count or approximate cell number by using # events collected x2).
    1. # events collected = _______
  8. Any remaining cells not put into REPs should go into CTL with IL-2 for use in functional assays. If >1e6 cells have been sorted, can also freeze down sorted cells directly for later REP.


Appendix
TCR cloning by RACE-PCR

This process can be used if Adaptive sequencing does not resolve your TCR down to the needed specificity.

RNA extraction
  1. Spray gloves with RNase away. Clean hood surface, racks, tip boxes, pipets, centrifuge etc. with RNase away.
  2. Thaw 5-10 M cells and wash in 1 x PBS
  3. Working quickly, follow instructions for RNaeasy Qiagen kit (summarized below, be sure to double check kit instructions).
  4. Add 600 μl Buffer RLT (with BME)
  5. Pipet up and down to mix and transfer lysate into QIAshredder spin column in a 2mL collection tube. Spin 2 mins at max speed.
  6. Add one volume 70% ethanol and mix by pipetting.
  7. Transfer 600 μl of lysate to an RNeasy spin column and spin 15 s. Discard flow through and repeat with remaining lysate.
  8. Add 700 μl buffer RW1 to column and spin 15 s. Discard flow through.
  9. Add 500 μl buffer RPE (with ethanol added) to column and spin 2 min. Discard flow through. Spin additional 1 min.
  10. Place column in clean eppendorf tube. Add 30 μl RNAse free water to membrane. Spin 1 min. Repeat with additional 30 μl water.
  11. Immediately transfer eluate to dry ice, then -80°C.


cDNA preparation

Note: You will likely need to order a new kit, as we do not use this protocol often and it is unlikely we will have a fresh kit. Order from Takara, catalog #634858 for 10 rxns or #634859 for 20 rxns. Kits have multiple components, some of which are stored at -20 and some at -80.

  1. Thaw RNA at R.T. Once thawed, keep on ice.
  2. Measure RNA concentration using nanodrop. An SOP for using the Nanodrop follows this protocol.
  3. Proceed with SMARTer RACE cDNA Amplification kit using 10ng – 1μg of RNA.
  4. Prepare sufficient Buffer Mix for all reactions, then set aside at room temperature until preparation of RT master mix (step 6):
Buffer mix1X (μL)X
5X 1st strand buffer4
DTT (20 mM)0.5
dNTP mix (10 mM)1
Total5.5
  1. Prepare RNA + primer in a separate tube to denature RNA
    1. 1-10 μl RNA (10-1000 ng)
    2. 1 μl 5’ CDS Primer A
    3. *For control reaction, use 1μl of Control Mouse Heart Total RNA (1μg/μl)
    4. Add sterile H2O to bring to a final volume of 11 μl.
    5. Mix and spin briefly
    6. Incubate tube(s) at 72°C for 3’ in heated-lid thermal cycler.
    7. Cool to 42°C for 2’ in heated-lid thermal cycler.
    8. Spin 10” to collect condensation.
    9. Add 1 μl of SMARTer IIA oligo to each tube of 5' RACE cDNA reactions
  2. Prepare RT master mix:
RT MM1X (μL)X
Buffer mix5.5
RNase inhibitor (40 U/μL)0.5
SMARTScribe RT (100 U)2
Total8
  1. Add RT master mix (8 μl from step 6) to denatured RNA (11 μl from step 5).
  2. Mix by pipetting gently. Spin 5 seconds.
  3. Incubate at 42°C for 90’ in incubator or heated-lid thermal cycler.
  4. Heat tubes at 70°C for 10 mins in heated-lid thermal cycler.
  5. Dilute the first-strand reaction product with 20 μl Tricine-EDTA buffer.
    1. Add 10 μL If you started with <200 ng total RNA
    2. Add 90 μL If you started with >200 ng total RNA

Nucleic acid quantification using Nanodrop Thermo Fisher 2000/2000c Spectrophotometer:

  1. Sign in on the computer attached to the Spectrophotometer.
  2. Wash the Spectrophotometer pedestal adding 2 μL of dwater to the lower pedestal and then lower the arm. Wait 30-60 seconds and lift the upper arm and use a wipe to vigorously scrub both the upper and lower pedestals.
  3. Click on the Nanodrop icon to open the Spectrophotometer software.
  4. Select the Nucleic acid bottom for DNA or RNA quantification.
  5. Blank the Spectrophotometer adding 2 μL of the buffer that you used to resuspend your sample (TE, EB, dH2O). Lower the upper arm of the NanoDrop and click the “Blank” bottom on the software. Wait ~ 20 seconds for the blank measurement to be made. When is done, lift the upper arm and dry the pedestal with a wipe.
  6. Measure your sample adding 2 μL of your sample to the lower pedestal, then lower the arm. Click the “measure” bottom on the software and wait ~ 20 seconds for the measurement. When is done, lift the upper arm and dry the pedestal.
  7. Clean the pedestal adding 2 μL of purified water to the lower pedestal ad then lower the arm. Wait 30-60 seconds and lift the upper arm and use a wipe to vigorously scrub both the upper and lower pedestals.
  8. Analyze results: purity ratios are important indicators of sample quality:
    1. 260/280 Ratio: A 260/280 ration of ~ 1.8 is generally accepted as “pure” DNA.
    2. 260/230 Ratio: The 260/230 values for a “pure” nucleic acid are often in the range of 1.8 – 2.2.

IMPORTANT: If the ratio is appreciably different than the accepted target described above, it may indicate the presence of protein, phenol or other contaminants that absorb strongly at or near 260 nm.

Topo cloning and sequencing RACE-PCR products
  1. Run the cDNA PCR products on a 1% gel.
  2. Cut out and purify the DNA using the Qiagen gel extraction and purification kit. Briefly:
    1. Weigh gel fragments in eppendorf tubes
    2. Add 3x volume of Buffer 1 (where 1x volume corresponds to the weight of the gel fragment).
    3. Dissolve gel piece by shaking at 42°C for 3-5 min.
    4. Add 1 x volume 2-propanol
    5. Load on column, spin briefly (Load & spin multiple times as needed to load all of sample). Discard flow-through.
    6. Wash column with 750 μL wash buffer. Discard flow through
    7. Transfer column to new eppendorf tube and elute DNA in 20 μL nuclease free H2O by adding water directly to the filter, spin 1 min. at 14000 rpm
  3. Clone PCR fragment into Topo vector (if desired).
  4. Set up Big Dye sequencing mix: Use ¼ reaction recipe from FH Genomics Core sequencing instructions:
RACE-PCR Seqencing1XX
Big Dye reaction mix2 μl
Primer**3-5 pmol (μl:____)
DNA template2-20 ng (μl:____)
DMSO0.5 μl
Water
Total10

**Seq primer: linker region primer from RACE cDNA kit)

  1. Submit samples for sequencing.


Riddell lab 5’/3’ SMARTer RACE protocol

Can use this as an alternative to Bleakley lab protocol if having problems. Main differences are:

  1. Enzymes (CloneAmp HiFi vs Phusion HiFi)
  2. TCR constant region primers
  3. Touchdown PCR rather than standard PCR is used in the RACE reaction (see below)

PCR Enzyme: CloneAmp HiFi PCR Premix (Clontech, cat# 639298)

RACE-PCR Primer List:

  • Human TCR Cbeta1 Reverse Primer (hTCR Cb1 R)

5’-CCACTTCCAGGGCTGCCTTCAGAAATC-3'

  • Human TCR Cbeta2 Reverse Primer (hTCR Cb2 R)

5’-TGGGATGGTTTTGGAGCTAGCCTCTGG-3’

  • Human TCR Calpha Reverse Primer (RACE_hTCR_Calpha R)

5’-CAGCCGCAGCGTCATGAGCAGATTA-3’

  • 10X Directional Universal Primer A Mix (dir UPM)

Long (0.4 µM)

5’-CACCTAATACGACTCACTATAGGGCAAGCAGTGGTATCAACGCAGAGT-3'

Short (2 µM)

5’-CACCTAATACGACTCACTATAGGGC-3’

Making 10 x UPM Primer Stock:

Mix 2 µl of Long primer stock (100 uM) + 10 µl of Short primer stock (100 uM) and then add 488 µl ddH2O to make 500 µl 10 x UPM stock.

PCR works best annealing at 60C and doing a touchdown by .2C for 35 cycles. Run PCR rxn on EtBr gel and expect 1kb bands. Gel Purify fragments for pENTR Directional TOPO cloning.

Determining TCR sequences from RACE-PCR products
  1. Open .ab1 files from sequencing.
  2. Copy the central portion of the sequence with minimal “N”s.
  3. Open IMGT vquest human TR nucleotide set, linked here: http://www.imgt.org/IMGT_vquest/input
  4. Paste the sequence into the search box on IMGT.
  5. Insert “>” and a title as the first line to make it FASTA format, then click “start” to see results. It should return an alpha or beta gene that matches your sequence.

We typically obtain our TCR constructs as GeneArt (synthetic DNA block) from Invitrogen/ThermoFisher. This allows for codon optimization and engineering in specific restriction enzyme cut sites.

The only disadvantages to using GeneArt are 1) cost and 2) potential for point mutation to occur in the gene synthesis process (although GeneArt is a very reliable platform and PCR cloning can also introduce mutations). If you are cloning your TCR from cDNA (i.e., not having it synthesized as GeneArt), see “Amplifying TCR alpha and beta chains” in Appendix. Otherwise, skip to “Assembling TCR consensus sequences” in the main protocol.


Amplify alpha and beta genes
NB: this step is NOT needed if you are purchasing your TCR as GeneArt!

Once we know the TCR sequence, we can clone the TCR genes into a vector plasmid and amplify the plasmid in E. coli. We can do this by designing primers that will incorporate a restriction site at each end of the TCR chains. Be sure that the restriction sites used are not present within the gene.

Primer design

Forward primer: Va and Vb genes:

i. Go to NCBI, nucleotide, search “Homo sapiens T cell receptor beta locus (TRB@) on chromosome 7”.

ii. Control F, search on your vb gene eg: ‘Trbv6-2’

iii. Click on ‘CDS’ then when it says “warning: cannot highlight feature because no sequence is shown,” click ‘show the sequence’

iv. Find the first 20 nucleotides. Copy and paste into a word document.

v. Add the nucleotides for the restriction enzyme. Use cloning software to check that the enzyme will not cut within the sequence.

vi. Add an extra C/G as needed to get a Kozak sequence (GCCGCCACCAUGG)

vii. Add an extra 3 bp so the enzyme isn’t cutting at the very end, eg ACT

a. Full primer sequence using NotI enzyme will be:

ACT GCGGCCGC C NNNNNNNNNNNNNNNNNNNN

Example:

f_va23_NotI

ACTGCGGCCGCCatggagaccctcttgggcctgctt

f vb6 NotI

ACTGCGGCCGCCATGGGCACCAGCCTCCTCTGCT

Reverse primers:

1. For beta chains, determine the constant gene used:

i. Use NCBI again and look up TRAC, TRBC1 and TRBC2 separately

a. Align the TCRb sequencing results with Cb1 and Cb2 sequences from NCBI to determine which gene is used by the TCR.

iii. For primers, find the last 20 bp of last exon of the constant gene used. (Should end in TAG, TGA, TAA)

TRBC1 gtc aag aga aag gat ttc tga

TRBC2 aga aag gat tcc aga ggc tag

iv. Add the restriction site eg EcoRI: 5'...G^A A T T C...3’

v. Add a random 3 base sequence

No Kozak sequence required. Use reverse complement since this is the reverse primer:

Example:

r_bC1 EcoRI

AGCTTGGAATTCTCAGAAATCCTTTCTCTTGACC

r _aC EcoRI

AGCTTGGAATTCTCAGCTGGACCACAGCCGCAGC

vi. Using the full length cDNA product as template, set up a reaction using the linker region primer for the known Vb chain along with a primer for the appropriate constant region.

Reaction:

10 μL 5x Buffer

1 μL dNTP

0.5 μL Primer 1

0.5 μL Primer 2

2.5 μL cDNA

0.5 μL Phusion Hi Fidelity polymerase

35 μL H2O

Run pcr:

98°C 1:00

98°C 0:10

62°C 0:10

72°C 0:10

(38 cycles)

72°C 0:10


Production of fresh TCR virus
If using fresh viral supernatant:

H20 = 10084.93μl x 11= 934.23μl90.7μl x 2= 181.25μl87.6μl x 2= 175.2μl

2.Thaw 2xHBS from -20°C. Once thawed, 2xHBS can be stored in 4°C for another use within a week.

3. While vortexing water, DNA, and CaCl2 solution mix, slowly and drop-wise add 150μL (300μl for plates 1 and 2) of 2xHBS. Keep vortexing for another ~5 secs.

4. Incubate 300μL (600μl) mixture at room temperature for 15 mins.

5. Add 300μL (600μl) of DNA suspension to each well in a slow drop-wise manner (use p200 pipetor; multiple spots on each well). Swirl the plate gently to distribute the DNA mixture evenly. Incubate in 37°C overnight. 10-12 hours

Day 0- (Medium exchange)

After incubation, aspirate all cDMEM medium from each well, replace it with 3mL of CTL medium.

Day 1- (Transduction)

1. Harvest supernatant: filter sup by using 0.45um filter

Fit the filter tip with 10mL pipet tip, collect the filtrate in 15 mL conical tube

Day 1: Transduction using fresh viral supernatant
  1. Remove 1mL of CTL media from each well.
  2. Add polybrene to 8 μg/mL f.c. into each well (stock at 1 μg/μl polybrene) and IL-2 to 100 units/mL into each well (will add back 1 mL media to controls or 1 mL viral sup to samples so f.c. will be 4 μg/mL for polybrene and 50 units/mL for IL-2)
  3. Add 1 mL filtered (0.45 um filter) viral supernatant to each well of T cells.
  4. Re-suspend cells in wells to mix all reagents.
  5. Spinoculation: 2000rpm (RCF 845xg), 32C, 90 min.
    1. Spininoculation is optional if using concentrated virus produced by method described above.
  6. Incubate the plate at 37C overnight. Incubation started at: _________
  7. Perform media changes as described for concentrated virus protocol.

TCR Cloning with gBlocks
Word20.3 KB· Yeh MD, Albert C· edited 2023-04-05
▼

Bench protocol from gBlock resuspension (1000 ng in 20 µL 1× TE) through SacII digestion and ligation.

⬇ Download Word · 20.3 KB

Source & citation

Document author metadata: Carrie L. Cummings (Bleakley Lab); edited by Albert Yeh.

Document record

Document author: Cummings, Carrie L
Last edited by: Yeh MD, Albert C
Created: 2023-04-05
Last modified: 2023-04-05
Revisions: 2

The protocol, step by step

TCR Cloning Protocol:
  1. Resuspend gBlocks (1000 ng) in 20 µLs 1x TE Buffer.
  2. Digest gBlock inserts (x2 tubes of each) as follows:

gBlock (300 ng) 6 µLs

10x CutSmart Buffer (NEB) 4 µLs

SacII Enzyme (NEB) 1 µL

RsrII Enzyme (NEB) 1 µL

MBG Water 28 µLs

40 µLs

Digest for 2 hours at 37°C.

Purify insert digests using Qiagen MinElute PCR Purification Kit.

Elute final product with 20 µLs MBG water.

Quantitate concentration of inserts using Qubit 1x dsDNA HS Assay Kit.

  1. Digest pRRL.PPT_MP_CBF Bracelet-RQR8 backbone vector as follows (x3-4 tubes):

Vector (2.5 µg) 2 µLs

10x CutSmart Buffer (NEB) 6 µLs

SacII Enzyme (NEB) 1 µL

RsrII Enzyme (NEB) 1 µL

BstEII-HF Enzyme (NEB) 1 µL

MBG Water 49 µLs

60 µLs

Digest for 3 hours at 37°C.

Add 1 µL of CIAP (20 U/µL) to each tube.

Incubate at 50°C for 15 minutes.

Run digests in 1% TAE Agarose Gel for 1 hour (combine tubes into as few wells as possible).

Cut largest bands and purify using QIAquick Gel Extraction Kit (use as few columns as possible).

Elute each column with 30 µLs MBG water and combine if necessary.

Quantitate concentration of purified backbone vector using Qubit 1x dsDNA HS Assay Kit.

  1. Set up ligations using the following formula (start with a 3:1 insert to vector ratio and 100-200 ng of vector). Also set up a negative control reaction (digested vector only, no insert):

(ratio)

ng of vector x kb of insert x insert = ng of insert to use

kb of vector vector

Example: 200 ng x 1.3 kb x 3 = 104 ng

7.5 kb 1

5x T4 DNA Ligase Buffer 4 µLs

Insert varies

Vector varies

T4 DNA Ligase (5 U/µL) 1 µL

MBG Water varies

20 µLs

Incubate overnight at 16°C.

  1. Transform ligations into One Shot Stbl3 Chemically Competent cells as follows:
    1. Thaw Stbl3 cells on ice.
    2. Add 5 µLs of each ligation reaction to one tube of competent cells.
    3. Incubate cells on ice for 30 minutes.
    4. Heat shock cells in a 42°C water bath for 45 seconds, then place on ice for 2 minutes.
    5. Add 200 µLs of room temperature SOC media (comes with cells) to each tube and incubate in a 37°C shaker (200-250 rpm) for 1 hour.
    6. Plate each tube of cells on LB/Carbenicillin plates (prewarmed to 37°C) and incubate overnight in a 37°C incubator.
  2. Remove plates from incubator and check for colonies. Ligation plates should have more colonies than the negative control plate. If the ligations failed, repeat them using a different insert to vector ratio (5:1 or 10:1).
  3. Pick 4-6 colonies from each plate and inoculate 6 mLs of LB Broth + 100 µg/mL Ampicillin (for minipreps). For each colony picked, also streak a small amount of the bacteria onto a new LB/Carbenicillin plate.
  4. Incubate the liquid cultures overnight at 37 deg C with shaking at 200-250 rpm. Incubate the streak plate at 37 deg C overnight.
  5. Remove streak plate from the incubator, wrap in parafilm, and store at 4 deg C (for future maxipreps).
  6. Perform minipreps on liquid cultures and elute with 60 µLs MBG water.
  7. Set up a diagnostic digest on minipreps as follows:

Miniprep DNA 10 µLs

10x CutSmart Buffer (NEB) 2 µLs

SacII Enzyme (NEB) 0.5 µL

RsrII Enzyme (NEB) 0.5 µL

MBG Water 7 µLs

20 µLs

Digest for 1 hour at 37°C.

Run digests in 1% TAE Agarose Gel for 1 hour and check for proper band size (positive clones will

have a band at the same size as the original insert).

  1. Set up sequencing PCR reactions on 2-4 positive minipreps from each using common forward and reverse primers as well as several primers that sit inside your insert as follows:

Miniprep DNA 1 µL

Primer (10 µM) 0.5 µL

BigDye® Mix 2 µL

DMSO 0.5 µL

MBG Water 6 µLs

10 µLs

Common Forward Primer: pRRL.PPT_JG2_BB_8775_Seq_F

Common Reverse Primer: pRRL.PPT_RQR8_Seq_R

PCR Reaction:

  1. 96°C for 5 min.
  2. 96°C for 10 sec.
  3. 50°C for 5 sec.
  4. 60°C for 4 min.
  5. Repeat steps 2-4 for 25 total cycles
  6. Hold at 4°C

Take sequencing reaction to core sequencing facility for processing.

  1. Check sequencing results for proper insert by blasting against original gBlock sequence.
  2. Choose a positive clone for each and perform maxipreps (pick matching colonies from streak plate), then sequence the maxipreps as above to verify inserts again. Proceed with virus production.
Reagents Needed:

1x TE Buffer: 10 mM Tris-HCl (pH 8.0) + 1 mM EDTA (can use Buffer TE from Qiagen kits)

Molecular Biological Grade (MBG) Water: Fisher Scientific (Corning Brand) Cat #MT46000CM

10x CutSmart Buffer: New England BioLabs Cat #B7204S (comes with enzymes)

SacII Enzyme: New England BioLabs Cat #R0157S

RsrII Enzyme: New England BioLabs Cat #R0501S

BstEII-HF Enzyme: New England BioLabs Cat #R3162S

MinElute PCR Purification Kit: Qiagen Cat #28004

Qubit™ 1x dsDNA HS Assay Kit: Thermo Fisher Cat #Q33230

10x TAE Buffer (Diluted to 1x with Millipore water): Fisher Scientific (Corning Brand) Cat #MT46010CM

Agarose LE: Fisher Scientific (Axygen Brand) Cat #14-223-080

QIAquick Gel Extraction Kit: Qiagen Cat #28704*

CIAP (20 U/µL): Thermo Fisher Cat #18009019*

T4 DNA Ligase (5 U/µLs): Thermo Fisher Cat #EL0014 (comes with buffer)*

One Shot Stbl3 Chemically Competent Cells: Thermo Fisher Cat #C737303*

LB/Carbenicillin Plates: Teknova Cat #L1010

QIAprep Spin Miniprep Kit: Qiagen Cat #27104*

BigDye® Terminator v3.1 Ready Reaction Mix: Purchase from Sequencing Core Facility

*Items available onsite in consignment room

TCR Reconstruction (slide deck)
PowerPointTCR Construction - Bleakley Lab/1.1 MB· Michelle Brault· edited 2020-07-24
▼

Slide walkthrough of the TCR reconstruction strategy.

⬇ Download PowerPoint · 1.1 MB

Source & citation

Bleakley Lab.

Document record

Document author: Michelle Brault
Last edited by: Michelle Brault
Created: 2020-07-23
Last modified: 2020-07-24
Revisions: 8

Slide by slide

Slide 1 · TCR reconstruction from Adaptive Sequencing
  • Prepared by: Michelle Brault
Slide 2 · Step 1: Go to IMGT Gene (http://www.imgt.org/genedb/)
  • The page should look like this!
Slide 3 · Step 2: Put in the correct identifiers
  • For gene type, it will be “Variable” for V regions and “Joining” for J regions. We will start with V regions.
  • You can attempt to search for your specific gene, like “TRAV13-01” but often the gene name will be slightly off and give you no results. I recommend leaving it blank.
Slide 4 · Step 3: Find your V regions
  • You will get a list of all variable regions for both alpha and beta chains. Find your V regions for each chain but BE SURE NOT TO MIX THEM UP!!
  • TRAV is an alpha V region.
  • TRBV is a beta V region.
  • Click the box of the regions you’d like to get a sequence for.
  • Note: Some genes have a dashed number, and some do not (e.g. TRAV1-1 vs TRAV10). Adaptive might give you a dashed number, but if that’s not available on the list, click the undashed one that corresponds to it (e.g. if you want TRAV10-1, click TRAV10)
Slide 5 · Step 4: Put in sequence parameters at bottom of page
  • After you’ve selected the regions you want, scroll all the way to the bottom. It should look like the image to the right.
  • Under right hand column, select to choose label(s), and use the box to scroll for L-PART1+V-EXON. This gives you the exon sequence. You do NOT want introns.
  • Click submit.
Slide 6 · Step 5: Copy your sequences to an excel sheet
  • You will get a list of results. Find the sequences that correspond to your identified region from Adaptive.
  • Example:
  • I was given TRAV13-1 and TRBV09*01 from Adaptive.
  • There are two results for TRAV13-1 (red circles), 13-1*01 and 13-1*03, but the *03 version does not seem to be complete (288nt vs 337nt, lacks atg at start). I will copy TRAV13-1*01.
  • There are two results for TRBV09 (blue circles), TRVB09*01 and TRVB09*02, but I want *01, so I will copy that.
  • Tip: If you copy the sequences right into excel, they will copy into multiple lines, which can cause problems with alignment later. I use http://removelinebreaks.net/ to remove the whitespace in the sequence so it copies into excel in one continuous sequence.
Slide 7 · Step 6: Mark where your V region stops in reference sequence
  • You will need the alpha and beta rearrangement sequence from Adaptive (indicated with red arrow). It helps if you copy it with the color formatting for alignment but it’s not necessary.
  • Copy your V regions from Step 5 into excel.
  • Using the FIRST 10ish bp of the rearrangement sequence, use “Find” (Ctrl+F) to match that sequence in your reference sequence. Moving 5’ to 3’, find the first base pair that is different between the two and mark breakpoint in both sequences. I’ve marked that point with ** and blue arrows. Notice how the sequence is the same before the ** and different after, which represents the rearrangement breakpoint. It also helps to use lowercase/capital letters or colors to help you see overlaps.
  • Note: If you have the Adaptive color coding, that will show you where breakpoints are.
Slide 8 · Step 7: Repeat steps 1-5 for J regions
  • For Step 2: Make sure to select “joining” in the “Gene type” dropdown box instead of “variable”
  • For Step 4: Make sure to select “J-REGION” from box instead of “L-PART1+V-EXON”
Slide 9 · Step 8: Mark where your J region starts in reference sequence
  • You will need the alpha and beta rearrangement sequence from Adaptive (indicated with red arrow). It helps if you copy it with the color formatting for alignment but it’s not necessary.
  • Copy your J regions into excel.
  • Using the LAST 10ish bp of the rearrangement sequence, use “Find” (Ctrl+F) to match that sequence in your reference sequence. Working 3’ to 5’ (backwards), find the first base pair that is different between the two and mark breakpoint in both sequences. I’ve marked that point with ** and blue arrows. The sequence in between your markers (red circle) is the unique rearrangement associated with your TCR. THIS SUPERCEDES THE REFERENCE SEQUENCES!!!!
  • Note: If you have the Adaptive color coding, that will show you where breakpoints are.
Slide 10 · Step 9: Reconstruct entire alpha/beta chain from references
  • Copy the reference sequence for your V region from the 5’ end UP TO THE MARKER (**). Paste into new cell.
  • Copy the sequence in between the markers in your alpha rearrangement sequence from Adaptive. Paste onto the end of the pasted sequence from part a. (red circle)
  • Copy the reference sequence of your J region STARTING AT THE MARKER all the way to the 3’ end. Paste onto the end of the pasted sequence from part b.
  • You now have your reconstructed alpha or beta chain. Notice that the rearrangement sequence from Adaptive lies in the middle of your chain – blue arrows at start and end for reference.

Titering Lentivirus (6/9/21)
Word718.0 KB· Lok, Jessica A· edited 2023-04-26
▼

Titre calculation by flow: plate concentrated virus in a 96-well V-bottom, 90 µL LCL 20% per well across 8 wells. Freeze-thaw the titre aliquot before use so it matches the stock you will actually thaw.

⬇ Download Word · 718.0 KB

Source & citation

Document author metadata: Melinda A. Biernacki, edited by Jessica A. Lok (Bleakley Lab).

Document record

Document author: Biernacki MD, Melinda A
Last edited by: Lok, Jessica A
Created: 2021-06-09
Last modified: 2023-04-26
Revisions: 4

The protocol, step by step

Lentivirus Titer calculation
Day 0:
  1. Produce concentrated virus by usual method. Set aside an aliquot of ~35 uL when making other aliquots.
    1. You can titer the same day as you make virus, but make sure to freeze and then thaw your titer aliquot before using in the experiment below.
  2. Plate concentrated virus in 96 well V bottom plate.
    1. Add 90uL LCL 20% to each of 8 wells
    2. Dilutions: 30 uL virus in the first well, mix, take from first well and then serial 4-fold serial dilutions (total 7 wells of virus). Leave 8th well as a no virus well.
  3. Transfer viral dilutions to 96 well plate in which you will be growing cells.
    1. Transfer 30uL from each well using a multi-channel pipette. Can transfer multiple rows if replicates are desired.
      1. NB: two replicates is standard
    2. NB: 96U is best for growing cells, but 96V is fine if you’d like to stain directly in the plate on d4
  4. Resuspend Jurkat 76 cells in media with polybrene
    1. For each well, we want 100,000 cells per well, 120 uL of cell suspension per well, and 0.6 uL polybrene.
    2. Here is an example of the math for the mastermix:
1x8x (1 row)
Cells100,000800,000
LCL 20%119.4uL955.2uL
Polybrene0.6uL4.8uL
  1. Plate 120 uL/well of cell mastermix (100,000 cells/well) on top of pre-plated diluted virus.
    1. You should get 150uL of total volume, which is important to the math at the end
  2. Incubate at 37C, 5% CO2, etc. overnight.
Day 1-2:
  1. Add 50 uL LCL 20% daily x 2-3 days and split to other well if need it.
  2. Evaluate transduction by flow at 72h or 3 days (e.g., for RQR8 tag or GFP expression).
    1. NB: results do not change much if you’d like to flow at a later timepoint, but make sure cells are kept well-fed to prevent die-off
Day 3 or 4: Tetramer Staining

Step 1: Tetramer stain (optional – dependent on construct)

  1. Plate cells (5,000 – 1M/well) in 96 well v-bottom plate if not already in one
  2. Spin down at 400g for 2 min
  3. Carefully remove supernatant. You want cells as dry as possible!
  4. Spin tetramer at 12000g for 2min at 4o before using
  5. Add 25uL tetramer mix (use 1:50 dilution if you do not have a known dilution)
  6. Gently resuspend cells in mix by pipetting
  7. Incubate 15 min at RT in the dark

Step 2: Antibody stain

  1. Make antibody mix
    1. Use titrations for 50uL staining volume
      1. CD34 AF700 (for RQR8 staining): 5 uL
      2. CD3 BV786: 3uL
      3. Optional: Murine C-beta PE-Cy5.5: 1uL
      4. Optional: IO test PE/FITC: 5uL
    2. Add MACS buffer to 25uL/well total, rather than 50uL
      1. Only if using tetramer (since it will be added on top of previous 25uL); if no tetramer, bring to 50uL/well
  2. Add 25uL or 50uL antibody (see above) mix to each well, pipette gently
  3. Incubate 10-15min in the dark, on ice (not at 4o)
  4. Wash in 150 uL cold MACS/PBS +5% FBS
  5. Spin at 400g for 2 min
  6. Remove wash
  7. Wash with 200 uL cold MACS/PBS +5% FBS, spin again
  8. Resuspend in 100+uL DAPI (1:12k)
  9. Run flow: no current machine or template
  10. Flow analysis:
    1. RQR8 staining is the standard for virus titration evaluation (other stains are helpful to gain understanding, but will not be factored into the math)
      1. NB: sometimes RQR8 does not appear well, but CD3 and other TCR-specific stains look good – if so, don’t worry, use CD3 for math
    2. You are looking for condition with ~10% transduced (well above background but not a huge amount).
    3. ~10% transduction indicates 10,000 cells transduced so 10,000 infectious doses (=particles).
    4. TU/mL = (Number of cells transduced x Percent fluorescent x Dilution Factor) / (Transduction Volume in mL)
  11. Then infect T cells with MOI 2 (standard chosen by ADE based on previous experiments)
    1. MOI = multiplicity of infection
  12. Virus volume = (Number of cells transduced x MOI) / (TU/mL)

Prot 101 — Human Ab General Protocol (panel folder copy)

Document record

No embedded metadata (legacy binary format).

The protocol, step by step

Immunofluorescence Staining Protocol using Directly Conjugated Antibodies:

(Used this protocol on 2/6/19 for Albert’s phenotype panel for exp 1903p)

  1. Prepare desired amount of single cell suspension (in DMEM) into 12x75mm polypropylene FACS tube and wash in 2mL staining buffer (PBS). Use ~1M PBMCs.
  2. Centrifuge (300 xg, 5 min, 4oC – already preset), discard supernatant by decanting.
  3. (Optional) Add 5ul/tube of blocking antibody (e.g. Fc Block). (We did not do this step.
    1. Vortex and incubate for 2 min at room temp.
  4. Add Live-Dead stain (we used L-34961 from Thermofisher, which takes up the DAPI channel).
    1. Vortex, stain for 15 min @ RT in dark.
  5. Add monoclonal antibody mix.
    1. Vortex and incubate for 15 min @ RT in dark.
  6. Add 2mL of FACS buffer (wash step, 1 is enough)
    1. Vortex and centrifuge (300 xg, 5 min, 4oC).
    2. Discard supernatant.
  7. Resuspend cells in 200uL FACS buffer
    1. Keep on ice.
    2. **Cells may be centrifuged and fixed in 1 ml of 1% paraformaldehyde (in PBS) at 4C for analysis next day. (we didn’t do this)

NOTES:
Use buffers without Phenol Red
The blocking antibody step (4 and 5) is optional but should be included if cells express high levels of Fc receptors which will contribute to non-specific binding and background fluorescence. We didn’t need to do this for the human experiment.

Prot 601 — Tetramer Staining Protocols
Word18.6 KB· Yeh MD, Albert C· edited 2024-07-22
▼

Working tetramer conditions, which vary by reagent in both temperature and concentration. Includes SFB-monomer-biotin (NIH) I-Aᵇ (DVQFSGAVPNKTD): premix 0.2 µg/mL monomer with SA-PE 1:1, then 2 µL per 100 µL sample, 37°C for 3 h.

⬇ Download Word · 18.6 KB

Yeh Lab (author metadata Yeh MD, Albert C). Monomer from the NIH Tetramer Core.

The protocol, step by step

[Prot 601] Tetramer Staining Protocols

Tetramer stains can be tricky, as the temperature required for optimal stain as well as concentration may vary. Below are staining protocols that have worked.

  1. SFB-Monomer-Biotin (NIH): I-Ab (DVQFSGAVPNKTD)
    1. Tetramerization: Premix 0.2ug/mL (diluted from NIH stock ~2mg/mL) SFB-monomer-biotin with 0.2ug/mL SA (1.2ug/mL SA-PE) in a 1:1 ratio, incubate at RT for 30 minutes prior to use.
    2. Use 2uL of resulting mix / 100uL sample, stain at 37C x 3 hours for optimal binding.
  2. SFB-Tetramer (NIH): I-Ab (DVQFSGAVPNKTD)
    1. Stain at 1:500 concentration, 37C x 2.5 hours (better than 0.5 or 1 hr). Seemed to get better results when fix cells in 4% PFA (+400uL to 100uL sample, incubate 15min on ice).
  3. CBir1-Tetramer (NIH): I-Ab (YSNANILSQ)
    1. Use 1:500 dilution of tetramer (NIH stock ~1.44 mg/mL), stain 100uL sample at RT x 30 minutes (this is sufficient).
  4. Bowie-Tetramer (NIH): H2-M3 B2M (fM)IIINA
    1. Use 1:100 or 1:200 dilution of tetramer (NIH stock ~1.4 mg/mL), stain 100uL sample at RT x 3 hours (longer is better). 4C and 37C both show sub-optimal stain. 1:100 seems to have more signal, but slightly more background when stained on control.
  5. m38-PE CMV tetramer
    1. Use 1:3200, stain at 37C for 30 minutes
  6. m45-APC CMV tetramer
    1. Use 1:800, stain at 37C for 30 minutes

The protocol, step by step

Flow Cytometry Core Facility After-Hours Training Contract (Startup/Shutdown)

The purpose of the training in the Flow Cytometry core facility is to protect the expensive instrumentation from abuse, ensure that it is operational for our entire user base, and protect the investment in the actual experiment being performed. This training is offered to all users of the facility. This training is designed for users who already have a working knowledge of how to run an experiment on a cell analyzer or cell sorter, which includes but not limited to opening an experiment, setting up parameters and voltages, creating compensation controls, creating a worksheet, and exporting experiment data.

Cell Analyzer?  Cell Sorter?  Cytometer Name(s): _________________________________________

Date(s) of Initial Training: ________________________ Date(s) of Competency Training: ________________________

Cell Analyzer Training (check when completed)
 Startup Filling sheath tank and emptying waste tank Turning on cytometer and computer Turning on lasers Starting cytometer software Running fluidics start up Running CST beads Running Ultra Rainbow beads Running clean plate Clearing a clog Recovering from software and/or computer crash Common errors during startup Other: __________________________ Shutdown Running fluidics shutdown Turning off cytometer and computer		Cell Sorter Training (check when completed) Startup Filling sheath tank and emptying waste tank Turning on cytometer and computer Turning on lasers Starting cytometer software Cleaning flow cell Running fluidics start up Turning stream on and off Recognizing a stable stream profile Adjusting breakoff window values Running CST beads Running ultra-rainbow beads Running accudrop beads Clearing a clog Recovering from software and/or computer crash Common errors during startup Other: __________________________ Shutdown Running fluidics shutdown Turning off cytometer and computer

By signing this contract, the user agrees that the flow cytometry core staff member has trained the user to a high degree of competency in all categories list above for the specific cytometer(s) mentioned above which allows for the user to be self-sufficient in using the equipment after-hours. Further, if at any time the individual fails to show competency, not follow standard operating procedures, or damages the equipment, after-hours access may be revoked. Further training will then be required to reinstate access.

The protocol, step by step

Training Guide

Overview of the Cytometer Software
  • Creating a new experiment: Under the ‘Experiments’ drop down select new experiment. This will bring up previously saved templates. Select your template or under the ‘general’ tab find ‘New Experiment with blank sample tube.’ Experiments must be labeled with your Fred Hutch ID first and then the day’s date (mmddyr). For example, if your Fred Hutch is user1 and the date is January 1, 2020, name the experiment user1 010120. Following this, you can add any unique identifiers for that specific experiment. This ensures that the data will be archived correctly.
  • Cytometer settings file (also known as instrument settings in older files): This file contains parameter voltage settings, threshold set point, and laser delay settings that are used to acquire data. Only the FSC voltage, SSC voltage, and the threshold are adjustable. All other settings are predetermined in the control file.
  • Cytometer settings file (global vs tube-specific): The global cytometer settings file is located directly under the experiment name. After collection this file is automatically copied to each tube run and is saved with each tube.
  • Parameter usage: Determine the detectors needed for each experiment and delete all parameters not being used in the experiment. This is located in the cytometer window under parameters.
  • Creating a new experimental template file: Right click on experiment name and export experiment template. Save the template under your name tab and use a template name with any unique identifiers needed.
  • Exporting data: Right click on experiment name and export FCS v. 3.0 files (while other formats can be exported always export using this format). Export this data to your HomeLink drive. All data in the flow core facility is archived monthly and older data is removed from the cytometer. It is your responsibility to obtain your data when after you complete your experiment.

For more information on using cytometer software, please refer to software manuals on the BD or Sony websites.


BD LSRFortessa X-50 SORP / BD FACSymphony A5 SORP: Startup and Shutdown
Filling the Sheath Tank and Emptying Waste Tank
  1. Check the weight of the sheath tank, a full tank (6L) is between 11 and 13 kg. Tanks should never run empty. A good indicator of the liquid level in the sheath tank is by checking how full the waste tank is.
  2. Empty waste tank: Unscrew the waste level sensor, remove it, and pour waste down the sink.
  3. Do not unscrew at the connection of the waste level sensor as this will break the sensor.
  4. Fill sheath tank: Pull pressure release valve (key ring) to release the pressure, unlatch air and sheath quick connects, and disconnect the sheath sensor. Remove lid and fill the sheath tank with sheath fluid (non-sterile 1X PBS) to the top weld line. Replace the lid and ensure that it is tight and secured. Reconnect the air and sheath quick connects and the sheath sensor.
  5. Do not unscrew at the connection of the sheath level sensor as this will break the sensor.
  6. Do not overfill the sheath tank as this will lead to back pressure and regulator issues
Starting Up Cytometer and Performing Fluidics Startup
  1. Start the cytometer by pressing the GREEN button on the right side of the instrument.
  2. Press the PRIME button.
  3. Once the prime is complete the instrument will reset, and the STANDBY button will illuminate.
  4. Place a FACS tube with ~ 2 mL 40% Contrad onto the cytometer and run on ‘high’ at a flow rate of 500 for 5 minutes.
  5. Remove FACS tube and press PRIME button.
  6. Place a FACS tube with ~2 mL of blue detergent (Coulter Clenz) onto the cytometer and run on ‘high’ at a flow rate of 500 for 5 minutes.
  7. Remove FACS tube and press PRIME button.
  8. Place a FACS tube with ~2 mL of 70% ethanol onto the cytometer and run on ‘high’ at a flow rate of 500 for 5 minutes.
  9. Remove FACS tube and press PRIME button.
  10. Place a FACS tube with ~2 mL of distilled H2O onto the cytometer and run on ‘high’ at a flow rate of 500 for 5 minutes.
  11. Remove FACS tube and press PRIME button.
  12. Place a FACS tube with ~2 mL of distilled H2O onto the cytometer and press STANDBY button.
  13. Bleed the sheath filter on the tank so that the line is free of air bubbles.
Starting Up Computer, Lasers, and Software
  1. Turn on the computer by pressing the computer power button.
  2. Log on to the ‘admin’ user for windows using the password: BDIS#1
  3. Open “Fiber Laser” software by clicking this button:
  4. If the ‘current,’ ‘power,’ and ‘SHG temp. values are blank without green numbering, then you are not connected to the correct com port (see figure to below). To connect to the right com port click this button: . Find the serial port where you see green number in the ‘current,’ ‘power,’ and ‘SHG temp. values.
    • Note: If none of serial ports are showing green numbering, restart the cytometer and computer.
  5. Type 200 into the power.
  6. Click On button.
  7. Click Activate button.
  8. The current should increase and the power should be ~200 mW.
  9. Open ‘BD Coherent’ icon on the desktop and the coherent connection software will open (see figure below)
  10. The 355 nm laser does not automatically start when opening the program and must be started manually. Enter 65 as the mW power then click ‘laser STOP’ and ‘laser START’. All lasers should be at the set power.
  11. Open BD FACSDIVA by clicking this button:
  12. Log in to Administrator profile by selecting Administrator in the drop down. No password is needed.
  13. The cytometer will connect to the computer.
    • Note: if the cytometer does not connect to the computer, restart the cytometer and computer.
Running Cytometer Setup and Tracking (CST) Beads
  1. Under ‘Cytometer’ select CST. This will open up a window for running a cytometer setup and tracking performance check.
  2. Load a FACS tube with ~2 mL of the appropriate CST beads in sterile 1x PBS.
  3. Match CST bead lot in FACS tube with the bead lot number set in the CST settings on the right of the CST window. If the bead lot is set to 00000, check with the flow cytometry core lab staff for the current bead lot number for this cytometer.
  4. Press RUN button and LOW button on cytometer.
  5. Ensure “Check Performance” is clicked. Click ‘Run’ on CST window.
  6. If performance check passes, click ‘finish’ and exit out of the CST setup window.
  7. If performance check does not pass, perform the following steps:
    • Ensure the cytometer is not clogged.
    • Prime cytometer.
    • Vortex beads or prepare new beads.
    • If the CST performance still does not pass, proceed to the Ultra Rainbow section below and check the CV values of each parameter. If they are all <10 then it is okay to run your samples.
  8. Remove FACS tube with CST beads from the cytometer.
  9. Place a FACS tube with ~2 mL of distilled H2O onto the cytometer and press STANDBY button.
  10. Once the CST window is closed and the cytometer reconnects, a prompt will appear asking what settings you would like to use. You will select “Use CST Settings” as you have just run a performance check that passed on the CST beads.
Running Ultra-Rainbow (UR) 3.8 um Beads
  1. Open the file for the current year and select the experiment for current month. Expand out the specimen and add a new tube label this tube with the current day’s date: mmddyr
  2. Click green arrow to the left of the tube you named with today’s date.
  3. Load FACS tube with ~2 mL of UR beads.
  4. Press RUN and LOW and set fine adjust to 250.
  5. Click acquire and ensure that the events are within the set gates and click record to record 10,000 events.
  6. Remove FACS tube with UR beads from the cytometer.
  7. Place a FACS tube with ~2 mL of distilled H2O onto the cytometer and press STANDBY button.
  8. Record UR bead values in log book.
Preparing to Run User Experiment
  1. In DIVA software, click File and Log Out.
  2. Log into FHCRC profile. No password is needed.
  3. Create a new daily folder and label it with the current day’s date: mmddyr
  4. Run your experiment.
Shutdown
  1. Place a FACS tube with ~ 2 mL 10% bleach (NaOCl) onto the cytometer and run on ‘high’ at a flow rate of 500 for 5 minutes.
  2. Remove FACS tube and press PRIME button.
  3. Place a FACS tube with ~2 mL of blue detergent (Coulter Clenz) onto the cytometer and run on ‘high’ at a flow rate of 500 for 5 minutes.
  4. Remove FACS tube and press PRIME button.
  5. Place a FACS tube with ~2 mL of distilled H2O onto the cytometer and run on ‘high’ at a flow rate of 500 for 5 minutes.
  6. Remove FACS tube and press PRIME button.
  7. Place a FACS tube with ~2 mL of distilled H2O onto the cytometer and press STANDBY button.
  8. Press the GREEN button on the right side of the cytometer to turn off the machine.
  9. Exit out of BD FACSDIVA software.
  10. Click the windows home icon and select shutdown.
  11. Ensure that the cytometer and computer turned off.

BD FACSCanto II: Startup and Shutdown
Filling the Sheath Tank and Emptying Waste Tank
  1. Check the fluid levels. This can be done by checking their weight or opening the lids. The weight of the sheath tank when full (20L) is approximately 20 kg. Tanks should never run empty. There is a level indicator in the diva program on the ‘cytometer’ window.
  2. Empty waste tank: Unscrew the waste level sensor, remove it, and pour waste down the sink.
  3. Fill sheath tank: Disconnect the sheath sensor. Fill the sheath tank with sheath fluid (non-sterile 1X PBS). Replace the sheath sensor.
    • Do not overfill the sheath tank as this will lead to back pressure and regulator issues
  4. Bleed the three fluid filters on the front of the fluidics cart by unscrewing the bleed port. These are gravity fed and pressure from the tanks should force air out of the bleed ports. Replace the plugs once the air is removed.
Starting Up Cytometer, Computer, and Performing Fluidics Startup
  1. Start the cytometer by pressing the GREEN button on the left side of the instrument.
  2. Turn on the computer by pressing the computer power button.
  3. Log on to the ‘admin’ user for windows using the following password:
    • Canto 2 - 1 password: BDIS#1
    • Canto 2 - 2 password: BDIS
  4. The lasers will automatically turn on.
  5. Open BD FACSDIVA by clicking this button:
  6. Log in to Administrator profile by selecting Administrator in the drop down. No password is needed.
  7. The cytometer will connect to the computer.
    • Note: if the cytometer does not connect to the computer, restart the cytometer and computer.
  8. A prompt will then appear asking what settings you would like to use. You will select “Use CST Settings” as you have just run a performance check that passed on the CST beads.
  9. Attach HTS coupler.
  10. From the drop down, menu at the top select ‘Cytometer,’ then select ‘Cleaning Modes’ and ‘Prime Tank after Fill.’ A window will appear with three boxes that can be check marked. Check all three boxes and select ‘Run’
    • Perform this action twice.
  11. From the drop down, menu at the top select ‘Cytometer,’ then select ‘Bubble Filter Purge and Degas Flow Cell’.
    • Perform this action twice.
  12. From the drop down, menu at the top select ‘Cytometer,’ then select ‘Fluidics Startup’. A window will appear notifying you that this action will take 10 minutes to complete. Click ‘Run’ to proceed.
    • If HTS coupler is not attached, it will prompt you to attach the HTS coupler.
  13. When fluidics startup is complete, click ok on the popup window.
Running Cytometer Setup and Tracking (CST) Beads
  1. Under ‘Cytometer’ select CST. This will open up a window for running a cytometer setup and tracking performance check.
  2. Load a FACS tube with ~2 mL of the appropriate CST beads in sterile 1x PBS.
  3. Match CST bead lot in FACS tube with the bead lot number set in the CST settings on the right of the CST window. If the bead lot is set to 00000, check with the flow cytometry core lab staff for the current bead lot number for this cytometer.
  4. Ensure “Check Performance” is clicked. Click ‘Run’ on CST window.
  5. If performance check passes, click ‘finish’ and exit out of the CST setup window.
  6. If performance check does not pass, perform the following steps:
    • Ensure the cytometer is not clogged.
    • Prime cytometer.
    • Vortex beads or prepare new beads.
    • If the CST performance still does not pass, proceed to the Ultra Rainbow section below and check the CV values of each parameter. If they are all <10 then it is okay to run your samples.
  7. Remove FACS tube with CST beads from the cytometer.
  8. Place a FACS tube with ~2 mL of distilled H2O onto the cytometer and press STANDBY button.
  9. Once the CST window is closed and the cytometer reconnects, a prompt will appear asking what settings you would like to use. You will select “Use CST Settings” as you have just run a performance check that passed on the CST beads.
Running Ultra-Rainbow (UR) 3.8 um Beads
  1. Open the file for the current year and select the experiment for current month. Expand out the specimen and add a new tube label this tube with the current day’s date: mmddyr
  2. Click green arrow to the left of the tube you named with today’s date.
  3. Load FACS tube with ~2 mL of UR beads.
  4. Set flow rate to LOW.
  5. Click acquire and ensure that the events are within the set gates and click record to record 10,000 events.
  6. Remove FACS tube with UR beads from the cytometer.
  7. Place a FACS tube with ~2 mL of distilled H2O onto the cytometer and press STANDBY button.
  8. Record UR bead values in log book.
Preparing to Run User Experiment
  1. In DIVA software, click File and Log Out.
  2. Log into FHCRC profile. No password is needed.
  3. Create a new daily folder and label it with the current day’s date: mmddyr
  4. Run your experiment.
Shutdown
  1. Attach HTS coupler.
  2. From the drop down, menu at the top select ‘Cytometer,’ then select ‘Fluidics Shutdown’. A window will appear notifying you that this action will take 5 minutes to complete. Click ‘Run’ to proceed.
    1. If HTS coupler is not attached, it will prompt you to attach the HTS coupler.
  3. Once completed. Click ‘Ok’ Press the GREEN button on the side of the cytometer to turn off the machine.
  4. Press the GREEN button on the left side of the cytometer to turn off the machine.
  5. Exit out of BD FACSDIVA software.
  6. Click the windows home icon and select shutdown.
  7. Ensure that the cytometer and computer turned off.

BD FACSAria II and BD Symphony S6: Startup and Shutdown
Filling the Sheath Tank and Emptying Waste Tank
  1. Check the weight of the sheath tank, a full tank (6L) is between 11 and 13 kg. Tanks should never run empty. A good indicator of the liquid level in the sheath tank is by checking how full the waste tank is.
  2. Empty waste tank: Unscrew the waste level sensor, remove it, and pour waste down the sink.
    • Do not unscrew at the connection of the waste level sensor as this will break the sensor.
  3. Fill sheath tank: Pull pressure release valve (key ring) to release the pressure, unlatch air and sheath quick connects, and disconnect the sheath sensor. Attach sheath hose and quick connect vent. Do not remove lid and fill the sheath tank with sheath fluid (sterile 1X PBS). Reconnect the air and sheath quick connects and the sheath sensor.
    • Do not unscrew at the connection of the sheath level sensor as this will break the sensor.
    • Do not overfill the sheath tank as this will lead to back pressure and regulator issues
  4. Reattach all tanks to the cytometer.
Starting up the Cytometer, Computer, Lasers, and Software
  1. Start the cytometer by pressing the GREEN button on the side of the machine.
  2. Turn on the computer and log on to the ‘admin’ user (Arias) or ‘BDadmin’ user (Symphony S6) for Windows using the following password:
    • Aria 2 - 2 and Symphony S6: BDIS#1
    • Aria 2 - 3: BDIS
  3. Open “Fiber Laser” software by clicking this button:
  4. If the ‘current,’ ‘power,’ and ‘SHG temp. values are blank without green numbering, then you are not connected to the correct com port (see figure below). To connect to the right com port click this button: . Find the serial port where you see green number in the ‘current,’ ‘power,’ and ‘SHG temp. values.
    • Note: If none of serial ports are showing green numbering, restart the cytometer and computer.
  5. Type 200 into the power.
  6. Click On button.
  7. Click Activate button.
  8. The current should increase and the power should be ~200 mW.
  9. For Aria 2 - 2 only: Open ‘BD Coherent’ icon on the desktop and the coherent connection software will open (see figure below).
    • The 355 nm laser does not automatically start when opening the program and must be started manually. Enter 65 as the mW power then click ‘laser STOP’ and ‘laser START’. All lasers should be at the set power.
  10. Open BD FACSDIVA by clicking this button:
  11. Log in to Administrator profile by selecting Administrator in the drop down. No password is needed.
  12. The cytometer will connect to the computer.
    • Note: if the cytometer does not connect to the computer, restart the cytometer and computer.
Performing Fluidics Startup
  1. Sonicate nozzle that will be used in experiment. After sonicating, spray the nozzle with 70% ethanol and use the air compressor or canned air to completely dry the nozzle.
  2. Remove the closed loop nozzle and black nozzle holster.
  3. Twist up the corner of a Kimwipe and place it into the space where the nozzle would normally go. Also, insert a Kimwipe below the nozzle location above the deflection plates.
  4. Load a 5 mL FACS tube with 40% Contrad on the loader.
  5. Under “cytometer” from the top bar, select ‘cleaning modes’ and then ‘clean flow cell.’ Repeat this until the tube is dry.
  6. Load a 5 mL FACS tube with 70% ethanol on the loader.
  7. Under “cytometer” from the top bar, select ‘cleaning modes’ and then ‘clean flow cell.’ Repeat this multiple times.
  8. Remove the Kimwipes. Replace the closed loop nozzle and the black nozzle holster.
  9. Under ‘cytometer’ click ‘fluidics startup.’ Click Ok on all prompts after completing each step. This will run for 5-10minutes.
  10. Bleed the sheath filter on the tank so that the line is free of air bubbles.
  11. Wipe off the deflector plates and soak up any droplets where the closed loop nozzle and black nozzle holster were using a Kimwipe.
  12. Insert the clean, sonicated nozzle into where the closed loop nozzle was. Replace the black nozzle holster.
  13. Turn on the stream by clicking the red X above the stream image window and let run for 10 minutes to allow for purging of any residual air or ethanol.
Obtaining Purity Sample
  1. Fill the tube containing ~2.5 mL of LB broth with about 1.0 mL of PBS from the sort stream.
  2. Replace cap and leave in biosafety cabinet to incubate at room temperature for 7 days.
Running Cytometer Setup and Tracking (CST) Beads
  1. Under ‘Cytometer’ select CST. This will open up a window for running a cytometer setup and tracking performance check. Make sure the configuration is for the nozzle that is being used in the experiment.
  2. Load a FACS tube with ~2 mL of the appropriate CST beads in sterile 1x PBS onto loader.
    • Match CST bead lot in FACS tube with the bead lot number set in the CST settings on the right of the CST window. If the bead lot is set to 00000, check with the flow cytometry core lab staff for the current bead lot number for this cytometer.
  3. Ensure “Check Performance” is clicked. Click ‘Run’ on CST window.
  4. If performance check passes, click ‘finish’ and exit out of the CST setup window.
  5. If performance check does not pass, perform the following steps:
    • Ensure the cytometer is not clogged and stream profile looks okay.
    • Vortex beads or prepare new beads.
    • If the CST performance still does not pass, proceed to the Ultra Rainbow section below and check the CV values of each parameter. If they are all <10 then it is okay to run your samples.
  6. Remove FACS tube with CST beads from the cytometer.
  7. Once the CST window is closed and the cytometer reconnects, a prompt will appear asking what settings you would like to use. You will select “Use CST Settings” as you have just run a performance check that passed on the CST beads.
Running Ultra-Rainbow (UR) 3.8 um Beads
  1. Open the file for the current year and select the experiment for current month. Expand out the specimen and add a new tube label this tube with the current day’s date: mmddyr
  2. Click green arrow to the left of the tube you named with today’s date.
  3. Place FACS tube with ~2 mL of UR beads onto loader.
  4. Click load. The cytometer will automatically start acquiring data.
  5. Set flow rate to 1.0.
  6. Ensure that the events are within the set gates and click record to record 10,000 events.
  7. Unload FACS tube.
Running Accudrop Beads
  1. In DIVA software, click File and Log Out.
  2. Log into FHCRC profile. No password is needed.
  3. Open accudrop delay experiment.
  4. Load Accudrop beads onto loader.
  5. Click load. The cytometer will automatically start acquiring data.
  6. Adjust flow rate such that the event rate is 2,000 – 3,000 events/second.
  7. Open up the sort layout. Set device to 2 tube. Set precision to Fine Tune. Make sure that the correct accudrop gate is selected for the beads to be sorted into the left.
  8. Turn on the voltage.
  9. Turn on the optical filter.
  10. Start the sort. Click ‘cancel’ on the pop-up note for the waste drawer.
  11. Adjust the deflection to ensure accudrops are centered in the optical filter box on the left.
  12. Adjust “drop delay” to maximize the sort stream percentage or run the “auto delay” program to get the percentage as close to 100% as possible.
  13. Unload FACS tube.
Preparing to Run User Experiment
  1. Create a new daily folder and label it with the current day’s date: mmddyr
  2. Run your experiment.
Shutdown
  1. Click ‘Cytometer’ and select ‘fluidics shutdown.’ Then follow the prompts and click ‘OK’ after each one is complete. Allow the sorter to finish the shutdown procedure.
    • Remove nozzle from flow cell.
    • Install the closed loop nozzle into the flow cell.
    • Connect the air and fluidics lines to the stainless-steel ethanol tank
      • Do NOT remove the sheave filter from the sheath tank.
      • Make sure the ethanol tank is full.
    • Release pressure from sheath tank.
    • Place a FACS tube with ~5 mL FACS Rinse as the “cleaning solution” onto the loading port.
  2. Press the GREEN button on the left of the cytometer to turn off the machine.
  3. Exit out of BD FACSDIVA software.
  4. Click the windows home icon and select shutdown.
  5. Ensure that the cytometer and computer turned off.


Sony MA900: Startup and Shutdown
Filling the Sheath Tank and Emptying Waste Tank
  1. Before powering on the system, check the levels of sheath fluid and waste in the tanks.
  2. Empty waste tank: Remove the tank from the fluidics cart (disconnecting the red waste line) and disposing of the liquid in the sink before securely reconnecting the tank to the system.
  3. Fill sheath tank: Attach sheath hose to female-female adaptor and attach the female adaptor to sheath line quick connect. Do not remove lid and fill the sheath tank with sheath fluid (sterile 1X PBS). Reconnect the air and sheath quick connects to the wet cart.
Cleaning Collection Chamber and Checking Air Compressor
  1. Open the instrument’s collection chamber and remove the black plates from the instrument by carefully unscrewing them. Spray the plates with 70% ethanol and dry with a Kimwipe.
  2. Spray a Kimwipe with 70% ethanol and wipe down all surfaces inside the collection chamber. Dry with a new Kimwipe
  3. Check that the air compressor is on and functioning.
Starting up the Cytometer, Computer, Lasers, and Software
  1. If a user has logged out of windows, log into the ‘fcm’ user with the password ‘fcm’.
  2. Power on the cytometer using the Power/Standby button and wait until it goes into Standby.
    1. Do not continue if it says processing, error, or initializing. Wait until it is in Standby.
  3. Open the MA900 Cell Sorter Software from the desktop.
  4. Login to the MA Cell Sorter software using ‘user1’ and ‘password1’.
  5. Proceed through the software’s prompts for Chip Detection and Chip Loading. Use a new 100 um sorting chip each day.
  6. Scan the QR code on the packaging using the camera on the monitor. Follow the commands in the software, hitting ‘Next’ prior to loading the chip.
  7. Open the cytometer lid using the black “Push open” button and install the chip vertically into its slot, ensuring that the ‘100 um’ text is upright and facing you. Push down gently on the chip and wait for it to load.
    1. A used chip will appear: discard any old sorting chips, but retain the cleaning chip.
  8. Proceed through the startup commands by ensuring that the check boxes for all four lasers are checked (they may not populate automatically depending on the MA900 version).
  9. Select the Standard option and hit Next.
  10. The instrument will now do a pressure and fluidics check. Watch to see if the flow stream is stable between flushes.
    1. Jittery/jumpy streams are a sign that the instrument may not pass this startup step successfully due to ethanol or air in the system. If so, perform bubble filter purges until the stream is stable.
  11. As instructed by the software, look inside the lit sample chamber. Check to ensure that back dripping is occurring by looking for droplets coming from the sample line.
  12. It should take ~10 minutes for this setup step to complete.
Obtaining Purity Sample
  1. Fill the tube containing ~2.5 mL of LB broth with about 1.0 mL of PBS from the sort stream.
  2. Replace cap and leave in biosafety cabinet to incubate at room temperature for 7 days.
Performing Automatic Calibration
  1. Click the prompts to perform the automatic calibration.
  2. Prepare the automatic calibration beads:
    1. The automatic calibration beads are stored at room temperature near the cytometers.
    2. Mix the beads very well by vortexing.
    3. The beads are used undiluted. Use ~2 mL of bead-containing solution in a 5mL FACS tube to calibrate.
  3. Once the beads are loaded in the proper tube holder, initiate the Auto Calibration by selecting Standard, hit Okay, load the tube of well-mixed, undiluted ~1mL beads on the instrument and hit Start. This step should take ~10 minutes.
  4. If the calibration fails:
    1. Check that the deflection plates are attached correctly
    2. Perform the Chip Debubble, Vent Air, and Sheath Filter Air Debubble functions
    3. If it continually fails, perform a chip exchange. Follow the prompts to restart the system and perform automatic calibration again.
  5. When finished, hit Okay, remove the beads and check the droplet image. If the green light is lit solidly (not off, not blinking), the instrument is ready for use.
    1. If the green light continues to blink, a prompt will show informing you to reperform the sort calibration. From the cytometer menu, click sort calibration and follow the prompts as you did in the initial start up.
Shutdown
  1. Select “Software and Hardware Shutdown” from the cytometer menu in the shutdown section and follow the prompts in the shutdown wizard.
  2. Click ‘start’.
  3. Click ‘normal cleaning’.
  4. Load 10 mL bleach in a 15 mL conical tube into the sample chamber.
  5. Click ‘start’.
  6. Click ‘next’.
  7. Click ‘normal cleaning’ and ‘keep DI water inside sample probe’.
  8. Load 10 mL distilled H2O into the sample chamber.
  9. Click ‘start’.
  10. Click ‘next’.
  11. Click ‘ok’ to shutdown.
  12. Ensure that the cytometer has turned off. Leave the computer on.

Instrument Configuration Overview

Cell Analysis Instruments

  • BD LSRFortessa X-50 Special Order Research Product (1)
    • Fortessa X-50: 5 lasers (355 nm, 406 nm, 488 nm, 552 nm, 628 nm), 28 color detection
  • BD FACSymphony A5 Special Order Research Product (4)
    • Symphony 1: 5 lasers (355 nm, 405 nm, 488 nm, 552 nm, 628 nm), 28 color detection
    • Symphony 2: 5 lasers (355 nm, 406 nm, 488 nm, 552 nm, 628 nm), 28 color detection
    • Symphony 3: 5 lasers (355 nm, 406 nm, 488 nm, 552 nm, 628 nm), 28 color detection
    • Symphony 4: 5 lasers (355 nm, 406 nm, 488 nm, 552 nm, 628 nm), 28 color detection
  • BD FACSCelesta Special Order Research Product (3)
    • Celesta 1: 4 lasers (405 nm, 488 nm, 561 nm, 637 nm), 16 color detection
    • Celesta 2: 4 lasers (405 nm, 488 nm, 561 nm, 637 nm), 16 color detection
    • Celesta 3: 4 lasers (405 nm, 488 nm, 561 nm, 637 nm), 16 color detection
  • BD LSRII Special Order Research Product (1)
    • LSR 2 - 2: 4 lasers (405 nm, 488 nm, 532 nm, 642 nm), 18 color detection
  • BD FACSCanto II (2)
    • Canto 2 - 1: 3 lasers (405 nm, 488 nm, 633 nm), 8 color detection
    • Canto 2 - 2: 3 lasers (405 nm, 488 nm, 633 nm), 8 color detection
Cell Sorting Instruments
  1. BD Symphony S6 Special Order Research Product (2)
    • Symphony S6 - 1: 5 lasers (355 nm, 404 nm, 488 nm, 552 nm, 628 nm), 29 color detection
    • Symphony S6 - 2: 5 lasers (355 nm, 402 nm, 488 nm, 552 nm, 628 nm), 29 color detection
  2. BD FACSAria II (2)
    • Aria 2 - 2: 5 lasers (355 nm, 404 nm, 488 nm, 561 nm, 627 nm), 18 color detection
    • Aria 2 - 3: 4 lasers (405 nm, 488 nm, 552 nm, 633 nm), 14 color detection
  3. Sony MA900 Multi-Application Cell Sorter (3)
    • Sony MA900 - 1: 4 Lasers (405nm, 488 nm, 561nm, 638 nm), 12 color detection
    • Sony MA900 - 2: 4 Lasers (405nm, 488 nm, 561nm, 638 nm), 12 color detection
    • Sony MA900 - 3: 4 Lasers (405nm, 488 nm, 561nm, 638 nm), 12 color detection

For more information on the configurations, please refer to our website at fredhutch.org.

Cell Prep for 10x Sequencing — Experiment Template

Fill-in bench template for a 10x run: harvest BM from 9 mice (3 groups × 3), keep counts within ~5–10×10⁶ of each other within a group, and hashtag each mouse separately at 5×10⁶ per 100 µL.

⬇ Download Word · 17.7 KB

Source & citation

Document author: Nemychenkov, Nicole

Created: 2024-04-12
Last modified: 2024-04-17
Revisions: 15

The protocol, step by step

Cell Prep for Sequencing (Week 7)
  1. Harvest BM from 9 mice (3 groups, 3 mice per group)
    1. Ensure counts are within ~5-10 x 106 of each other within each group
  2. Hashtag each mouse separately (stain @ 5 x 106 / 100 uL)
Hash1234578910
Mouse ID
GroupNL-201NL-201NL-201PBS RelPBS RelPBS RelPBS ConPBS ConPBS Con
Counts (in 10 mL)
  1. Resuspend each sample in ~ 50 x 106 cells / 1 mL FACS buffer
  2. Add 1 uL hash / 1 mL sample & stain for 20 mins on ice
  3. Wash x2
  1. Combine 3 NL-201 samples into one tube, 3 PBS relapsed samples into second tube, and 3 PBS controlled samples into third tube
  2. Combined CD4 and CD8 MACS (assume 50% target)
    1. Resuspend samples in 40 uL FACS buffer / 107 cells
    2. Add CD4 and CD8 beads @ 1 uL beads / 106 target cells (target cells = 50% of actual count) – beads in green/yellow boxes
FACS BufferBeads
  1. Fridge for 15 mins
  2. Wash, spin, and resuspend in 500 uL FACS buffer / 100 x 106 cells (minimum 500 uL)
  3. Wash LS columns with FACS buffer, change collection tube
  4. Run cells through x2, wash columns with FACS buffer x2
  5. Plunge columns with 5-6 mL FACS buffer, KEEP FLOWTHROUGH
  1. Stain for sort & TotalSeqC simultaneously, keeping NL-201, PBS relapsed, and PBS controlled separate
    1. Stain in 100 uL for 20 mins on ice (if in a rush, 10 mins RT is ok)
T Cells (Plunged)TME (Flowthrough)
CD4 (GK1.5) – FITC (0.5) CD8 – APC (0.5) CD90.2 – PE (0.125) CD4 (RM4-5) – TotalSeqC (0.5) CD8 – TotalSeqC (0.5)CD45.2 – FITC (0.5) CD138 – PE (0.5)
  1. Prep cells for sort
    1. Resuspend all samples in 1/3 7AAD and 2/3 FACS. Volumes for each sample below:
NL-201 T CellsNL-201 TMEPBS Rel T CellsPBS Rel TMEPBS Con T CellsPBS Con TME
  1. Use blue sort tip filter lid to filter cells into regular sort tube
  2. Prep 9 labeled sort tubes with 500 uL IMDM complete for collection
  3. Prep 50 mL conical of FACS buffer in case dilution is needed
  1. Sort into 9 groups, listed below
  2. Combine into two samples: NL-201 & PBS. Resuspend in calculated volume (in Eppendorf tube), then count both samples on hemocytometer to confirm cell concentration.
NL-201, aim to capture 12k cellsPBS, aim to capture 20k cells
Minimum needed for captureAcquired on sortSample volumeMinimum needed for captureAcquired on sortSample volume
4k CD8s 4k CD4s 4k TME3k Rel CD8s 3k Rel CD4s 4k Rel TME 3k Con CD8s 3k Con CD4s 4k Con TME

Enrichment of Human γδ TCR mRNA for 10x V(D)J

Custom primer mixes (e.g. 10x_hTCR-D/G Outer Mix at 4 µM) that let the standard 10x V(D)J workflow capture γδ chains. All other steps are identical to the stock 10x protocol.
