The protocol, step by step

10x Genomics V(D)J Library Construction for Human γδ TCRs

Written by: Megan Maerz, Dec. 6, 2021

Note: Unless otherwise indicated, all steps are identical to those described in the protocol here: https://support.10xgenomics.com/single-cell-vdj/library-prep/doc/user-guide-chromium-single-cell-vdj-reagent-kits-v11-chemistry

  1. Prepare Custom Primer Mixes
10x_hTCR-D/G_Outer Mix (4 uM Working Solution)
PrimerSequenceVolume (100 uM stock)
10X-Outer-FAATGATACGGCGACCACCGAGATCTACACTCTTTCCCTACACGACGCTC2 uL
R1_hTRDCAGCTTGACAGCATTGTACTTCC1 uL
R1_hTRGCTGTGTCGTTAGTCTTCATGGTGTTCC1 uL
Molecular-grade H2O96 uL
10x_hTCR-D/G_Inner Mix (4 uM Working Solution)
PrimerSequenceVolume (100 uM stock)
Illumina-P5AATGATACGGCGACCACCGAGATCTACAC2 uL
R2_hTRDCTCCTTCACCAGACAAGCGAC1 uL
R2_hTRGCGATCCCAGAATCGTGTTGCTC1 uL
Molecular-grade H2O96 uL
  1. cDNA Synthesis and Amplification
    1. Complete steps 1-3 of the Chromium Next GEM Single Cell V(D)J Reagent Kit v1.1 protocol.
  2. Step 4.1: Target Enrichment 1
    1. Thaw, equilibrate, and prepare 10x reagents as described on pg. 47 of the protocol.
      1. Deviation: Substitute the 10x_hTCR-D/G primer mixes for Human T Cell Mix 1 and Human T Cell Mix 2.
    2. Add 33 µl nuclease-free water into a tube strip on ice and then transfer 2 µl sample (post cDNA Amplification & QC, step 3.3) to the same tube for a total of 35 µl.
    3. Prepare Target Enrichment 1 Reaction Mix on ice. Vortex and centrifuge briefly.
      1. Deviation: Substitute the 10x_hTCR-D/G_Outer primer mix for Human T Cell Mix 1, as indicated below.
Target Enrichment 1 Reaction MixPN1X (uL)
Nuclease-free Water-5
Amplification Master Mix22012550
cDNA Additive2200675
10x_hTCR-D/G_Outer Mix-5
Total-65
  1. Add 65 µl Target Enrichment 1 Reaction Mix to each tube containing 35 µl sample.
  2. Pipette mix 5x (pipette set to 90 µl). Centrifuge briefly.
  3. Incubate in a thermal cycler with the following protocol.
    1. Deviation: Annealing temperature is optimized for the custom primer mix.
Lid TemperatureReaction VolumeRun Time
105°C100 uL~20-30 min
StepTemperatureTime
198°C00:00:45
298°C00:00:20
362°C00:00:30
472°C00:01:00
5Go to Step 2, 9x (10 total cycles)
672°C00:01:00
74°CHold
  1. Store at 4°C for up to 72 h or proceed to the next step.
  1. Step 4.2: Post Target Enrichment 1 Cleanup – SPRIselect
    1. Follow the protocol on pg. 49
  2. Step 4.3: Target Enrichment 2
    1. Prepare Target Enrichment 2 Reaction Mix on ice. Vortex and centrifuge briefly.
      1. Deviation: Substitute the 10x_hTCR-D/G_Inner Mix primer mix for Human T Cell Mix 2, as indicated below.
Target Enrichment 1 Reaction MixPN1X (uL)
Nuclease-free Water-5
Amplification Master Mix22012550

  1. Add 65 µl Target Enrichment 1 Reaction Mix to each tube containing 35 µl sample.
  2. Pipette mix 5x (pipette set to 90 µl). Centrifuge briefly.
  3. Incubate in a thermal cycler with the following protocol.
    1. Deviation: Annealing temperature is optimized for the custom primer mix.

  1. Store at 4°C for up to 72 h or proceed to the next step.
  1. Step 4.4: Post Target Enrichment 2 Double Sides Size Selection – SPRIselect
    1. Follow the protocol on pg. 51
  2. Step 4.5: Post Target Enrichment QC & Quantification
    1. Follow the protocol on pg. 52
    2. See the reference image below for an example of cDNA enriched for γδ TCR.
      1. 1 uL of Step 4.4 product was run on an Agilent TapeStation D1000 Chip.
  3. Step 5: Library Construction
    1. Continue with library construction and QC as described in the Chromium Next GEM Single Cell V(D)J Reagent Kit v1.1 protocol.
    2. See the reference image below for an example of a γδ TCR V(D)J Library.
      1. 1 uL of Step 5.5 product was run on an Agilent TapeStation D1000 Chip.

γδ Library Construction (slide deck)
PowerPoint3.2 MB· Yeh MD, Albert C· edited 2023-05-10
▼

Slide deck on γδ TCR library construction.

⬇ Download PowerPoint · 3.2 MB

Source & citation

Chetan (Cheta) Seshadri lab, University of Washington.

Document record

Document author: mdmaerz
Last edited by: Yeh MD, Albert C
Created: 2019-11-12
Last modified: 2023-05-10
Revisions: 529

Slide by slide

Slide 1 · Preparing custom TCR libraries using the10x Genomics platform
  • Megan Maerz
Slide 2 · TCR Targeted Enrichment
  • 2
  • Primers that are complementary to the TCR constant regions and to Read 1 are used to amplify TCR-encoding transcripts in nested PCRs.
  • 10x only sells a kit for human or mouse αβ TCRs → Custom primers required for other library preps (γδ, NHP).
  • Custom primers for amplifying human γδ TCRs have been published (PMID #31011186 and #33674358).
Slide 3 · Primer Design
  • 3
  • P5
  • Adaptor
  • Six unique primers are needed:
  • 10x_outer_F (Forward)
  • Constant_A/D_Outer_R1
  • Constant_B/G_Outer_R2
  • Illumina_P5 (Forward)
  • Constant_A/D_Inner_R1
  • Constant_B/G_Inner_R2
  • Original transcript
  • Added during cDNA Amp.
  • Gel bead primers
  • TCR transcripts are amplified in two nested PCRs.
  • First PCR: Forward primer adds P5 adaptor (binds to Illumina flow cell), reverse primers bind to outer TCR constant regions.
  • Second PCR: Forward primer binds to P5 adaptor, reverse primers bind to inner TCR constant regions.
Slide 4 · PCR Optimization
  • 4
  • Custom primers may not amplify well without additional optimization.
  • We’ve found success in focusing on the annealing temperatures.
  • TapeStation Trace
  • PCR not optimized
  • TapeStation Trace
  • Optimized
  • Final TCR library
  • TapeStation Trace
  • Optimized
Slide 5 · TCR Analysis
  • 5
  • With some adjustments to the cellranger VDJ pipeline, the resulting data can be analyzed like any other TCR dataset (if the tool is compatible with your TCR/species of interest!).
  • Gene usage
  • CDR3 matches
  • Clonality
  • TCR clustering

RNeasy Tips (bench notes)
Word15.7 KB· Dawson, Alyssa D· edited 2014-07-25
▼

Hard-won practical notes: use kits under 9 months old; RLT and RW1 contain guanidine salt that precipitates below 20–25°C — warm to 50°C to redissolve; keep the centrifuge at RT; add an extra RPE wash and incubate 3–5 min at RT.

⬇ Download Word · 15.7 KB

Source & citation

Document author metadata: Alyssa D. Dawson, 2014-07-25.

Document record

Document author: Dawson, Alyssa D
Last edited by: Dawson, Alyssa D
Created: 2014-07-25
Last modified: 2014-07-25
Revisions: 2
Company: FHCRC

The protocol, step by step

RNeasy Tips:

  • Make sure kit is not over 9 months olds
  • RLT buffer and RW1 both contain guanidine salt which will precipitate out below RT (20-25°C) make sure they don’t have precipitates by warming to 50°C for a few minutes then cooling to RT. Guanidine is an RNase inhibitor so using it slightly warm should not be a degradation problem.
  • Make sure centrifuge is at RT (not just previously used at 4°C)
  • Add an extra RPE wash (does not contain guanidine salt) and incubate RPE washes for 3-5 minutes at RT before centrifugation
  • Wash columns completely and take care not to carry over any previous reagent to the next step

________________________________________________________________________

Clean-up to improve the 260/230 ratio after extraction:

Add 500 ul of RPE buffer to the samples

Transfer it to the column

Incubate at RT for 5 min

Centrifuge

Repeat wash/incubation with RPE

Continue with kit instructions.

Notes:

-We recommend skipping RLT and RW1, it contains guanidine salt and can decrease 260/230, just use buffer RPE.

-If you have less than 1 ug you can follow the cleanup protocol in the RNeasy Micro kit or RNeasy Minelute Cleanup kit.

-If you have more than 1 ug you can use the cleanup protocol in the RNeasy Mini kit.

-You can increase the number of washes of buffer RPE, just make sure it has the right vol of ethanol added.

-Do not to use smaller volumes of elution buffer than what is recommended in each kit.

BaseScope / multiplex IHC Methods
Word33.0 KB
▼

Combined BaseScope in situ hybridisation and multiplex IHC methods write-up.

⬇ Download Word · 33.0 KB

Source & citation

Initials TG, dated 2023-05-03 in the filename.

Document record

No embedded metadata (legacy binary format).

The protocol, step by step

In Situ Hybridization (ISH), Immunofluorescence (IF) and Imaging was performed by

FHCC Experimental Histopathology Shared Resource

Basescope + mIHC staining:

Formalin-fixed paraffin-embedded tissues were sectioned at 4 microns onto positive-charged slides and baked for 1 hour at 60°C. The slides were loaded to the Leica Bond Rx Autostainer platform (Leica, Buffalo Grove, IL) to start the run. The slides were baked and dewaxed using Leica Bond reagents for dewaxing (Dewax Solution). Antigen retrieval was performed at 95°C for 15 minutes using Leica Epitope Retrieval Solution 2 followed with 0.5% Triton-X/PBS solution pretreatment at 40°C for 60 minutes. After the pretreatment steps, the slides were exposed to hydrogen peroxide for 10 minutes and then incubated with ACD Target probe, BA-Mm-Tra-V4J33-CDR3-C1 (ACD #1210058-C1), Positive control probe BA-Mm-Ppib-1zz (ACD #712358) or Negative control probe BA-DapB-zz (ACD #701028) at 42°C for 120 minutes. After the probe incubation, the staining continues with the Basescope LS Reagent Kit (ACD #323600) for the amplification and detection steps, followed by use of the Bond Polymer Refine Red detection kit (Leica #DS9390). To perform the CD3 IF staining, protein blocking with TCT buffer (0.05M Tris, 0.15M NaCl, 0.25% Casein, 0.1% Tween 20, pH 7.6 +/- 0.1) for 10 minutes was used. The CD3 (Abcam #ab215212) primary antibody at 1:200 was applied for 60 minutes followed by the OPAL Polymer Anti-Rabbit HRP (Akoya Biosciences #ARR1001KT) for 20 minutes. The tertiary TSA-amplification reagent Opal fluor 690 (Akoya Biosciences #OP-001006) was added at 1:100 in Akoya 1x Plus Automation Amplification Diluent (Akoya #FP1609) for 20 minutes. Slides were removed from the Bond Autostainer and stained with DAPI (10ug/ml distilled water) for 5 minutes, rinsed in water, and coverslipped using Prolong Gold Antifade reagent (Invitrogen #P36930). The slides were cured at room temperature and the images of the slides were acquired on the Vectra Polaris Imaging System.

Mine:

RNA ISH staining was performed on fixed and paraffin-embedded tissue using custom probe design by Advanced Cell Diagnostics, Inc (ACD). These include probe V4J33 targeting a 49 basepair sequence centered on the CBir1 Tg CDR3 region: GCTGTGTACTACTGTGCTGTCACAGGAAACTACAAATACGTCTTTGGAG and probe V15J8 targeting a 47 basepair sequence centered on the SFB Tg CDR3 region: CCATCTACTTCTGTGCAGAAGGCAACATGGGCTACAAACTTACCTTC.

Isopentane Snap-Freezing (slide deck)
PowerPoint19.1 MB· Benitez-Martinez, Elizabeth· edited 2018-08-02
▼

Illustrated walkthrough of isopentane snap-freezing for tissue histology.

⬇ Download PowerPoint · 19.1 MB

Source & citation

Fred Hutch-branded internal slide deck.

Document record

Document author: "Chanthaphavong, Savanh"
Last edited by: Benitez-Martinez, Elizabeth
Created: 2016-11-04
Last modified: 2018-08-02
Revisions: 317
Company: Fred Hutch

Slide by slide

Slide 1 · Experimental Histopathology Shared Resources
  • © Fred Hutchinson Cancer Research Center
  • 1
  • Basic Tissue Freezing Workshop
  • Presenter Elizabeth Benitez, M.Ed., Research Histotechnician III
Slide 2 · Why Isopentane Freezing?
  • The quicker you freeze samples the better the results.
  • Less ice crystals = better morphology
  • Samples freeze in about 10-12
  • seconds.
  • Isopentane freezing produces better morphology for muscle and tissues with a lot of fat.
Slide 3 · Materials
  • Gloves
  • Cryo Gloves
  • Safety gear for handling Liquid nitrogen
  • Intermediate Cryomold Mold (15mm x 15 mm x 5 mm)
  • Thermo flasks
  • Forceps
  • Styrofoam container
  • Aluminum foil
  • Box for -70 freezer storage
  • Tissue Tek OCT
  • Isopentane/2-methylbutane (1liter)
  • Liquid Nitrogen (900ml)
  • Dry Ice or easy access to -70 freezer
  • Safety Gear
  • Materials
  • Solutions & misc.
Slide 4 · Setting up for freezing
  • Flask method: Open a hole on either side of the plastic 500 ml beaker and run a metal wire through on either side. Make sure that the beaker fits inside and can hang off of the top of the Thermo Flask.
  • Styrofoam box method: Just use a regular 500 ml polypropylene container and Styrofoam box with a lid.
Slide 5 · Setting up for freezing
  • Bend edge of cryomolds upward for better handling of mold while freezing.
  • Label Cryomolds with a sharpie pen
  • Styrofoam box with dry ice and lid (for when you are done embedding the tissue).
  • Liquid nitrogen (500 ml – 1 liter)
  • Aluminum foil (to wrap up embedded tissue)
  • Have all materials ready for embedding
  • Use proper safety materials and precautions while freezing.
  • © Fred Hutchinson Cancer Research Center
  • 5
Slide 6 · Setting up solution for Thermo flask
  • Fill 500 ml container/beaker with approximately 300 ml of isoptentane/2-Methlybutane
  • Lower 500 ml container/beaker with isopentane/2-methylbutane into a thermoflask with 500 ml of Liquid nitrogen
  • Allow beaker to sit in liquid nitrogen until you see edges of the beaker turning white (cold) approximately 10 minutes
  • isopentane should look like a slurry before use (approximately 5-10 minutes depending on amount of isopentane)
Slide 7 · Setting up solution for dry ice method
  • Fill bottom of Styrofoam box with about 1 inch of liquid nitrogen
  • Fill a 500 ml container/beaker with approximately 300 ml of Isoptentane/2-Methlybutane.
  • Slowly lower container into liquid nitrogen. Ensure that the container does not spill into the liquid nitrogen.
  • Allow beaker to sit in liquid nitrogen until you see edges of the beaker turning white (cold) approximately 10 minutes
  • Isopentane should look like a slurry before use (approximately 5-10 minutes depending on amount of isopentane)
  • © Fred Hutchinson Cancer Research Center
  • 7
Slide 8 · Prepping the tissue
  • Cut down tissue samples:
  • <3 mm in thickness
  • <10 mm in length
  • Keep fresh tissue on a moist gauze with saline to avoid drying artifact.
  • Right before embedding, dry the tissue on a paper towel or dry cloth to avoid ice crystals before freezing.
Slide 9 · Embedding
  • Put a small amount of OCT in the labeled cryomold before placing the tissue inside. Avoid or pop any air bubbles that emerge.
  • Place the tissue in the center of the mold and carefully surround the tissue with OCT. Use a circular motion.
  • Fill the mold entirely making sure that the tissue is submerged in OCT.
  • Use forceps to keep tissue flat or area of interest downward.
Slide 10 · Isopentane Freezing
  • Slowly lower the mold into the isopentane with forceps using the bent edge of the cryomold. *keeping the mold as flat as possible
  • Use a circular or back and forth motion to ensure complete freezing of the sample/OCT
  • Typical freezing will take 10-12 seconds however, samples can stay longer in the isopentane.
Slide 11 · After freezing
  • After the samples have been in the isopentane a minimum of 10-12 seconds, remove samples using forceps and shake off excess liquid isopentane.
  • Use gloves and wrap samples in aluminum foil for storing. If necessary, keep samples on dry ice before relocating samples into a -70 freezer.
  • For long term frozen sample storage use a cardboard box
  • Tissue can be submitted to Experimental Histopathology for frozen cutting
Slide 12 · Ordering information
  • Cryomold Mold (4566 Cryomold® Intermediate 15 x 15 x 5 mm, 100/pcs)
  • 4 Fl oz.Tissue-Plus OCT (Sciegen, 4583)
  • Thermo Scientific Thermo Flask 2L (Thermo Fisher Scientific 2123)
  • Tempshield Cryo Gloves (Fisher Scientific S98416B or S98416C)
  • Isopentane/2-Methylbutane (Fisher Scientific AC126470010, 1 liter)
Slide 13 · © Fred Hutchinson Cancer Research Center
  • 13
  • THANK YOU!
  • Questions?
  • Savanh Chanthaphavong, Director of Experimental Histopathology rchantha@fredhutch.org
  • Elizabeth Benitez, Research Histotechnician III
  • ebenitez@fredhutch.org

xCELLigence Real-Time Cell Analyzers (slide deck)
PowerPointEsight/3.3 MB· Cole, Gabby· edited 2020-03-31
▼

Overview deck on the RTCA platform.

⬇ Download PowerPoint · 3.3 MB

Source & citation

Vendor slide deck (Agilent / ACEA).

Document record

Document author: Cole, Gabby
Last edited by: Cole, Gabby
Created: 2020-03-25
Last modified: 2020-03-31
Revisions: 20

Slide by slide

Slide 1 · Tech Journal ClubxCELLigence® Real-Time Cell Analyzers
  • 04/01/20
  • Gabby Cole
Slide 2 · Issues with Golden standard: Chromium 51(Cr) release assay
  • Cytotoxicity of effectors against tumor cells
  • Suffers from problems with sensitivity
  • Unable to run flow on the cells or use the supernatant for cytokine analysis
Slide 3 · Monitored with the real-time cell analysis (RTCA; xCELLigence ACEA Biosciences
  • Simple Workflow and sensitivity of evaluating low E:T
  • Real-Time Quantitative monitoring of both minutes and days
  • Automatic Data Plotting by RTCA software enables facile data display and objective analysis
Slide 4 · Unit of a cellular impedance assay is a set of gold microelectrodes
  • Presence of cells at the electrode-solution interface impedes electron flow
  • Extremely sensitive readout of cell number, cell size/morphology, and cell-substrate attachment quality
Slide 5 · Suspension Tumor Line Tethering Approaches
  • Cancer Type
  • Validated Cell Line
  • Selective Tethering Mechanism
  • Verified Target Expression by Flow Cytometry
  • Acute Lymphoblastic Leukemia (ALL)
  • NALM6
  • CD9
  • CD3-, CD9+, CD19+, CD20-, CD22+, CD38+, CD138+, CD269+, HLA-DR+
  • RS4;11
  • CD9
  • CD3- , CD9+, CD14+, CD19+, CD20-, CD22+, CD38+, CD138+, CD269+, CD123+(low)
  • Chronic Myelogenous Leukemia (CML)
  • K562
  • CD29 or CD71
  • CD3-, CD14-, CD15+, CD19-, CD29+, CD33+, CD71+, CD235a+
  • Non-Hodgkin Lymphoma (NHL)
  • Daudi
  • CD40 or CD19
  • CD3-, CD19+, CD20+, CD40+
  • Raji
  • CD40 or CD19
  • CD3-, CD19+, CD20+, CD40+
  • Ramos
  • CD40 or CD19
  • CD3-, CD19+, CD20+, CD40+
  • Multiple Myeloma (MM)
  • RPMI 8226
  • CD9
  • CD3-, CD9+, CD19-, CD38+, CD138+, CD269+, HLA-DR+
  • MM1R
  • CD9 and CD71
  • CD3-, CD9+, CD19-, CD38+, CD71+, CD138+, CD269+, HLA-DR+
  • Acute Myeloid Leukemia (AML)
  • HEL 92.1.7
  • CD29
  • CD13+, CD29+, CD33+, CD15+, CD123+
  • Chronic Lymphocytic Leukemia (CLL)
  • MEC2
  • CD40
Slide 6 · Non-adherent Cancer Cell Killing
  • Day 1:
  • Coat with diluted Tethering Reagent
  • Take background measurement with culture medium
  • Add tumor cells and let settle (30 minutes)
  • Load and start data acquisition to monitor target cell attachment and proliferation.
  • Day 2:
  • Pause data acquisition and add effector cells
  • Start data acquisition to monitor effector cell-mediated killing of immobilized target cells.
  • Cell Index (CI)
  • CI = (impedance at time point n – impedance in the absence of cells)
  • /nominal impedance value
Slide 7 · Tendency to foam: Use the reverse pipetting technique
  • Do not go all the way down to the second stop in Step 1
  • Press the operating button slightly past the first stop then take up the volume and release to the first stop
Slide 8 · Application Highlight: Detecting Cytolytic Activity of
  • CD8+ T Cells
  • Figure adapted from J Vis Exp. 2012 Aug 8;(66):e3683.
  • Side by side comparison with the 51Cr release assay vs xCELLigence RTCA assay
  • Shows sensitivity and dynamic range
Slide 9 · Live Cell Imaging with xCELLigence Biosensor
  • Simultaneously tracking five different perspectives
  • Impedance
  • Blue nuclei labeled tumor cell counts
  • Induction of apoptosis tracked by percent cellular confluence
  • Caspase 3 activation
  • Phosphatidylserine translocation
  • Red fluorescence: Annexin V
  • Green fluorescence: Activated Caspase 3
  • Blue fluorescence: Nuclear-localized BFB
Slide 10 · Questions?

Presentation Template
PowerPointPowerpoint Templates/4.1 MB· Yeh, Albert C· edited 2019-09-24

General-purpose presentation template.

Fred Hutchinson Cancer Center brand template.

Slide by slide

Slide 2 · Study Concept
  • Pegylated Urate Oxidase to Reduce Graft vs. Host Disease (GVHD) Following Myeloablative HLA-matched Allogeneic Stem Cell Transplantation (ASCT)
Slide 3 · Outline
  • Brief primer on transplant and GVHD
  • Phase I Clinical Data
  • Animal / Mechanistic Data
  • Study Concept
Slide 4 · Outline
  • Brief primer on transplant and GVHD
  • Phase I Clinical Data
  • Animal / Mechanistic Data
  • Study Concept
Slide 5 · Annual Number of HCT Recipients in the US by Transplant Type
Slide 6 · Indications for Hematopoietic Cell Transplant in the US, 2017
Slide 7 · ASCT and Graft vs. Host Disease
  • Recipient
  • Donor
  • SCT
  • +/- acute GVHD
  • *ASCT used to treat a variety of malignant and benign hematologic conditions
  • *GVHD: donor immune system attacks genetically disparate recipient
  • *Acute GVHD vs. Chronic GVHD
  • *20-60%: risk of acute GVHD grade 2+ (depending on type of transplant)
  • *40-50%: response rate to first-line treatment with high-dose steroids
  • Conditioning
  • Engraftment
Slide 8 · Pathogenesis of Acute GVHD
Slide 9 · Outline
  • Brief primer on transplant and GVHD
  • Phase I Clinical Data
  • Animal / Mechanistic Data
  • Study Concept
Slide 10 · Identification of Uric Acid as Endogenous Adjuvant
  • [1] Shi Y, et al. Nature. 2003;425:516-521.
  • Uric Acid
  • CTL priming in mice reduced by 90% in those treated with allopurinol and uricase [1].
  • Addition of uric acid in vitro stimulated bone-marrow derived DCs to express CD86 (B7-2) and CD80 (B7-1), the effect of which was stronger than LPS [1].
  • Tumor implants in mice undergoing immune rejection harbored increase uric acid levels, and suppression of uric acid by allopurinol or uricase inhibited tumor immune rejection [2].
  • [2] Hu DE, et al. Cancer Res. 2004;64:5059-5062.
  • Depletion of uric acid reduces the proliferation of autoreactive T-cells in a transgenic diabetes model [3].
  • [3] Shi Y, et al. J Immunol. 2006;176:3905-3908.
Slide 11 · Phase I Study Design
  • Recipient
  • Donor
  • SCT
  • +/- acute GVHD
  • Conditioning
  • Engraftment
  • Uricase given d-7 to d-2
  • Hypothesis:
  • Suppression of uric acid at time of transplantation may modulate the immune activation of host APCs and alter the incidence of acute GVHD.
Slide 12 · Phase I Study Design
  • 21 patients
  • Rasburicase Group
  • IV Rasburicase 0.20 mg/kg (d-7 to -2)
  • Standard GVHD prophylaxis
  • MRD: Cyclosporine or tacrolimus + MTX
  • MUD: Tacrolimus and MTX + Thymoglobulin
  • Inclusion Criteria:
  • Indication for conventional myeloablative allogeneic SCT
  • 18< Age <65
  • GCSF-mobilized PSCs
  • HLA 6 of 6 antigen matched (A, B, DR) related or unrelated donors
  • Exclusion Criteria:
  • Active HIV infection
  • Uncontrolled DM2
  • Active CHF
  • Active angina pectoris
  • O2 dependent COPD
  • Failure to give full informed consent
  • 20 patients
  • Primary outcome: incidence of acute aGVHD grade 2+
  • Secondary outcome: PFS, OS, MRD/MUD comparison
  • 1 case of hemolytic anemia (G6PD)
  • 21 patients
  • Matched Controls
  • Allopurinol per protocol 300mg QD (d-7 to d-1)
Slide 13 · Phase I Study Results
  • Transplant Day
  • Control
  • Rasburicase
  • Serum Uric Acid (mg/dL)
  • p<0.0001
  • Transplant Day
  • Rasburicase Group

  • We would not require repeated administration

Slide 24 · Study Concept
  • Recipient
  • Donor
  • SCT
  • Myeloablative Conditioning
  • Engraftment
  • Pegylated uricase (d-7)
  • 1) Phenotypic analysis of host APC and various T-cell subsets from PBMCs.
  • 2) Serum cytokine analysis
  • 3) Serum uric acid levels
  • 4) Pre-transplant and at intervals up to day 100
  • Primary and secondary outcome measures (e.g. incidence, timing, severity of acute GVHD)

The protocol, step by step

IRRADIATION DOSES
MOUSE STRAINIRRADIATION DOSE (cGy) (Caesium)COMMENTS
B6D2F11100Standard transplant using spleen as the innoculum
B6D2F11300Transplant using bone marrow (BM) as the innoculum. BM contains less T cells so induces less GVHD. Increasing the radiation dose increases the gut damage and hence the GVHD – makes it easier to see a treatment effect following a BM transplant.
C57Bl/6j KO on C57 background1000Take care if giving these mice a higher radiation dose – need to ensure that the donor innoculum is sufficient to rescue them from GVHD as they are more sensitive to irradiation than B6D2F1
PTPRCA1000As for C57Bl/6
Balb/B900Minor model
Balb/C900These mice are very sensitive to irradiation
Chimerics950Chimeric mice made using C57Bl/6 or PTPRCA as the recipient
C57xC3H F11100
C3H/Hej950
LP/J900
CELLS2000Macrophages and whole spleen used as stimulators in an MLC
CELL YIELDS
CELL SOURCENUMBER
Naïve whole spleenC57Bl/6 - 100 million Balb/c - 120 million B6D2F1 - 70-80 million
G-CSF pretreated whole spleen 6 days @ 10 µg/day 4 days @ 10 µg/day (assume around 50-100 million less)C57Bl/6 – 200-250 million Balb/c – 300-400 million NB Balb/c IL-17R ko - 120 million
ProGP-1 pretreated whole spleen 10 days @ 20 µg/dayC57Bl/6 - 500 million plus C57xC3H F1 – 350 million
Peg-G-CSF pretreated whole spleen 12 µg on day -612 µg on day -6 – 160 million
FLT-3 pretreated whole spleen10 days @ 10 µg/day - 150 million
Biomag T prep from one spleen (80-90% purity)C57Bl/6 - 12-18 million Balb/c – 18-25 million
MACS CD4 from one spleen (using LS column)C57Bl/6 - 8-12 million
Bone marrow – 2 back legsC57Bl/6 - 80 million Balb/c – 60 million B6D2F1 – 50 million C3H.SW – 40 million C57xC3H F1– 40-50 million
DC gradientRecover 10-15% of cells that go over the gradient
Macrophage washVaries from 2-8 million with highly variable macrophage purity. Probably only 1-2 million F4/80 +ve cells so usually the higher the cell recovery the lower the purity
Lymph nodesHighly variable depending on whether mouse is naïve or has GVHD and what stage the GVHD is at so not going to mention numbers here – usually in the very low millions
Liver following perfusion and percoll gradientDepending on treatment and GVHD state – 1-8 million leukocytes

Reference — Transplant Systems
Word38.0 KB
▼

Summary of the transplant models in use: minor histocompatibility antigen mismatch pairs (B10.D2 → Balb/c, both CD45.2), T-cell depletion to prevent rejection, ProGP-treated grafts and tolerogenic cell expansion.

⬇ Download Word · 38.0 KB

Source & citation

Yeh Lab reference sheet.

Document record

No embedded metadata (legacy binary format).

The protocol, step by step

Major Histocompatibility Antigen Mismatch

Irradiation

  1. C57 (CD45.2) -------→ B6D2F1 (CD45.2)

PTP (CD45.1) cell transfer

H-2b H-2b/d

  • donor cells see H-2d as foreign and initiate “rejection” response against host/recipient cells expressing H-2d
  • this means host tissue cells of all organs, but certain organs are more severely affected – skin, liver, gut, and lung.
  • Host doesn’t see donor as foreign (both have H-2b)
  • Donor cells include mobilised stem cells from the spleen or stem cells from the bone marrow to allow engraftment
  • Spleen graft usually give 10x106 mobilised spleen (includes GCSF, pegGCSF, SD-03, ProGP-1 and ProGP-4 – these all increase stem cells) OR control group of 7-10x106 TCD mobilised spleen.
    • Recipients of unmobilised spleens will die as there are insufficient stem cells to allow engraftment
    • Irradiation dose of 1100cGy to recipient
    • Spleen contains both T cells, stem cells and other cell types
  • Bone Marrow graft usually give 5x106 bone marrow (can go as low as 3 for chimeras but more is better) – some systems give 10x106 bone marrow
    • Needs to supplemented with purified naïve splenic T cells – dose from 0.5 to 5 x 106 T cells depending on system as bone marrow alone won’t induce GVHD effectively
    • Irradiation dose of 1100cGy or 1300cGy depending on the system
    • This system allows various knockout animals to be used as donors – all our knockouts are on a C57 background.
Major Histocompatibility Antigen Mismatch

irradiation

  1. Balb/c (CD45.2) ------- > C57 (CD45.2)

Balb/c (CD45.1) Cell transfer PTP (CD45.1)

H-2d H-2b

  • donor cells see host cells as foreign and initiate GVHD
  • however, host cells (what immune cells are remaining post-irradiation) see donor cells as foreign also and may initiate graft rejection – extent is limited by the irradiation.
  • This means that the number of cells going in may need to be greater that 10 x 106 to counteract the graft rejection effect – tend to use 25x106 mobilised spleen cells
  • This system allows various knockout animals to be used as recipients
Major Histocompatibility Antigen Mismatch

irradiation

  1. PTP x C3H F1 ------- > B6D2F1

(CD45.1 + CD45.2) cell transfer (CD45.2)

H-2b/k H-2 b/d

  • system used for Angela’s mixed chimerism for solid organ transfer system
  • donor cells recognise host as foreign (see H-2d) -> GVHD
  • host cells recognise donor as foreign (see H-2k) -> graft rejection
  • CD45.1 (PTP) allows us to track donor cells Vs host cells
  • Mixed chimerism aims to get donor and host immune systems coexisting – tolerant of each other – without making host ill.
    • Idea is to put immune cells from a solid organ donor (eg kidney) into organ recipient prior to organ transplant
    • Get two immune systems coexisting then you can transplant the organ and the recipient won’t see it as foreign as it has already been “tolerised” to donor immune cells
  • irradiation dose needs to be really low (optimised to 200cGy) to allow maintenance of host cells – they just need to be suppressed sufficiently to prevent graft rejection
  • recipients pretreated with anti–CD3 (αCD3) to deplete T cells prior to graft -> prevent rejection
  • grafts have been ProGP treated – expand tolerogenic cells
Minor Histocompatibility Antigen Mismatch

irradiation

  1. B10.D2 (CD45.2) ------- > Balb/c (CD45.2)

Balb/c (CD45.1)

H-2d cell transfer H-2d

  • degree of GVHD is much less than major mismatch as minor antigens are less immunogenic
  • result is that more T cells need to be transferred to induce sufficient GVHD to be able to compare treatment effects
  • system has mainly used ProGP treatment of donor cells but can use unmobilised cells but need to give larger numbers to ensure sufficient stem cells for engraftment
  • irradiation is 900cGy – Balb/c mice are quite sensitive to irradiation – higher doses can be lethal even with graft transfer.
Minor Histocompatibility Antigen Mismatch

irradiation

  1. C57 (CD45.2) ------- > Balb/B (CD45.2)

PTP (CD45.1) cell transfer

H-2b H-2b

  • system requirements similar to other minor system
  • irradiation is 900cGy
  • has potential for knockouts to be used as donors
Chimeric Mice
  • all chimeras are B6 -> B6 (donor and /or recipient can be various knockout animals)
  • chimeras can then be used as donors in B6 -> B6D2F1 system, or recipients in the Balb/c -> B6 system
  • these allow us to look at the effect of the haematopoietic system (immune cells) versus their non-haematopoietic system (stroma etc)
  • also allows knockout animals with reduced viability (eg RelB) to be used as recipients
  • allows us to look at knockout in certain cell types by mixing bone marrow from different strains.
Tumour Systems
  • GVHD is bad side effect of bone marrow transplant/stem cell transplant -> GVL is good effect
  • To measure GVL effectively you need a tumour model
  • Recipient is the person with the tumour hence the tumour needs to have the same MHC as the recipient
  • B6 -> B6D2F1 – tumour is P815
    • P815 is H-2d and was raised in DBA/2 mice
    • We have luciferase transfected P815
  • Balb/c -> B6 – tumour is EL4
    • EL4 is H-2b
    • We have luciferase transfected EL 4
  • B10.D2 -> Balb/c – tumour is A20
    • A20 is H-2d and was raised in Balb/c mice
    • We have A20 luciferase transfected
    • A20 we have is immunogenic to some extent so we are having some problems with this tumour at the moment
  • B6 -> Balb/B – we don’t have a tumour available for this system

Reference — What's in a Mouse
Word34.0 KB
▼

Strain-by-strain reference of what each mouse line carries.

⬇ Download Word · 34.0 KB

Source & citation

Yeh Lab reference sheet.

Document record

No embedded metadata (legacy binary format).

The protocol, step by step

MOUSE STRAINMHC CLASS I & IICD45 (LY-5)COMMENTS
C57Bl/6jb2Nearly all knock-out mice are on a C57Bl/6 background. Used as the donor for a standard allogeneic transplant into a B6D2F1, Balb/c or LP/J mouse to induce GVHD. Also used as a recipient with Balb/c or C3H.SW mice as the donor
PTPRCAb1Identical to a C57Bl/6 except for CD45. Often used as a donor or recipient in certain models when wanting to track either the recipient or donor cells. Can be mixed with a donor C57Bl/6 inoculum to track individual cell types.
B6D2F1 (C57Bl/6 x DBA/2 F1)b & d2A first generation cross (F1) between a C57Bl/6 (H2b) and a DBA/2 (H2d). All cells will express both H2b and H2d. Used as the standard recipient for most transplants with the C57Bl donor T cells (H2b) recognising the H2d component of the F1 as foreign and so causing GVHD.
PTPRCA x DBA/2 F1b & d1 & 2Identical to a B6D2F1 except that all cells will express 45.1 and 45.2 which will allow tracking of cells originating from this mouse.
PTPRCA x C3H/Hej F1b & k1 & 2A first generation cross between a PTPRCA (H2b & 45.1) and a C3H (H2k & 45.2). All cells will express both H2b and H2k, and also 45.1 and 45.2 which allows tracking of cells originating from these mice. These mice will be used as donors for transplants using the B6D2F1 mouse as a recipient. The H2k component of the donor will recognise the H2d component of the recipient as foreign thus causing GVHD in the recipient. Also, the H2d component of the recipient will recognise the H2k component of the donor as foreign and so initiate graft rejection. These mice have also been used as recipients with C57Bl/6 mice as the donors. The C57Bl/6 donor cells will recognise the H2k component of the recipient as foreign and initiate GVHD.
Balb/Cd2Used as a donor for transplants when the recipient is a PTPRCA or a C57Bl/6. The H2d of the donor recognises the H2b of the recipient as foreign, causing GVHD. The host H2b will also recognise the donor H2d as foreign and residual irradiation resistant NK cells can initiate some degree of graft rejection. Also used as the recipient for studying a minor model where the donor is a B10.D2 (H2d, CD45.2) mouse. Have been used as recipients with C57Bl/6 mice as the donor. When used as recipients they tend to get a very severe acute GVHD so need a very small donor T cell inoculums.
Balb/Bb2Used as the recipient for a minor system transplant where a PTPRCA or a C57Bl/6 is the donor. The MHC is identical but there are minor haplotype differences.
LP/Jb2Used as the recipient for a minor system where the donor is a C57Bl/6. Tend to develop scleroderma-like GVHD which is being used as a model for human scleroderma in chronic GVHD.
